# Phase 1 근거 목록 — 주장별 소스 위치와 교차 검증 판정

[SDK_ANALYSIS.md](SDK_ANALYSIS.md)의 근거 부록이다. 8개 분석 축의 각 주장에 대해 분석 에이전트가 인용한 소스 위치(site-packages 기준,
`reachy_mini 1.11.0`)와, **별도의 적대적 검증 에이전트**가 인용 줄을 다시 열고 수치를 독립 스크립트로 재계산해 내린 판정을 그대로 싣는다.
`probes/…`는 분석·검증에 쓴 오프라인 탐침 스크립트와 출력으로, `results/phase1/probes/`에 보관했다(개발 PC에만 있고 git 저장소에는 올리지 않았다)
(데몬·로봇 접속 없음). 주장 원문은 영어(에이전트 출력), 보고서 본문은 이 판정을 반영해 작성했다.

판정: ✅ 소스가 주장 전체를 뒷받침 · 🟡 일부 수정 필요(수정문 기재) · ❌ 틀림 · ⚪ 컴파일 코드/웹 등으로 검증 불가 · – 검증 대상 아님

| 분석 축 | 주장 수 | ✅ | 🟡 | ❌ | ⚪ | – |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| goto_target 호출 경로 | 30 | 22 | 7 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| 보간 4종의 수학 | 30 | 26 | 4 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| IK/FK | 30 | 20 | 10 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| 제어 루프·백엔드·텔레메트리 | 29 | 25 | 4 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| 관절 제한·안전 | 30 | 20 | 10 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| 커스텀 모션 재생 API | 30 | 25 | 5 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| 공식 문서·모터 사양 | 28 | 22 | 6 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| HW3 재사용 | 30 | 16 | 14 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

## 정오표 — 최종 교차 검토(2026-11-10) 후 정정

아래 표는 이 부록이 만들어진 뒤 최종 적대적 교차 검토(5개 관점 + 반박 검증)에서 확인된 정정이다. 본문 표의 원문(에이전트 출력)은 기록으로
그대로 두고, 해당 주장을 인용할 때는 이 정정을 따른다. 보고서 본문([SDK_ANALYSIS.md](SDK_ANALYSIS.md))에는 이미 반영되어 있다.

| 위치 | 원문 요지 | 정정 | 근거 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 관절 제한·안전 L12 검증 메모 | roll×pitch 상자의 최악 모서리가 같은 부호 (+,+)/(−,−) | 최악은 **pitch 음수(고개 든) 두 모서리** (+,−)·(−,−): s = 27.44°에서 stewart_1 = +70.0°, stewart_6 = −70.0°(여유 10.00°). (+,+)·(−,+)는 여유 17.34°. `v05_box_fine.txt`는 (roll, pitch) 순서 (−,−),(−,+),(+,−),(+,+)로 출력하는데 이를 잘못 읽었다 | 최종 검토 LS-02 (AnalyticalKinematics 재계산, 자동 body yaw 켬·끔 동일) |
| 제어 루프 '검증자가 추가한 발견'의 Windows sim 타이밍 항목, 해결 단계 `time.time()` 행 | Windows에서 daemon `play_move`는 ≈ 62 Hz | ≈ 62 Hz는 **유휴 이벤트 루프 스텁** 값이다. SDK `goto_target` 중의 daemon 루프는 연결된 /ws/sdk 클라이언트에 tick마다 브로드캐스트하느라 깨어나 ≈ 100–145 Hz로 불규칙(스텁, 해결 단계 'Predicted play_move rate on Windows' 참조). 실행 중 daemon의 평가 주기는 **미측정** | 최종 검토 RG-03 |
| 해결 단계 resolve_0 (`/api/state/full` 명령값) | `with_target_head_pose=true` → HTTP 500은 추론(미실행) | 2026-11-10 로컬 sim에서 **실행 확인**: `with_target_*` 키 없음(HTTP 200), `with_target_head_pose=true` → HTTP 500(daemon 로그에 AssertionError). 남은 불확실성 열의 'HTTP 500 추론' 항목은 해소 | 최종 검토 RG-10, `results/phase1/sim_smoke/api_checks.json`·`api_check_daemon.log` |
| 보간 4종의 수학 C21, 해결 단계의 'worst-case' 끝점 미달 | 최악 미달 = 1 − s(1 − Δt/D) | 이 식은 **마지막 간격 g = 공칭 Δt일 때의 값**이다. 로봇(Linux)에서 각 간격은 Δt + 깨어남 지연 이상이므로 실제 최악은 1 − s(1 − g_max/D) ≥ 이 값(g_max 미측정). CSV 열 이름도 `shortfall_if_last_gap_eq_assumed`로 바꿈 | 최종 검토 CT-03 |
| 명령 경로 지연 모델(본문 §5.2와 이 부록의 지연 관련 주장) | 0.5 ms 위상 격자 CSV 값 | 지연 = 30 ms(실기기) / 10 ms(MuJoCo 구조) + (위상 mod 10 ms). CSV는 30.0–39.5 / 10.0–19.5 ms(평균 34.75 / 14.75)이며 독립 정수 모델과 위상별로 일치(ZOH를 칸 중앙에서 표본화, tick 시각은 정수 µs) | 최종 검토 RG-01·CT-09, `tests/test_goto_replay.py` |

## goto_target 호출 경로 (`goto_pipeline`)

| ID | 판정 | 주장 (분석 원문) | 근거 (파일:줄) | 검증 메모 / 수정 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G01 | 🟡 부분 수정 | ReachyMini.goto_target(head=None, antennas=None, duration=0.5, method=MIN_JERK, body_yaw=0.0) raises ValueError only in two cases: head, antennas and body_yaw are all None, or duration <= 0. It does not check matrix shape, NaN, reachability or joint limits before sending. | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:672-703 | On the client, goto_target raises ValueError when head, antennas and body_yaw are all None, or when duration <= 0. Pydantic also raises ValidationError, a ValueError subclass, for an unknown method string or non-numeric values. The client never checks matrix shape: a 3x3 is sent as 9 floats and only fails at the daemon reshape, which comes back as Exception('Task failed...'). It also never checks antenna count, NaN, reachability or joint limits. A NaN duration passes `duration <= 0` and produces timeout=NaN. — v1: method='bogus' raised pydantic_core.ValidationError (isinstance ValueError True). duration=NaN, a 3x3 head, a NaN head and 3 antennas were all sent. |
| G02 | ✅ 확인 | body_yaw defaults to 0.0 (not None) in both ReachyMini.goto_target and Backend.goto_target. So any call that omits it drives the body yaw back to 0 rad: antenna-only gotos, look_at_world/look_at_image, and even goto_target() with no arguments, which passes validation. body_yaw=None keeps the present yaw. The REST GotoModelRequest and the typed GotoTargetCmd default body_yaw to None (keep). | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:680<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:689<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:695-698<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:824<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:866<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:951<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:39<br>reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:234-241<br>probes/goto_pipeline/check_protocol.out.txt | T7: an antenna-only Backend.goto_target starting from body yaw 0.5 rad ended with target_body_yaw 0.0106 rad (tail truncation). With body_yaw=None it stayed at 0.5. The physical body joint still comes from IK, and automatic body yaw (daemon default ON) keeps it within 65 deg of the head yaw, so it may not reach 0. |
| G03 | ✅ 확인 | goto_target serializes GotoTaskRequest(head = 16 floats or None, antennas, duration, method, body_yaw). All five fields are required, and method is validated client-side against linear/minjerk/ease_in_out/cartoon. The request is wrapped in TaskRequest{type:'task', uuid4, timestamp} and sent on the /ws/sdk WebSocket. | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:705-721<br>reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:1081-1110<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_client.py:268-280<br>probes/goto_pipeline/check_protocol.out.txt | '16 floats' is only the intended format. The length is not validated (a 3x3 is sent as 9 floats). |
| G04 | ✅ 확인 | wait_for_task_completion blocks the calling thread on a threading.Event for at most duration+1.0 s. On timeout it raises TimeoutError: no cancel is sent, the daemon move keeps running, and the task entry is never removed. If TaskProgress.error is set, it raises a bare Exception('Task failed with error: ...'). Otherwise it returns None. | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:721-722<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_client.py:197-204<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_client.py:282-299 | No cancel path exists. A late TaskProgress still sets the orphaned Event, which is harmless. |
| G05 | ✅ 확인 | On the daemon, WSServer._recv_loop parses the frame, and _handle_task_request schedules run_task with asyncio.create_task on uvicorn's main event loop. run_task forwards head (reshaped 4x4), antennas, duration, method and body_yaw to Backend.goto_target. It turns any exception into TaskProgress.error and broadcasts TaskProgress(finished=True) to every /ws/sdk client. | reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:146-160<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:174-200<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/sdk_ws.py:8-14<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/main.py:593-599<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/main.py:639-640 |  |
| G06 | ✅ 확인 | GotoTargetCmd (type 'goto_target') has no method field, and pydantic's default extra='ignore' silently discards a 'method' key sent by a client. process_command then calls _async_goto, which calls goto_target without method. So goto_target frames on the WebRTC data channel, and typed goto_target frames on /ws/sdk, always use MIN_JERK. On /ws/sdk such a frame also gets no completion reply, because send_response is a no-op there. | reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:234-241<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:950<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1701-1708<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:2658-2673<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:3027-3057<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:166-172<br>probes/goto_pipeline/check_protocol.out.txt |  |
| G07 | 🟡 부분 수정 | POST /api/move/goto accepts 'interpolation' (default minjerk) but never passes it to backend.goto_target, so REST gotos always use MIN_JERK. The endpoint returns a MoveUUID immediately. Completion is reported only as move_started, move_completed, move_failed or move_cancelled on /api/move/ws/updates. POST /api/move/stop can cancel only REST-created tasks (task.cancel()). | reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:34-41<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:76-128<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:137-149<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:195-212<br>probes/goto_pipeline/check_protocol.out.txt | POST /api/move/goto accepts 'interpolation' (default minjerk) but never passes it to backend.goto_target, so REST gotos always use MIN_JERK. The endpoint returns a MoveUUID immediately. Completion shows up as move_started/move_completed/move_failed/move_cancelled events on /api/move/ws/updates, and can also be polled with GET /api/move/running (move.py:131-134). POST /api/move/stop cancels only REST-created tasks (task.cancel()), which leaves the target frozen mid-way. — 'Only on /ws/updates' is overstated. Everything else holds. |
| G08 | ✅ 확인 | In SDK 1.11.0 the Python ReachyMini client never sends GotoTargetCmd. Inside the package, GotoTargetCmd is used only in the docstring example of media/central_consumer.py, an aiortc WebRTC consumer whose protocol 'matches reachy-mini-sdk.js'. The JSON-RPC relay handles only apps.* locally and relays other namespaces to the running app, so it has no motion method. | reachy_mini/media/central_consumer.py:31-57<br>reachy_mini/daemon/jsonrpc_relay.py:81-155<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:705-722 | The docstring's 'matches reachy-mini-sdk.js' refers to the central signaling protocol summary, not to the data-channel command set. The conclusion still holds for any WebRTC client: the typed goto loses the method. |
| G09 | ✅ 확인 | Backend.goto_target builds GotoMove from the present (measured) state, not from the last target. start_head_pose is current_head_pose: on the robot, FK of the last-read motor positions, refreshed every 50 Hz tick; in MuJoCo, the 'head' site pose; in mockup, FK of the previously applied target. start_body_yaw is present head joint 0, and start_antennas are the present antenna positions. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:966-977<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1088-1098<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1116-1157<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:236-247<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:230-243<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:294-308<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mockup_sim/backend.py:89-105 | On the robot, start_head_pose is FK from the last 50 Hz tick, but start_body_yaw and start_antennas come from a fresh c.get_last_position() call, so they are sampled at slightly different instants. The MuJoCo head-site pose also has z -= 0.177 m applied. |
| G10 | ✅ 확인 | GotoMove holds any omitted channel at its start (measured) value. evaluate(t) computes s = time_trajectory(t/D, method) and returns linear_pose_interpolation(start, target, s, yaw_as_scalar=True): translation linear, yaw as a scalar xyz-Euler angle, and the roll/pitch residual by a scaled rotation vector. Antennas and body_yaw are linear in s. With CARTOON, s spans [-0.100, 1.100], so head, antennas and body yaw all anticipate and overshoot by about 10% of the step. | reachy_mini/motion/goto.py:30-44<br>reachy_mini/motion/goto.py:51-74<br>reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:59-105<br>reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:117-153<br>probes/goto_pipeline/cartoon_range.out.txt |  |
| G11 | ✅ 확인 | Backend.play_move uses play_frequency=100.0, and goto_target never overrides it. It loops while time.time()-t0 < D: it re-reads t, evaluates, writes targets, then sleeps 1/100 s minus the iteration's compute time, or 1 ms if the iteration overran. Timing is indexed to wall-clock time, so a late tick skips ahead in the trajectory instead of slowing the motion. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:826-833<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:866<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:879<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:903-925<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:966-977 | time.time() is an adjustable wall clock, not a monotonic one (see G14). |
| G12 | ✅ 확인 | Nothing writes the goal after the play_move loop. The target that stays held is the sample at t_last < D, and the SDK states this itself in the INIT_TARGET_ATOL comment. In the offline harness, 0 of 24 gotos ended with target == goal (atol 1e-9, rtol 0). | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:903-942<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1233-1236<br>probes/goto_pipeline/harness_play_move.out.txt |  |
| G13 | 🟡 부분 수정 | If the last sample falls one period P before D, the fraction of the move still missing is P/D for LINEAR, about 10(P/D)^3 for MIN_JERK and 2(P/D)^2 for EASE_IN_OUT. CARTOON ends past the goal by 2*c2*(P/D)^2 - 4(c2+1)(P/D)^3, with c2 about 2.595. At P=10 ms that is 2% / 0.008% / 0.08% / -0.2% for D=0.5 s, and 10% / 0.86% / 2% / -3.75% for D=0.1 s. Measured in the harness on this Windows PC (30 deg yaw + 10 mm goto): LINEAR ended 0.44-0.90 deg short at D=0.5 s and 2.4-3.0 deg short at D=0.1 s; MIN_JERK ended at most 0.007 deg short at D=0.5 s; D=0.005 s gave a single sample at t=0 and no motion. | reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:117-153<br>probes/goto_pipeline/harness_play_move.out.txt<br>probes/goto_pipeline/short_duration_check.out.txt | The formulas are correct. If the last sample falls one period P before D, the missing fraction is P/D for LINEAR, about 10(P/D)^3 for MIN_JERK and 2(P/D)^2 for EASE_IN_OUT, and CARTOON overshoots by 2c2(P/D)^2-4(c2+1)(P/D)^3 with c2=2.5949. At P=10 ms that gives 2% / 0.0078% / 0.08% / -0.196% at D=0.5 s and 10% / 0.86% / 2% / -3.75% at D=0.1 s. On this Windows PC, however, real ticks are about 15-25 ms, so measured shortfalls change from run to run and can exceed the P=10 ms bound. The verifier saw (30 deg yaw + 10 mm): LINEAR 0.03-0.55 deg short at D=0.5 s and 0.47-5.1 deg short at D=0.1 s; MIN_JERK at most 0.0085 deg short at D=0.5 s, but up to 9.65 deg (32%) short at D=0.05 s. D=0.005 s produced a single t=0 sample and no motion. The analyst's ranges are samples from one session, not bounds. — The single-sample result for D=0.005 s was reproduced: any D shorter than one tick is a no-op. |
| G14 | ✅ 확인 | time_trajectory raises ValueError for t/D > 1. play_move re-reads time.time() after the loop test without clamping; the client-side async_play_move clamps t to D-0.01, but the daemon does not. A clock update between the test and the read would therefore fail the goto with 'time value is out of range [0,1]', which the client receives as an Exception. | reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:121-122<br>reachy_mini/motion/goto.py:57<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:904-913<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:1155<br>probes/goto_pipeline/harness_play_move.out.txt | time.time() is adjustable. A backward clock step gives t<0, which also raises ValueError, and a forward step ends the goto early. The race window is microseconds wide, so the failure is rare. |
| G15 | ✅ 확인 | The single-move guard is a threading.RLock taken with acquire(blocking=False). Every /ws/sdk task, typed /ws/sdk command and REST goto runs on the same uvicorn event-loop thread, so a second goto issued there re-enters the lock and runs alongside the first. In the harness, _active_move_depth reached 2, the commanded yaw flipped between the two trajectories 1361 times, and both calls returned without error. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:282-291<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:484-499<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:844-853<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:2456-2458<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:174-200<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:137-149<br>probes/goto_pipeline/harness_play_move.out.txt | Reproduced: depth reached 2 and both calls returned None with no error. The flip count depends on timing: one run had 46 direction flips, and 12 runs had 3710-4331 yaw jumps larger than 5 deg (busy loop, see G26). |
| G16 | ✅ 확인 | WebRTC data-channel commands and the daemon's idle-reset task run on a separate backend event-loop thread. While a move runs on one thread, a goto from the other thread is dropped with only an 'Ignoring play_move request' log line, and play_move returns None. The /ws/sdk client then receives TaskProgress(finished=True, error=None) almost immediately: 0.28 ms instead of 500 ms in the harness. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:844-846<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:2994-3002<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:3027-3057<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:3104-3112<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:3149-3157<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:179-198<br>probes/goto_pipeline/harness_play_move.out.txt | The WebRTC side has the same blind spot: if its goto is dropped, _async_goto still replies completed:true (abstract.py:2667-2671). |
| G17 | ✅ 확인 | While any move runs, process_command ignores SetTarget, SetHeadJoints, SetBodyYaw, SetAntennas and SetFullTarget, logging a warning only on the daemon, yet still acks {'status':'ok'}; /ws/sdk discards acks anyway. REST /api/move/set_target, by contrast, returns {'status':'ignored'}. The harness confirmed the target did not change. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1661-1699<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:166-172<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:216-233<br>probes/goto_pipeline/harness_play_move.out.txt | The streaming WS /api/move/ws/set_target sends nothing back for an ignored target; it replies only on errors. |
| G18 | ✅ 확인 | StopMoveCmd sets a global flag that every tick polls, so the goto ends normally (no error to the client) with the target frozen mid-way; in the harness it returned 15 ms after the stop with yaw frozen at 8.5 of 30 deg. CancelMoveCmd only cancels uploaded moves, matched by upload_id. ReachyMini.cancel_move() only sets a client-side flag and stops audio. The Python API therefore has no method that stops a daemon goto; WSClient.send_command(StopMoveCmd()) is type-valid but was not tested against a live daemon. | reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:844-876<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:848-853<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:903-910<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:2130-2133<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:2419-2463<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:1105-1113<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_client.py:145-159<br>probes/goto_pipeline/harness_play_move.out.txt | Verifier: the goto returned 14.1 ms and 15.3 ms after the stop, with no exception, frozen at 4.56 and 15.16 of 30 deg (stops at 0.29 s and 0.50 s). A stop sent while idle acks stopped:false. |
| G19 | 🟡 부분 수정 | set_target_head_pose stores the pose and sets ik_required, unless head tracking weight is >= 1. set_target_body_yaw stores the yaw (deduplicated within 1e-9) and sets ik_required. set_target_antenna_joint_positions only stores the value. These are latest-value fields with no queue. ik_required is never cleared after IK (only the joint and current setters clear it), so IK runs on every control tick while a Cartesian target exists. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:260<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:596-638<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:657-667<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:824<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:253-257 | set_target_head_pose stores the pose and sets ik_required, unless a tracking aim exists AND the tracking weight is >= 1. set_target_body_yaw stores the yaw (skipped if within 1e-9 of the stored value) and sets ik_required. set_target_antenna_joint_positions only stores the value. All three are latest-value fields with no queue. ik_required is cleared only by set_target_head_joint_positions and set_target_head_joint_current, so after any Cartesian target, IK reruns on every control tick. — Minor: the early return needs _tracking_aim to be non-None as well as weight >= 1. |
| G20 | ✅ 확인 | update_target_head_joints_from_ik blends in the tracking aim and adds speech-wobble offsets, then calls head_kinematics.ik(pose, body_yaw). Any NaN raises ValueError. Every control loop catches it (throttled warning; the mockup backend is silent) and keeps the previous joint targets, so an unreachable sample freezes the joints while the goto still reports success. Offline, AnalyticalKinematics.ik returns NaN for z+50 mm, roll 60 deg and pitch 80 deg. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:544-594<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:253-260<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:247-255<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mockup_sim/backend.py:108-114<br>probes/goto_pipeline/check_ik_failure_path.out.txt | z+30 mm is already unreachable (NaN). |

Offline, a 170 deg head yaw with body_yaw=0 produced a body joint of 105 deg. | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:113<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:161<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:1096-1103<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:152<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:210-213<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1159-1166<br>reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:65-97<br>reachy_mini/daemon/daemon.py:330<br>probes/goto_pipeline/check_ik_failure_path.out.txt | AnalyticalKinematics also defaults to automatic_body_yaw=True, so the daemon runs in this mode with or without the Python client, and the setting is daemon-global. | G22 | ✅ 확인 | RobotBackend runs a 50 Hz loop and sleeps 1 ms when a tick overruns. Only then does it read get_last_position, run FK, head tracking and IK, and publish. | G23 | ✅ 확인 | MujocoBackend steps physics every 2 ms. On every 10th step (nominally 50 Hz) it reads qpos, sets current_head_pose from the 'head' site, runs IK, and writes data.ctrl[:7] and the negated antenna targets in the same tick. All 9 actuators use the 'perfect_actuator' class (kp=10, kv=0.1, force +/-20) rather than the XC330 or STS3215 motor classes. The loop only sleeps max(0, 2 ms - took), with no real-time locking. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:90-93<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:227-288<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:294-308<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:328-335<br>reachy_mini/descriptions/reachy_mini/mjcf/reachy_mini.xml:20-25<br>reachy_mini/descriptions/reachy_mini/mjcf/reachy_mini.xml:624-634<br>probes/goto_pipeline/check_mujoco_actuators.out.txt | 'Idealized lower bound' is an assumption, not a measurement. Offline, a 10 deg yaw_body step gives t63 = 36 ms, t90 = 54 ms and 4.9% overshoot, and frictionloss 0.1 / kp 10 implies a dead band of about 0.57 deg. |
| G24 | ✅ 확인 | Each control tick publishes JointPositionsMsg (measured 7 head + 2 antenna joints) and HeadPoseMsg (present pose) via call_soon_threadsafe into per-client asyncio queues (maxsize 100, drop-oldest). The messages carry no timestamp and no commanded values, and WSClient keeps only the latest sample. DaemonStatus, including the robot's control_loop_stats, is pushed at 1 Hz. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:263-285<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:305-323<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:86-103<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:126<br>reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:995-1007<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_client.py:183-226<br>reachy_mini/daemon/daemon.py:811-839 | On the wireless robot an ImuDataMsg is added every tick (3 msgs/tick), so the 100-slot queue holds only about 0.67 s of backlog. |
| G25 | ❌ 반박 | GET /api/state/full and WS /api/state/ws/full?frequency=... can return target_head_pose, target_head_joints (post-IK), target_body_yaw and target_antennas_position. These come in one snapshot together with present values and a daemon UTC timestamp. The WS stream defaults to 10 Hz and the rate is configurable. | reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/state.py:88-142<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/state.py:145-196<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/main.py:312-334 | GET /api/state/full and WS /api/state/ws/full accept the with_target_* flags and read backend.target_*. But FullState declares only control_mode, head_pose, head_joints, body_yaw, antennas_position, timestamp, passive_joints, doa and imu, and pydantic's default extra='ignore' silently discards the target values. Responses therefore carry only present values plus a UTC timestamp. with_target_head_pose=True raises an AssertionError while no target exists. The WS sleeps 1/frequency after each frame, so the real rate is below the requested one (default 10 Hz). In SDK 1.11.0 no daemon API returns commanded or post-IK targets. — This invalidates the HW4 implication and the 'Rates' table row that call this endpoint the source of commanded-vs-measured pairs. |
| G26 | 🟡 부분 수정 | On this Windows / Python 3.12 PC, time.monotonic() advances in about 16 ms steps, and asyncio treats any timer due within 15.625 ms as ready. As a result, the real Backend.play_move ran at about 65 Hz on an idle loop (median tick 15.1-15.6 ms) and at irregular 7.5-24 ms ticks with 50 Hz cross-thread wakeups. Two overlapping gotos busy-looped (961 and 989 evaluations for 0.8 s and 0.4 s moves). The robot's Linux daemon should run close to 100 Hz, but this was not measured. | cpython-3.12/Lib/asyncio/base_events.py:421<br>cpython-3.12/Lib/asyncio/base_events.py:738-745<br>cpython-3.12/Lib/asyncio/base_events.py:1953-1974<br>probes/goto_pipeline/timing_probe_stdlib.out.txt<br>probes/goto_pipeline/harness_play_move.out.txt | On this PC, time.monotonic (GetTickCount64) steps about every 16 ms, and asyncio runs every timer due before now+15.625 ms. asyncio.sleep is quantized to about 16 ms (median 15.8 ms for sleep(0.001), 31.9 ms for sleep(0.02)), while time.sleep is precise. An idle play_move therefore ticks every 15-16.5 ms (about 60-66 Hz). With cross-thread call_soon_threadsafe wakeups at 50 Hz, ticks become irregular, from under 1 ms up to about 21-24 ms (verifier: mean 11.8, median 12.3, p5 0.65 ms), because a woken loop fires timers up to 15.6 ms early. Two overlapping gotos on one loop can ping-pong without ever blocking: the analyst saw 961/989 evaluations, and the verifier saw 2958-4907 head writes in 0.8 s in 18 of 19 runs, with requested 10 ms sleeps lasting a median of 0.09 ms. One run had only 73 writes, so the busy loop depends on phase. The Linux daemon was not measured. — The idle ~65 Hz comes from wait quantization, not from the early-firing rule. Early firing explains the short ticks and the busy loop. |
| G27 | 🟡 부분 수정 | A stdlib copy of the MuJoCo loop's time.sleep(max(0, 0.002 - took)) averaged 2.70 ms per step on this PC (median 2.51 ms). Goto trajectories, meanwhile, are timed by the wall clock. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:283-288<br>probes/goto_pipeline/timing_probe_stdlib.out.txt | A stdlib copy of the MuJoCo pacing loop, time.sleep(max(0, 0.002 - took)), averaged 2.46-2.70 ms per step across sessions (median 2.51 ms). That means a control tick of about 37-41 Hz and about 0.74-0.81x real time, before any physics or viewer cost. Goto trajectories, meanwhile, are timed by the wall clock. — The median reproduces exactly; the mean depends on the session. |
| G28 | ✅ 확인 | Backend.goto_joint_positions (joint-space, honours method) has no caller anywhere in the package. ReachyMini._goto_joint_positions (private) streams minimum-jerk joint targets via SetHeadJointsCmd, bypassing IK. ReachyMini.play_move/async_play_move streams a Move's evaluate() at 100 Hz with SetTargetCmd, SetBodyYawCmd and SetAntennasCmd, and clamps t to D-0.01. So a GotoMove played client-side honours any method but also never sends the exact endpoint. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:979-1034<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:872-924<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:961-993<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:1115-1171 | The client-side loop is paced by asyncio.sleep, so on Windows it runs at about 60 Hz (v7). The t clamp to D-0.01 means it always stops at least 10 ms before D. |
| G29 | 🟡 부분 수정 | Backend.goto_target does not disable head tracking or speech wobbling; only goto_sleep and reset_to_sleep call _quiesce_aim_sources. So an active tracking aim and wobble offsets are blended into every IK target during a goto. With tracking weight >= 1, set_target_head_pose does not even arm IK. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:566-583<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:596-609<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:944-977<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1325-1384 | Backend.goto_target does not disable head tracking or speech wobbling; only goto_sleep and reset_to_sleep call _quiesce_aim_sources. An active tracking aim (weighted by _tracking_weight) and the wobble offsets are therefore blended into every IK target during a goto. When a tracking aim exists with weight >= 1, set_target_head_pose does not arm IK, and step_head_tracking re-arms IK every tick with the aim fully replacing the goto pose. — Same minor condition as G19: the aim must exist. The main conclusion holds. |
| G30 | ✅ 확인 | HW3 already streams client-side trajectories with mini.set_target, omitting body_yaw so it stays unchanged. The trajectories are evaluated with its own minimum-jerk law 'so they can be stopped at any tick'. HW3 also timestamps events with perf_counter_ns, because time.time and time.monotonic tick every 15.6 ms on Windows / Python 3.12. | reachy_hw3: reachy_s2s/motion/sinks.py:46-48<br>reachy_hw3: reachy_s2s/motion/trajectory.py:1-6<br>reachy_hw3: reachy_s2s/motion/trajectory.py:19-23<br>reachy_hw3: reachy_s2s/evaluation/event_logger.py:1-7 | HW3 also paces its motion thread with perf_counter_ns + time.sleep (reachy_s2s/motion/controller.py:16-20,410-419) and has platform_timing.enable_high_resolution_timer. That thread design sidesteps the Windows asyncio quantization described in G26. |

**검증자가 추가한 발견**

| 내용 | 근거 |
|---|---|
| No daemon interface in SDK 1.11.0 exposes commanded targets (pre- or post-IK), and REST and /ws/sdk clients cannot read is_move_running. FullState drops the target fields. StateSnapshot (WebRTC only) has is_move_running but no targets. DaemonStatus has neither. Daemon recording stores only records the client appends. Commanded values must therefore be logged by the HW4 client itself (client-side streaming), or a daemon goto must be reconstructed from the measured start pose, its parameters and the unknown tick times. Motor target angles must be recomputed offline with AnalyticalKinematics using the same automatic_body_yaw setting. | reachy_mini/daemon/app/models.py:149-160; reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/state.py:114-142; reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:125-150,164-184; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1045-1057,1577-1609; grep is_move_running (only protocol.py:145, abstract.py, move.py:222); verify_goto_pipeline/v6_ik_mj_state.out.txt |
| Windows asyncio ping-pong: any two coroutines on one loop that sleep for less than 15.625 ms keep each other's timers 'due' and the loop never blocks. Requested 10 ms sleeps lasted a median of 0.09 ms, and overlapping gotos wrote 2958-4907 head targets in 0.8 s in 18 of 19 runs. asyncio.sleep alone is also quantized to about 16 ms. An HW4 Executor running on Windows should pace with a dedicated thread using perf_counter + time.sleep (the HW3 pattern), not with asyncio.sleep. Linux, with 1 ns monotonic resolution, is not affected. | Python 3.12 Lib/asyncio/base_events.py:421,1953-1974; verify_goto_pipeline/v3_overlap.out.txt, v4_overlap_diag.out.txt, v7_sleep_granularity.out.txt; reachy_hw3: reachy_s2s/motion/controller.py:410-419 |
| On Windows, short daemon gotos are truncated for every method, not only LINEAR, because play_move ticks are 15-25 ms. At D=0.05 s (3-4 samples) MIN_JERK ended up to 9.65 deg and LINEAR up to 12.9 deg short of a 30 deg goal; EASE_IN_OUT ended up to 5.9 deg short. Any D shorter than one tick commands nothing (only s(0) is written). | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:903-925; verify_goto_pipeline/v2_harness.out.txt T1 |
| On the robot, antennas and head are skewed by one control tick. Antenna targets are written at the next tick (0-20 ms after play_move sets them), while head and body joints come from IK computed in the following tick and are written one tick later (about 20-40 ms). In MuJoCo both are written in the same tick, so the sim-vs-hardware difference must be accounted for in synchronization and lag analysis. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:203-228,253-257; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:247-260; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mockup_sim/backend.py:89-114 |
| Client-side ReachyMini.play_move/async_play_move clamps t to D-0.01, so it always stops at least 10 ms before D: a deterministic endpoint shortfall of at least 0.01/D for LINEAR, worse than the daemon path's random (0, P) tail. Its 100 Hz asyncio loop also runs at about 60 Hz on Windows. HW4 streaming should write the exact final keyframe explicitly. | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:1144-1169; verify_goto_pipeline/v7_sleep_granularity.out.txt |
| The daemon never validates goto duration. Backend.goto_target has no check, and the REST GotoModelRequest.duration and GotoTargetCmd.duration have no positivity constraint. D <= 0 returns immediately with no motion and is reported as success (move_completed / completed:true / TaskProgress without error). The Python client's `duration <= 0` check lets NaN through. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:944-977; reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:34-41,93-99; reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:234-241; reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:700-703; verify_goto_pipeline/v2_harness.out.txt T8 (D=0 returned in 0.044 ms with 0 writes), v1_static.out.txt (NaN duration sent) |
| Automatic body yaw is ON by default inside the daemon (AnalyticalKinematics default) and is a daemon-global switch that any client's SetAutomaticBodyYawCmd flips. It clamps \|head yaw - body yaw\| to 65 deg, so the body_yaw commanded by a goto may never be reached. Offline: identity head with body_yaw 114.6 deg gives body joint 65 deg; head yaw 100 with body 0 gives 35 deg; head yaw 90 with body 30 gives 30 deg. | reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:33,80-97; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:210-213,1159-1166,1809-1811; reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:161; verify_goto_pipeline/v6_ik_mj_state.out.txt |
| MuJoCo actuator dynamics measured offline: a 10 deg yaw_body step (kp 10, kv 0.1, joint damping 0.15, armature 0.001, frictionloss 0.1) reaches 63% in 36 ms and 90% in 54 ms, overshoots 4.9%, and keeps a friction dead band of about 0.01 rad (0.57 deg). Sim lag and RMSE therefore include their own actuator dynamics on top of the 0-20 ms tick, and calling them a 'lower bound' for hardware is unverified. | reachy_mini/descriptions/reachy_mini/mjcf/reachy_mini.xml:20-25,624-634; verify_goto_pipeline/v6_ik_mj_state.out.txt |
| A goto's TaskProgress can be lost (code-derived risk, not tested). It goes through the same 100-slot drop-oldest per-client queue as the 50 Hz state stream: 2 msgs/tick, or 3 with the IMU on the wireless robot, i.e. 100-150 msg/s. A send-side stall of about 0.67-1 s (for example a Wi-Fi hiccup) can evict it, and the blocked goto_target then raises TimeoutError after D+1 s even though the move completed. | reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:86-103,126,140-144,189-198; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:263-285; reachy_mini/io/ws_client.py:282-292 |
| False completions are not limited to /ws/sdk. When the cross-thread guard drops a goto, the WebRTC typed goto still replies {status: ok, command: goto_target, completed: true}, and a REST goto still emits move_completed. No path reports 'ignored' except the REST set_target endpoint. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:844-846,2658-2673; reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:93-99,216-225; verify_goto_pipeline/v2_harness.out.txt T4 |

수치 재계산: 34개 중 28개 일치. 불일치:
- Harness LINEAR shortfall D=0.5 s: 0.44-0.90 deg, 0.15-0.30 mm: Not reproducible as a range. Verifier got 0.026-0.555 deg and 0.009-0.185 mm in 3 runs; the value depends on tick phase (bound about 1.5 deg at 25 ms ticks)
- Harness LINEAR shortfall D=0.1 s: 2.37-2.99 deg: Verifier got 0.475-5.119 deg in 3 runs (5.1 deg = 17%, above the 10% P=10 ms bound)
- time.sleep(0.002) loop: median 2.51, mean 2.70 ms (about 37 Hz): Median reproduced (2.51). Mean was 2.46-2.49 ms in 3 reps, giving about 40 Hz and 0.81x real time; the mean depends on the session
- Table 'Rates', row /api/state/ws/full 'can include target + present': Targets are dropped by FullState (G25)
- Table 'Measured with the offline harness': Run-dependent. For example, D=0.05 minjerk is listed as 0.14-0.28 deg, but the verifier saw 0.0098-9.65 deg; D=0.05 linear was 0.03-12.9 deg; D=1.0 linear was 0.019-0.065 deg against the listed 0.28-0.39
- Table 'Goto entry points', daemon-internal row 'body_yaw 0.0 default': Mostly right, but the play_move initial goto passes the move's start body_yaw (abstract.py:857-865), not 0.0

## 보간 4종의 수학 (`interpolation_math`)

| ID | 판정 | 주장 (분석 원문) | 근거 (파일:줄) | 검증 메모 / 수정 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C01 | ✅ 확인 | time_trajectory(t, method) raises ValueError('time value is out of range [0,1]') for t<0 or t>1 before dispatching; InterpolationTechnique is a str Enum ('linear','minjerk','ease_in_out','cartoon'), so plain strings work and unknown strings raise ValueError. | reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:108-122<br>reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:147-153<br>probes/interpolation_math/verify_output.txt (domain block: tt(-1e-12), tt(1+1e-12) raise; 'minjerk' string returns 0.16308; 'foo' raises) | Over the Python SDK WS path an unknown string never reaches time_trajectory: GotoTaskRequest.method is typed InterpolationTechnique (reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:1081-1088), so pydantic rejects it on the client. NaN passes the range check (see C27). |
| C02 | ✅ 확인 | LINEAR returns s=tau: s'=1, s''=s'''=0 inside (0,1); the velocity steps 0->1 at tau=0 and 1->0 at tau=1, i.e. acceleration impulses (unbounded accel/jerk) at both ends; peak/average velocity ratio = 1. | reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:124-125<br>probes/interpolation_math/verify_output.txt (linear: s'(0+)=1, s'(1-)=1, s''~0) |  |
| C03 | ✅ 확인 | MIN_JERK returns s=10tau^3-15tau^4+6tau^5, exactly the quintic smoothstep ('smootherstep'), which is the normalized rest-to-rest minimum-jerk profile; s'=30tau^2(1-tau)^2, s''=60tau(1-tau)(1-2tau), s'''=60(1-6tau+6tau^2). | reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:127-128<br>probes/interpolation_math/verify_interpolation.py (ANALYTIC table, numpy Polynomial derivatives)<br>probes/interpolation_math/verify_output.txt (minjerk: max\|code-analytic\| = 4.552e-15) |  |
| C04 | ✅ 확인 | MIN_JERK: s(0)=0, s(1)=1, s'(0)=s'(1)=0, s''(0)=s''(1)=0; peak s'=15/8=1.875 at tau=0.5 (peak/avg = 1.875); peak \|s''\|=10/sqrt(3)=5.774 at tau=(3-+sqrt3)/6=0.2113/0.7887; jerk is bounded (60 at the ends, -30 at tau=0.5) but steps from 0 to 60 at tau=0. | reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:127-128<br>probes/interpolation_math/verify_output.txt (minjerk: peak s'=1.875 @0.5, peak \|s''\|=5.774 @0.7885, s'''(numeric, excl. edges)=59.28 vs analytic 60) | The numeric peak \|s''\| of 5.777 at 0.7883 (analyst: 5.774 at 0.7885) is second-difference round-off; the analytic value is correct. |
| C05 | ✅ 확인 | EASE_IN_OUT returns 2tau^2 for tau<0.5 and 1-(-2tau+2)^2/2 = 1-2(1-tau)^2 for tau>=0.5, exactly easeInOutQuad; s'=4tau \| 4(1-tau), s''=+4 \| -4, s'''=0 inside each half. | reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:130-134<br>probes/interpolation_math/verify_output.txt (ease_in_out: max\|code-analytic\| = 3.331e-16) |  |
| C06 | ✅ 확인 | EASE_IN_OUT: peak s'=2 at tau=0.5 (continuous: 2/2 one-sided; peak/avg = 2); s'(0)=s'(1)=0; s'' steps 0->+4 at tau=0, +4->-4 at tau=0.5 (jump 8) and -4->0 at tau=1, i.e. three unbounded-jerk instants. | reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:130-134<br>probes/interpolation_math/verify_output.txt (ease_in_out: s''(0+)=4, s''(1-)=-4, s''(0.5-)=4, s''(0.5+)=-4) |  |

The robot loop runs at control_loop_frequency=50 Hz. MuJoCo runs FK/IK/ctrl once every 10 physics steps of 0.002 s, which is 50 Hz of simulated time; its wall-clock pacing comes only from time.sleep(max(0, 0.002 - took)). The mockup runs at control_frequency=50 Hz. ik_required is never cleared after IK, so every control tick re-runs IK on the latest target_head_pose, and the ~100 Hz goto samples reach IK as a zero-order hold at the control rate. set_target_head_pose stores the pose but skips setting ik_required when daemon head tracking is active with weight >= 1 (abstract.py:606-609). On this Windows host the zero-physics MuJoCo pacing replica ran at 1.20-1.24 s of wall time per simulated second, i.e. <= ~41 Hz wall-clock control, so 50 Hz is not a wall-clock guarantee for MuJoCo here. |

When the bus write actually happens is inside the compiled reachy_mini_motor_controller (.pyd), so the total latency cannot be verified from source. The mockup also applies the IK result one tick later; MuJoCo writes ctrl in the same tick. — The code order is certain. The '+20 ms' is a Python-level difference between the head and antenna paths, not a measured motor latency. |

**검증자가 추가한 발견**

| 내용 | 근거 |
|---|---|
| While any daemon move is running (goto_target, play_move, wake_up and so on), all streaming target commands are silently dropped. SetTargetCmd, SetFullTargetCmd, SetHeadJointsCmd, SetBodyYawCmd and SetAntennasCmd are ignored with only a warning, and REST /move/set_target returns status 'ignored'. A HW4 executor therefore cannot overlay set_target streaming (e.g. antenna secondary motion) on an active goto_target; each segment must use one path. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1661-1699 (block_targets = self.is_move_running) and 483-486; reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:216-233; client setters reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:995-1026 and 650-656. |
| ReachyMini.goto_target defaults body_yaw to 0.0, not None, so every goto that omits body_yaw also interpolates the body back to 0 rad. GotoMove keeps the current body yaw only when target_body_yaw is None. REST /move/goto and GotoTargetCmd, in contrast, default body_yaw to None. Keyframe sequences must pass body_yaw=None explicitly to hold the body. | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:680, 689 and 705-719; reachy_mini/motion/goto.py:38-41; reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:234-241; reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:39; probes/verify_interpolation_math/vim_math_out.txt ('target_body_yaw=None keeps start' = 0.4). |
| On the Wireless robot the daemon (the backend goto/play_move loop and the 50 Hz control loop) runs on the onboard CM4 under Linux. The Windows asyncio quantum of about 15.6 ms (C24, the T3 'Windows asyncio tick' rows, T4) therefore applies to a MuJoCo or mockup daemon on this PC and to client-side loops, not to hardware goto_target. The CM4 loop rate has not been measured. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:340-341 ('bundled inside the desktop app or running on the CM4'); reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:131-142 (BMI088 IMU on i2c bus 4 when wireless_version); reachy_mini/daemon/daemon.py:368-371. |
| On this Windows host, MuJoCo probably runs slower than real time while goto timing uses the wall clock (time.time()). Each 2 ms physics step is paced only by time.sleep(max(0, 0.002-took)). A replica of that pacing with no physics took 1.197-1.238 s of wall time per simulated second, i.e. 40-42 control ticks per wall second. In sim time a goto would then be compressed by at least about 20%, and the control rate would be <= ~41 Hz wall-clock. Before any MuJoCo lag/RMSE analysis, measure data.time against the wall clock in the real sim daemon; this replica excludes the cost of mj_step. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:90-91 and 227-288; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:879 and 904-925; probes/verify_interpolation_math/vim_mj_pacing_out.txt; probes/verify_interpolation_math/vim_timing_out.txt (time.sleep(0.002) median 2.50 ms, time.sleep(0.0017) median 2.01 ms). |
| On Windows the overrun branch asyncio.sleep(0.001) also takes about 15 ms (median 15.35 ms), and single loop intervals of 20-31 ms occur (my max 25.9 ms, analyst max 30.6 ms). The T3 rows for dt=0.015625 are therefore typical, not worst case. With dt=0.026 s and D=0.3 s the endpoint residual is 8.67% for LINEAR, 0.569% for MIN_JERK, 1.50% for EASE_IN_OUT and -2.96% for CARTOON; with dt=0.0306 s it is 10.2%, 0.905%, 2.08% and -3.87%. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:921-925; reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:1165-1169; probes/verify_interpolation_math/vim_timing_out.txt; probes/verify_interpolation_math/vim_worst_dt_out.txt. |
| The 'one move at a time' guard does not serialize goto requests that arrive as asyncio tasks. _play_move_lock is a threading.RLock taken with acquire(blocking=False), and every WS GotoTaskRequest and REST /move/goto runs as an asyncio task on the same uvicorn event-loop thread. A second goto issued while one is running (e.g. from two FSM threads or clients) re-enters the lock, and both GotoMove loops then interleave their set_target writes. A pure-Python replica showed both tasks running at depth=2, while a call from another thread was rejected. This has not been observed on a real daemon. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:282-286, 488-499 and 844-853; reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:174-200; reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:76-108 and 137-149; probes/verify_interpolation_math/vim_rlock_probe_out.txt. |
| The pose that reaches IK can differ from the GotoMove output. update_target_head_joints_from_ik blends it toward the daemon head-tracking aim when the tracking weight is > 0 and adds the speech-wobbler offsets, and set_target_head_pose does not set ik_required at all when the tracking weight is >= 1. Both are off by default (_tracking_enabled=False, zero offsets), but HW4 telemetry must log or disable them so that the 'commanded' pose it records is the actual IK input. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:346-358, 556-583, 596-609, 669-700 and 721-758. |
| Body yaw is not passed through unchanged. The default engine is AnalyticalKinematics(automatic_body_yaw=True), which calls the compiled inverse_kinematics_safe(max_relative_yaw=65 deg, max_body_yaw=160 deg) and redistributes yaw between body and head. In an offline probe, head yaw 80 deg with body_yaw=0 gave a body joint of 0.2618 rad (15 deg). The body joint and its velocity therefore deviate from start + delta*s whenever the head-relative yaw would exceed 65 deg. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:206-213; reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:33-39 and 65-97; probes/verify_interpolation_math/vim_ik_probe_out.txt. |
| Motor-space speed and acceleration requirements must be computed by differentiating IK outputs, not from k*delta/D in joint space. The compiled IK can be evaluated offline without a robot. For a pure roll move from 0 to 20 deg, the six Stewart joint angles deviate from linear-in-s by up to 0.0066 rad (2.6% of the 0.2565 rad joint excursion) over s in [-0.1, 1.1]. Infeasible poses return NaN rather than clamped joints. | reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:65-97; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:586-588; probes/verify_interpolation_math/vim_ik_probe_out.txt. |
| The individual Euler angles of the commanded head pose are not linear in s. A roll/pitch-only move (10,5,0) -> (-10,15,0) deg produces up to 0.44 deg of yaw. A large mixed move (20,-25,-60) -> (-15,20,70) has a rotation path 4.06% longer than the geodesic and a 3.51 deg yaw deviation. Reference curves for commanded-vs-measured plots must therefore be generated with linear_pose_interpolation(..., yaw_as_scalar=True) itself, not by per-axis interpolation. | reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:74-84; probes/verify_interpolation_math/vim_omega_out.txt. |
| The client-side play_move (async_play_move) of a custom Move sends three separate WS commands per tick (SetTargetCmd, SetBodyYawCmd, SetAntennasCmd) instead of one SetFullTargetCmd. Head, body and antenna updates can therefore land in different 50 Hz control ticks, whereas the daemon-side GotoMove sets all three in one loop iteration. A custom streaming executor should use set_target(), which sends SetFullTargetCmd, to keep channels synchronized. | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:1157-1163, 995-1026 and 610-656; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:913-919 and 1691-1699. |
| On hardware, the 'measured' joint state (the goto start pose and the published joint positions) is the compiled controller's last successfully read motor positions, polled every 1/50 s (read_position_loop_period). It can be up to about one read period old. This biases lag estimates, so it must be accounted for in the RMSE/lag analysis. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:71-79, 236-247 and 480-517; compiled docstring of ReachyMiniPyControlLoop.get_last_position ('Get the last successfully read motor positions'). |
| The SDK uses two rotation-interpolation conventions: GotoMove uses the yaw-as-scalar path, while RecordedMove interpolates between recorded frames with the default geodesic SLERP. A HW4 keyframe Move should choose one deliberately and document the choice. | reachy_mini/motion/goto.py:59-64; reachy_mini/motion/recorded_move.py:163. |

수치 재계산: 35개 중 32개 일치. 불일치:
- asyncio.sleep(0.01) median on this Windows host (Proactor / Selector): Not reproduced exactly: 16.0-16.4 ms (Proactor) and 16.1-16.4 ms (Selector) over my 4 runs. Run-dependent; quote as about 15-16.5 ms.
- play_move replica rate on this Windows host: 58-63 Hz over my 9 runs. Run-dependent; quote as about 58-67 Hz.
- Table T4 measured ranges: Run-dependent. My replica gave 18-19 ticks for D=0.3 (60-63 Hz) and tau_last 0.9517-0.9979; the client replica at D=0.3 hit 0.9667 in 2 of 3 runs.

| K05 | ✅ 확인 | Compiled inverse_kinematics is an exact closed form: per leg, theta_i = atan2(p_y,p_x) + s_i*acos(c/r) with p = anchor_i expressed in the motor frame (motor frames rotated by body_yaw about world z), c = (L^2+\|p\|^2-ROD^2)/(2L), r = hypot(p_x,p_y), s_i = +1 if json solution=1 else -1. A Python re-implementation matched to 3.6e-15 rad, leg-closure residuals were <=2.2e-16 m, the call is stateless and ignores no_iterations/check_collision, and it costs about 3.6 us on this PC. | reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:65-97<br>reachy_mini/assets/kinematics_data.json:2-4<br>probes/kinematics_ik/ik_validate.out.txt (sections 1-2)<br>probes/kinematics_ik/ik_experiments.out.txt (statefulness, timing) | The analyst's 3.6e-15 rad was on their sample; on random poses the agreement is 7.8e-12 rad, still exact. The result equals the daemon's target only if the daemon's tracking/wobbler offsets and body_yaw input are reproduced. |
| K06 | 🟡 부분 수정 | The head joint vector has 7 elements, [body_yaw, stewart_1..stewart_6], in rad. Antennas are a separate 2-vector [right, left]; inside the MuJoCo backend they are stored in [left, right] order with the sign flipped. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:229-240<br>reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:91-97<br>reachy_mini/kinematics/placo_kinematics.py:170-178<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:203-209<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:76-80<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:257-260<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:328-335 | The head joint vector is [body_yaw, stewart_1..stewart_6] in rad. The robot backend sends q[1:] to set_stewart_platform_position and q[0] to set_body_rotation; MuJoCo writes q to ctrl[:7]. Antennas are a separate [right, left] vector, and MuJoCo keeps that order: the actuators end with right_antenna, left_antenna. MuJoCo only flips the sign (ctrl[-2:] = -target, present = -qpos). The '[left, right]' reorder at mujoco/backend.py:76-80 touches only the antisymmetric sleep constant, where reversing equals negating. |

| K12 | 🟡 부분 수정 | With automatic body yaw, inverse_kinematics_safe only alters body yaw: body_out = clip(clip(body_in, psi_head-65deg, psi_head+65deg), -160deg, +160deg), where psi_head is the requested pose's yaw, without angle wrapping (156/156 probe cases match; a wrapped rule mismatched 61). The Stewart joints are then solved exactly for the requested pose (warm FK round trip <=1.5e-4 mm / 1.8e-3 deg at yaw +-90). Here psi = atan2(-R[0,1], R[0,0]) of the requested head rotation, the yaw of an R = Rx*Ry*Rz decomposition; translation has no effect. psi equals the create_head_pose yaw only for pure-yaw poses: it is off by 0.15 deg at roll 10/yaw 80, 1.5 deg at roll 30/yaw 100, and up to about 16 deg for \|roll\|,\|pitch\| <= 30. The Stewart joints are the exact IK at body_out. Head -170 deg with body 160 deg drives the body to -105 deg (a 265 deg swing). The quoted FK round trip (<=1.5e-4 mm / 1.8e-3 deg at yaw +/-90) needs an orthonormal FK seed; with the daemon's constructor seed it is about 0.03 mm / 0.012 deg. |

Body-yaw commands further than 65 deg from the head yaw are silently overridden (head 0, body 90 -> body 65). goto_target defaults to body_yaw=0.0, not None, so every goto re-centres the body unless told otherwise. | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:672-722<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:944-977<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:611-626<br>probes/kinematics_ik/ik_experiments.out.txt (Yaw probes, body_yaw input vs output) | With automatic body yaw and body command b, the body stays at b while \|psi - b\| <= 65 deg, with psi as in K12. For pure yaw, a head yaw within +/-65 deg is done entirely by the Stewart platform (yaw +60 gives s1/s3/s5 = 60.28 deg, body 0). With tilt, the body can move even when the create_head_pose yaw is within +/-65: 168 grid cases moved it by up to about 3 deg. The body stays put for \|yaw\| <= 64.5/63/60.5 deg when \|roll\|,\|pitch\| <= 10/20/30 deg. Body commands farther than 65 deg from psi are silently overridden (head 0, body 90 gives body 65). The Python client goto_target and Backend.goto_target default to body_yaw=0.0 and re-centre the body. The REST /api/move/goto model and the WS GotoTargetCmd default to None, which keeps the body yaw. |

It raises before assigning, so target_head_joint_positions keeps the last valid value. The robot and MuJoCo loops catch it and log 'IK error' throttled to once per 0.5 s; the mockup-sim loop swallows it silently. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:544-594<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:251-261<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:245-255<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mockup_sim/backend.py:107-114 |  |
| K17 | 🟡 부분 수정 | After an IK failure the move is not aborted. ik_required is only cleared by joint/current setters, so IK reruns every 50 Hz tick while play_move keeps updating targets. The goto task still reports finished with error=None, so the client sees no exception, and joint targets jump when the trajectory re-enters the reachable set (offline replay of a 40->60->40 deg pitch ramp: 63/101 ticks held). Comments saying IK 'does nothing if the targets did not change' are wrong, and _fk_kin_tolerance, _ik_kin_tolerance and _last_target_* are never read. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:251-273<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:596-638<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:824<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:904-925<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:251-252<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:174-200<br>reachy_mini/io/ws_client.py:282-299<br>probes/kinematics_ik/ik_extra.out.txt (IK failure along a trajectory) | An IK failure does not abort the move. ik_required is cleared only by set_target_head_joint_positions and set_target_head_joint_current, so IK reruns every 50 Hz tick, even after it succeeds. The joint target stays at the last reachable value, the goto task still reports finished with error=None, and the head resumes when the trajectory re-enters the reachable set. Near the boundary the per-tick joint jumps are 3-4 deg. In my replays a linear 40->60->40 deg pitch triangle over 101 ticks held 45 ticks (pitch > 50.83 deg) and a min-jerk up/down held 47; the analyst's 63/101 was not reproduced and depends on the trajectory. The 'does nothing if the targets did not change' comments are wrong, and _fk_kin_tolerance, _ik_kin_tolerance and _last_target_* are never read. |

Measured convergence is linear (about x0.1 per 3 iterations) down to a floor of about 6e-4 to 1.1e-3 deg and 1e-4 to 4e-4 mm that more iterations do not improve (consistent with an SVD pseudo-inverse Newton step plus a stopping tolerance; the algorithm itself is not readable). | reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:1-5<br>reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:99-142<br>probes/kinematics_ik/fk_floor.out.txt<br>probes/kinematics_ik/pyd_strings_filtered.txt | FK is iterative inside Rust. The wrapper calls forward_kinematics no_iterations times (default 3), each refining an internal seed. If the extracted \|roll\| > 90 deg, it re-seeds at neutral and adds 0.001 rad to the body yaw and every Stewart joint. The pitch test can never fire, because as_euler returns pitch within +/-90. Convergence is linear, about x0.1 per 3-iteration call. The floor depends on the seed. From an orthonormal neutral seed it is 6.5e-4 to 2.3e-3 deg and 1e-4 to 5.5e-4 mm (yaw 40 is the worst case). From the constructor seed the daemon actually uses (SLEEP_HEAD_POSE, det 1.000507), every FK output keeps det(R) = 1.000507 and floors at about 0.03 mm and 0.003-0.012 deg. More iterations do not help either way. |

**검증자가 추가한 발견**

| 내용 | 근거 |
|---|---|
| The daemon's FK is biased by a non-orthonormal seed. AnalyticalKinematics seeds the Rust FK with SLEEP_HEAD_POSE, whose rotation block has det 1.000507. Every later FK result keeps det(R) = 1.000507 (it never drifts back in a 60 s random walk) and carries a floor of about 0.03 mm and 0.003-0.012 deg. Only the upright-fallback reset clears it. On robot and mockup backends this FK output is the published head pose and the goto start pose. Re-arming IK from it moves the joints by about 0.04 deg near neutral and up to 0.45 deg near the sleep pose. HW3 already measured det of about 1.0005 on real-daemon head poses. HW4 telemetry should orthonormalize (SVD) before extracting angles and compute RMSE/peak/lag in joint space. | analytical_kinematics.py:19-27, 57-60; abstract.py:966-977, 1093-1098, 1146; robot/backend.py:172-179, 270-274, 352-367; reachy_hw3: reachy_s2s/motion/pose.py:111-113; probes/verify_kinematics_ik/v_det.out.txt, v_fk_more.out.txt, v_pin.out.txt, v_sleep.out.txt |
| Automatic body yaw makes the joint rates jump at the 65 deg boundary. Just below it the yaw rate is carried by the Stewart motors (d s1/d yaw = 0.458, d body/d yaw = 0). Just above it the body takes 1.0 deg/deg and the Stewart rates drop to 0. A large look-around crossing the boundary therefore has a velocity step on both the body and the Stewart motors, and the body motor needs the full head-yaw rate. The Safety Checker and the speed analysis must model this rather than use the neutral Jacobian. | analytical_kinematics.py:80-90; probes/verify_kinematics_ik/v_kink.out.txt |
| The packaged wireless launcher starts the robot's own daemon with '--wireless-version --no-wake-up-on-start' and no --kinematics-engine or --check-collision. So on the robot (Linux, where Placo could be installed) the daemon also uses AnalyticalKinematics with no collision check, and the Windows argument in K18 is not the only reason. This assumes the robot runs this 1.11.0 launcher; the robot is offline, so it is unconfirmed. | reachy_mini/daemon/app/services/wireless/launcher.sh:18; reachy_mini/daemon/app/services/wireless/reachy-mini-daemon.service:10; main.py:87-88 |
| Restart paths do not keep the start options. Daemon.restart() rebuilds its params from _start_params, which stores neither kinematics_engine nor check_collision, so any restart (including POST /api/daemon/restart) falls back to AnalyticalKinematics with no collision check. POST /api/daemon/start passes args.sim but not args.mockup_sim. A daemon launched with --mockup-sim, stopped, and started through that endpoint would therefore build the real RobotBackend, which matters for HW4's mock-by-default rule. | reachy_mini/daemon/daemon.py:376-383, 647-667, 855-903; reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/daemon.py:24-46, 68-81 |
| A joint-space command path bypasses IK, the NaN check and every range check. SetHeadJointsCmd calls set_target_head_joint_positions, which clears ik_required, so the 7 joints go straight to the motors (robot) or to ctrl (MuJoCo, clamped only by ctrlrange). It is used by the client's underscore-internal _set_joint_positions/_goto_joint_positions and by Backend.goto_joint_positions. HW4 should not build on these internal APIs, or must check joint ranges itself. | abstract.py:628-638, 979-1034, 1661-1679; reachy_mini.py:872-993 |
| Gravity compensation is unavailable with the default engine. RobotBackend.set_motor_control_mode raises RuntimeError for GravityCompensation unless the engine is Placo. The SetGravityCompensationCmd handler only catches ValueError, so this error escapes process_command. The client's enable_gravity_compensation is a kinematics-dependent feature that HW4 cannot use on Windows or with the default launcher. | reachy_mini.py:1088-1094; robot/backend.py:576-580, 632-641; abstract.py:1798-1807 |

수치 재계산: 33개 중 31개 일치. 불일치:
- FK accuracy floor: Seed-dependent. From an orthonormal neutral seed: 6.5e-4 to 2.3e-3 deg and 1e-4 to 5.5e-4 mm (yaw 40 worst). From the daemon's constructor seed (det 1.000507): about 0.03 mm and 0.003-0.012 deg.
- IK call time (auto, incl. wrapper) / FK call time (3 its): IK 3.5-3.7 us reproduced. FK wrapper 33-38 us in 4 runs here (load-dependent; other jobs running), not 22.7. A raw Rust forward_kinematics call is about 4.3 us; the rest appears to be Python-side overhead (inference).

## 제어 루프·백엔드·텔레메트리 (`control_backends_telemetry`)

| ID | 판정 | 주장 (분석 원문) | 근거 (파일:줄) | 검증 메모 / 수정 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CBT-01 | ✅ 확인 | `reachy-mini-daemon` (reachy_mini.daemon.app.main:main) picks the backend in this order: --mockup-sim → MockupSimBackend, otherwise --sim → MujocoBackend(scene, headless), otherwise RobotBackend on a serial port (default 'auto'). Other relevant flags: --scene (default 'empty'; 'empty'\|'minimal'), --headless, --no-media, -p/--serialport, --hardware-config-filepath, --kinematics-engine {Placo, NN, AnalyticalKinematics} (default AnalyticalKinematics), --check-collision (Placo only), --[no-]wake-up-on-start, --[no-]goto-sleep-on-stop, --[no-]autostart, --fastapi-host/--fastapi-port. | reachy_mini-1.11.0.dist-info/entry_points.txt:1-4<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/main.py:68-104<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/main.py:653-833<br>reachy_mini/daemon/daemon.py:841-903<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:188-191 | If both --sim and --mockup-sim are passed, MockupSimBackend is built (daemon.py:855-860), but the status reports simulation_enabled=True (daemon.py:365-366) and the media sim_mode is MUJOCO (main.py:288-294). --autostart is store_true with default True, so only --no-autostart changes anything. |
| CBT-02 | ✅ 확인 | FastAPI/uvicorn listens on 127.0.0.1:8000 by default. It binds 0.0.0.0 only with --wireless-version or an explicit --fastapi-host. REST routes are under /api, the SDK WebSocket is /ws/sdk, and a state stream with configurable rate is /api/state/ws/full. | reachy_mini/daemon/app/main.py:100-116<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/main.py:312-334<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/main.py:593-599<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/sdk_ws.py:8-14<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/state.py:145-196 | The wireless-only routers (cache, logs, update, wifi_config) are mounted at app level, without the /api prefix (main.py:325-331). |
| CBT-03 | ✅ 확인 | Daemon.start() runs Backend.wrapped_run() in its own thread, starts a 1 Hz DaemonStatus publisher thread and waits up to 2 s for backend.ready. By default it then enables the motors and runs wake_up(): a goto to the INIT pose (duration proportional to distance) followed by a 20° roll gesture. Daemon.stop() runs reset_to_sleep() by default: lift to init, move to the sleep pose, torque off. | reachy_mini/daemon/daemon.py:402-433<br>reachy_mini/daemon/daemon.py:471-484<br>reachy_mini/daemon/daemon.py:557-564<br>reachy_mini/daemon/daemon.py:829-839<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1284-1323<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1325-1372<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1386-1423 | wake_up() stands down with no motion when the motors are Enabled and the targets are already at INIT (abstract.py:1296-1299). goto_sleep_on_stop is forced to False when the state is STOPPING or ERROR (daemon.py:528-529). In MuJoCo the final torque-off is a no-op. |
| CBT-04 | ✅ 확인 | RobotBackend runs a Python loop at control_loop_frequency = 50 Hz with relative scheduling (sleep = period − took, via multiprocessing.Event.wait). On overrun it only logs at DEBUG and sleeps 1 ms; there is no deadline or catch-up. It reads from a compiled ReachyMiniPyControlLoop(read_position_loop_period = 20 ms, allowed_retries = 5, stats_pub_period = 1 s), and nothing in the Python code synchronizes the two loops. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:12<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:71-79<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:154-198 |  |
| CBT-05 | ✅ 확인 | Each RobotBackend tick does five things in order: (1) if torque is on, write the current joint goals (6 Stewart + body rotation + antennas); (2) read the cached positions and run FK for the present head pose; (3) step head tracking; (4) run IK if ik_required; (5) publish JointPositionsMsg + HeadPoseMsg (+ IMU). A Cartesian head target is therefore IK'd at tick k and reaches the motors at tick k+1 (+20 ms). Joint and antenna targets are written at the next tick. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:200-288 | The same tick also runs the 1 s stats update and the 1 Hz hardware-error poll (305-335). Steps 2-5 and these checks run only after the publishers are wired by WSServer.start (236-239), which happens before the backend thread starts (daemon.py:402-425). |
| CBT-06 | ✅ 확인 | ik_required is set by set_target_head_pose, a changed body yaw, speech offsets and tracking. | CBT-27 | 🟡 부분 수정 | The most faithful recipe for an external client without changing the SDK: (i) generate trajectories on the client and stream ReachyMini.set_target (one SetFullTargetCmd per tick) from a single executor thread on perf_counter deadlines, logging each sent target with perf_counter_ns/time_ns plus joints computed locally with AnalyticalKinematics; (ii) run a separate listen-only websocket client on /ws/sdk that parses joint_positions/head_pose with the SDK's protocol models and timestamps each message on arrival. On the robot, add /api/state/ws/full (daemon UTC timestamps) to separate Wi-Fi jitter. Three caveats apply. Local IK matches the daemon only with the same automatic_body_yaw flag (ReachyMini turns it on by default and the daemon may move body yaw: a yaw-170° pose gave body yaw 105°). In /api/state/ws/full, head_joints are read from the Rust cache at send time while head_pose is the last tick's FK, so the two fields are not the same sample, and its uncompensated sleep makes the real rate lower than requested. The SDK's own utils/rerun.py already polls /api/state/full at 50 Hz with the daemon timestamp, which is a precedent. |

The 'p50 20.01 ms, p95 ≤ 20.9 ms' figure (line 28) comes from the simulation key-figures section. For the robot run the report gives motion thread 50 Hz, p95 ≤ 20.7 ms, max 40 ms (line 496). The 5 s event windows show p50 20.0-20.01 ms and p95 20.43-21.62 ms, with one 320 ms stall while torque was off (line 4). |

**검증자가 추가한 발견**

| 내용 | 근거 |
|---|---|
| Windows asyncio timer slop affects the daemon's goto/play_move loop. loop.time() is time.monotonic (15.625 ms resolution), and _run_once marks every timer due within that resolution as ready. Two overlapping gotos started ≥20 ms apart on the uvicorn loop therefore busy-spin, alternating targets at about 6-7k writes/s. Cross-thread wakeups like WSServer._broadcast make a single 100 Hz goto tick irregularly at 99-145 Hz instead of the ~60 Hz seen on an idle loop. | Python 3.12 Lib/asyncio/base_events.py:421, 738-745, 1953-1974; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:904-925; reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:86-103. Measured with no-I/O stubs: wf1/verify_control_backends_telemetry/v_move_guard_offsets.out.json (offset 0-8 ms: 124-134 writes/s; offset 20/50/100 ms: 7312/6678/6263 writes/s) and v_asyncio_wakeups.out.json (alone 60.2 Hz; waker 40 Hz×2: 99 Hz; 50 Hz×3: 144.7 Hz, min Δt 0.02 ms). |
| On the robot, /api/state/full and /api/state/ws/full read head_joints directly from the Rust cache (get_last_position) at response time, while head_pose is the FK pose from the last Python tick. The two fields in one FullState can come from different serial reads. In MuJoCo, head_joints reads live qpos from the uvicorn thread while physics steps in another thread. | reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/state.py:111-121; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:480-506; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1093-1098; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:319-326 |
| The SDK already contains a REST telemetry precedent: utils/rerun.py polls /api/state/full at 50 Hz with with_head_joints=true and uses the daemon's UTC timestamp as the time axis. | reachy_mini/utils/rerun.py:186-241 |
| mean_control_loop_frequency is mean(1/dt) over about 1 s of time.time() tick-start stamps, which is biased upward compared with 1/mean(dt) when ticks jitter. max_control_loop_interval covers only that window. HW4 should compute its own rate from arrival timestamps. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:185-186, 305-323 |
| The 1 Hz hardware-error poll runs at least 9 synchronous raw reads (addr 70, plus addr 144 when a voltage error is flagged) inside the 50 Hz control tick. This is a likely cause of once-per-second max-interval spikes (HW3 saw 20.1-21.2 ms). Inferred from code, not measured. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:325-335, 685-735 |
| In MuJoCo the published head pose (site 'head' − 0.177 m) matches SDK FK of the same qpos within about 0.02 mm and 0.01°. Sim task-space metrics can therefore use HeadPoseMsg or FK(joints) interchangeably. Settled static error against the command after 3 s was ≤0.27° and ≤0.13 mm (≤0.004 rad per joint). | wf1/verify_control_backends_telemetry/v_site_vs_fk.py and v_site_vs_fk.out.json; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:239-243, 294-308 |
| Stewart joints are friction-dominated in sim because the 7 passive ball joints also have frictionloss 0.1 and damping 0.15. Stewart t90 is 0.24-0.70 s against about 0.05 s for yaw and antennas, and small joint moves (≈0.05 rad) can stall at 10-40 % residual after 1 s. | reachy_mini/descriptions/reachy_mini/mjcf/reachy_mini.xml:264-381 (class="chosen_actuator" on passive_1..7); wf1/verify_control_backends_telemetry/v_mujoco.out.json (passive_joints, step_pitch15, step_pitch15_back) |
| Commanded body yaw needs explicit logging. Both ReachyMini.goto_target and Backend.goto_target default to body_yaw=0.0, so a goto without body_yaw drives the body to 0 (REST /api/move/goto defaults to None and keeps it). ReachyMini also enables automatic_body_yaw by default, which lets the daemon IK redistribute yaw to the body: a yaw-170° pose gave body joint 1.83 rad. | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:113, 161, 680, 1096-1103; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:944-977; reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:39, 146; reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:80-97; wf1/verify_control_backends_telemetry/v_ik_unreachable.out.txt |
| Timestamp types are inconsistent. TaskRequest and TaskProgress use naive local datetime.now(), FullState uses timezone-aware UTC, recording records use client time.time(), and joint, pose and IMU messages carry no timestamp. Alignment code must normalize them, and over Wi-Fi it must also estimate the clock offset. | reachy_mini/io/ws_client.py:273; reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:192-197; reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/state.py:141; reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:660-663; reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:995-1013, 1100-1121 |
| Latent race in the daemon's play_move: t is recomputed after the loop check without clamping, and time_trajectory raises ValueError for t/duration > 1. If time.time() steps past the duration between the two calls, the goto ends with an error and the client raises 'Task failed'. This is rare. The client-side async_play_move clamps t to duration − 10 ms. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:904-913; reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:121-122; reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:1155; reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:179-198 |
| WSClient.get_status() returns at once only if a DaemonStatus has arrived since the previous call. Otherwise it blocks for the next 1 Hz status (timeout 5 s, then TimeoutError). The backend-type check and a 1 Hz stats sampler should run off the executor thread. | reachy_mini/io/ws_client.py:228-234; reachy_mini/daemon/daemon.py:829-839 |
| MujocoBackend imports GStreamerUDPCamera, which requires PyGObject 'gi' and GStreamer, at module import even with --headless. In this venv gi resolves from the gstreamer_python bundle and the import works. A Windows sim setup without that bundle would fail before the backend starts. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:28; reachy_mini/media/gstreamer_udp_camera.py:13-24; wf1/verify_control_backends_telemetry/v_imports.out.txt |

수치 재계산: 37개 중 36개 일치. 불일치:
- MuJoCo t90 yaw / antenna / Stewart 0.050 / 0.054 / 0.26-0.70 s: Yaw 0.050 and antenna 0.054 reproduced. Stewart: 0.24-0.70 s for only 4 of 6 joints; the 2 joints with \|Δq\| ≈ 0.05 rad never reach 90 % within 1 s (also null in the analyst's JSON).

Torque figures do appear, but none is documented as a datasheet spec: the unlabelled 0.52 N*m / 1.47 A pair, and the force ranges of the unused MJCF classes (xc330m288t +-0.8 N*m 'probably wrong', sts3215_345 +-3.23, sts3215_147 +-1.35). — Only the last sentence of the original was too absolute. |

Python has no recovery path. | reachy_mini/assets/config/hardware_config.yaml:63 (same value for all 9 motors)<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:122-129,325-335,685-735<br>reachy_mini/tools/setup_motor.py:3,256-271<br>scratch xl330_definition.txt (shutdown addr 63, hardware_error_status addr 70) | shutdown_error 52 sets bits 2, 4 and 5. Read with the backend's register-70 decode table, these are Overheating, Electrical Shock and Overload; that the shutdown register shares this bit layout is a Dynamixel convention not stated in the SDK. Bit 0 (Input Voltage) is excluded on purpose. The daemon polls register 70 on each motor once per second and only logs errors; they are not exposed in the backend status. Python has no targeted error-clear or motor-reboot. The only Python remedy is a full daemon restart (RestartDaemonCmd or POST /api/daemon/restart). The compiled controller contains a motor-reboot routine whose trigger is unknown. — Logs can be streamed from the robot via daemon/app/routers/logs.py:11-24 (journalctl WebSocket). |
