**검증자가 추가한 발견**

| 내용 | 근거 |
|---|---|
| Daemon moves are NOT serialized when started on the same event loop. _play_move_lock is a threading.RLock taken with acquire(blocking=False), and it re-enters for the owning thread. All SDK goto task requests and REST moves are asyncio tasks on uvicorn's loop. Running the SDK's own play_move offline on a stub, a second move was accepted (depth 2) and both trajectories interleaved target writes. A cross-thread refusal returns silently and the client's task still reports 'finished'. The HW4 Motion Executor must serialize moves itself. | abstract.py:282-286, 488-499, 844-853, 941-942; io/ws_server.py:174-200; probes/verify_limits_safety/v07_move_guard.txt |
| No idle reset or torque-off protects a crashed SDK client. The RobotAppLock is bypassed by SDK clients over LAN/WebSocket. ReachyMini.__exit__/__del__ only disconnect. HW3 found that when the PC program dies, torque stays on and the last pose is held. HW4 needs its own watchdog and an E-stop path, such as a separate REST connection. | daemon/robot_app_lock.py:3-6; abstract.py:3093-3106; reachy_mini.py:173-191; HW3 docs/PHASE4_5_REPORT.md:242 |
| Commands from the SDK WebSocket are fire-and-forget, because the server's send_response is a no-op. Ignored targets during a move, refused moves and command errors never reach the Python client. Worse, ReachyMini.enable_gravity_compensation() on a default (AnalyticalKinematics) robot daemon raises a RuntimeError that process_command does not catch (it catches only ValueError). That error escapes WSServer._recv_loop, and handle_client logs 'WS client error' and returns, ending that SDK session. | io/ws_server.py:116-171; daemon/app/routers/sdk_ws.py:8-14; abstract.py:1798-1807; robot/backend.py:632-636; reachy_mini.py:1088-1090; offline stub test probes/verify_limits_safety/v10_gravcomp_cmd.txt |
| enable_motors(ids=[...]) and disable_motors(ids=...) bypass the anti-snap pinning. SetTorqueCmd with ids goes to set_motor_torque_ids, which calls enable_torque_on_ids directly, with no target pin and no change to _torque_enabled. Only the global enable pins targets. | reachy_mini.py:1063-1086; abstract.py:1786-1793; robot/backend.py:646-667 vs 352-376 |
| The ReachyMini constructor always sends SetAutomaticBodyYawCmd. The signature default is True while the docstring says False, and the daemon applies the setting globally. Creating one client with automatic_body_yaw=False removes the 160/65 deg yaw clamps for every client of that daemon. | reachy_mini.py:113, 139, 161, 1096-1103; abstract.py:1159-1166, 1809-1811 |
| An offline Safety Checker must reproduce the daemon's body-yaw state. Daemon IK uses target_body_yaw. enable_motors pins it to the measured body yaw, and every goto_target resets it to 0.0 by default. HW3 found that PC IK with body_yaw 0 was 7 deg off the daemon's joints when the torso stood at 8.2 deg. | abstract.py:563-564, 586, 951; robot/backend.py:367; reachy_mini.py:680; HW3 reachy_s2s/motion/limits.py:146-149 |
| Raw serial pass-through. The daemon route /api/move/ws/raw/write forwards arbitrary bus packets to the servos, and ReachyMiniPyControlLoop exposes async_read_raw_bytes and async_write_raw_bytes. This bypasses every software limit. It is also the only SDK path to read Profile Velocity/Acceleration (112/108) and Velocity Limit (44) on hardware, which needs explicit approval. | daemon/app/routers/move.py:257-274; robot/backend.py:737-750; introspection probes/verify_limits_safety/v02_servo_defs.txt |
| Starting the real-robot daemon is itself a motor command. By default it reflashes the EEPROM if the config differs and runs wake_up, which includes a 20 deg roll in 0.2 s (about 2.4 rad/s Stewart peak). Under the 'no motor motion before approval' rule, any hardware daemon launch needs approval and --no-wake-up-on-start. | daemon.py:471-480, 892-893; daemon/app/main.py:93, 746-757; abstract.py:1314-1320; v06_builtin_speeds.txt |
| MuJoCo cannot simulate torque-off, E-stop droop or gravity compensation. Its get_motor_control_mode always returns Enabled, and set_motor_control_mode and set_motor_torque_ids are no-ops. The mockup only stores the mode. Stop and E-stop behaviour can be validated only on hardware. | daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:337-347; daemon/backend/mockup_sim/backend.py:155-168 |
| Sim timing on this Windows PC differs from the nominal 50/100 Hz. Python's monotonic and asyncio clock resolution is 15.625 ms (GetTickCount64), and time.sleep(0.002) actually takes about 2.5 ms. Running the SDK's play_move offline gave about 62 Hz instead of 100 Hz. A replica of the MuJoCo pacing loop ran at a real-time factor of about 0.8, i.e. about 40 Hz control ticks. These are offline estimates. Sim-side rates and lag must be measured, with perf_counter timestamps. | abstract.py:866-925; mujoco/backend.py:90-91, 227-288; probes/verify_limits_safety/v07b_loop_timing.txt, v08_clock_check.txt, v09_mujoco_checks.txt |
| The SDK documents that play_move never evaluates t = duration, so the last written target is one velocity-eased tick short of the endpoint. The client's async_play_move clamps t to duration - 0.01. This matters for final-error and peak-error baselines in Part 3. | abstract.py:1233-1236, 904-925; reachy_mini.py:1155 |
| With the NN engine (onnxruntime is installed), the daemon's only IK failure check never fires. NNKinematics applies no limits and never returns NaN, and the check is NaN-only. | kinematics/nn_kinematics.py:25-45; abstract.py:206-209, 586-588 |
| Collision coverage is limited. The compiled MJCF has only 9 colliding geoms: the floor, 7 body_down meshes and 1 head (xl_330) mesh. The analytical IK has no collision model, and Placo collision checking is unavailable. Head-body contact is observable only in MuJoCo, and rod or arm contact nowhere. | probes/verify_limits_safety/v11_mj_collision_geoms.txt; abstract.py:188-191; mjcf/config.json:8-11 |

수치 재계산: 33개 중 30개 일치. 불일치:
- roll x pitch symmetric box with >=10 deg margin: Fine search gives about 27.4 deg (>=10) and 30.2 deg (>=5). The 26 came from a 2-deg grid.
- TABLE spot-check: software clamps inventory, row 'second concurrent move: ignored + warning': True only across threads; same-loop moves both run. The inventory also lacks the speech-tapper clips: loudness [0,1] and envelope [0,1].
- TABLE spot-check: feasibility table pitch-/x- columns: These are sweep-end artifacts. Pitch-up >=5 deg margin reaches -65 deg and stays in-limit to -70. x- first NaN is at -63 mm, not 'none to -60'.

**검증자가 추가한 발견**

| 내용 | 근거 |
|---|---|
| StopMoveCmd stops only the current segment of a multi-segment daemon sequence (wake_up, goto_sleep, reset_to_sleep). Each goto in the sequence is a new top-level play_move, which clears _stop_move_requested, so the sequence continues, and goto_sleep still ends by disabling torque. An FSM cannot abort these sequences from the WS SDK. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:848-853,1284-1323,1325-1372; v_daemon_loop.json e2_stop_during_wake_up: 26 target writes more than 30 ms after a StopMoveCmd; wake_up returned normally |
| CancelMoveCmd cannot interrupt an uploaded move's initial goto or its audio-lead sleep, because the nested goto_target -> play_move is given no cancel_token. Cancelling 0.1 s into a 0.6 s initial goto ended the run only after 515 ms. StopMoveCmd does interrupt it, because it uses the global flag. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:856-865,873-875,966-977; v_daemon_loop.json h_upload.cancel_during_initial_goto (cancelled, cancel_to_end_ms=514.9) |
| A CancelMoveCmd processed before the play task has installed its token is silently lost, and the move plays to 'finished'. The reason: PlayUploadedMoveCmd only schedules a task, and _handle_cancel_move returns when no token matches. Comments claiming the lock closes this race are inaccurate. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:297-299,2128-2131,2427-2430,2860-2862; reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:853-855; v_daemon_loop.json h_upload.cancel_sent_immediately_after_play = finished |
| A Python WS client cannot observe whether a daemon move is running. DaemonStatus has no is_move_running field. StateSnapshot has one, but only for WebRTC push or get_state, and get_state's reply is discarded on WS. /api/move/running lists only REST-created tasks. The executor/FSM must track motion ownership itself. | reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:125-150,164-184; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1577-1609; reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:166-172; reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:27,131-134 |
| Automatic body yaw is on by default: ReachyMini(automatic_body_yaw=True) sends SetAutomaticBodyYawCmd, and AnalyticalKinematics defaults to True. IK then uses the compiled inverse_kinematics_safe (max_relative_yaw 65 deg, max_body_yaw 160 deg), which can change joint 0 relative to the commanded body_yaw. For exact logging of motor targets, the client must read target_head_joints via REST /api/state/full?with_target_head_joints=true, or disable automatic body yaw. | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:113,161,1096-1103; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1159-1166,1809-1811; reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:33,80-95; reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/state.py:118-121 |
| Uploaded and dataset moves never command their last sample exactly. duration is t_last - t0, and the loop exits before t_last, so the final command interpolates between the last two frames with alpha < 1. The 'started' duration_s for N uniform samples at 100 Hz is (N-1)/100 (0.99 s for 100 samples). To land exactly on the end pose, pad the move with a duplicate hold frame at least one play period long. | reachy_mini/motion/recorded_move.py:96-103,122-123; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:904-913; v_daemon_loop.json h_upload.play_events duration_s=0.99 |
| REST POST /api/move/stop cancels the asyncio task. play_move then skips stop_sound, because 'interrupted' is set only by the stop flag or cancel token, so a recorded move's sidecar sound keeps playing. This matters for sound-synced Term Project moves. | reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:111-128; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:902-942; v_daemon_loop.json j_task_cancel_sound media_calls ['play'] (no 'stop') |
| The MuJoCo backend is unlikely to reach 50 Hz in real time on this PC (inference, not measured on a running daemon). Each physics step sleeps max(0, 0.002 - took) with no deadline tracking, and time.sleep(0.002) takes about 2.5 ms here. Emulating the loop with zero physics cost gave a 24.9 ms control tick: about 40 Hz, with simulated time at 0.8x wall time or less. Before using MuJoCo for lag or RMSE, measure the live JointPositionsMsg rate; a 50 Hz stream could outpace the sim control loop. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:90-91,227-288; clocks.json time_sleep_2ms_median_ms=2.511; v_misc.json mujoco_pacing_emulated (24.86 ms median, 39.7 Hz, sim/real <= 0.795) |
| The SDK's client and daemon loops compute t from time.time(). On this PC (Python 3.12) that is GetSystemTimeAsFileTime, with nominal 15.625 ms resolution and ~1 ms steps currently observed, so evaluation times in the client loop and in a Windows-hosted MuJoCo daemon are quantized. The HW4 executor and telemetry should use perf_counter_ns (QueryPerformanceCounter, 100 ns). | clocks.json (time: GetSystemTimeAsFileTime, 0.015625; perf_counter: QueryPerformanceCounter 1e-07); reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:1149-1165; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:879-923 |
| Building RecordedMove only checks description, time and set_target_data, so frame contents are not validated. A frame missing 'head' or 'antennas' passes upload_move_finish and fails mid-playback with KeyError, which becomes an error broadcast. Finished but unplayed uploads are never evicted, because the cap and TTL cover only in-progress slots. | reachy_mini/motion/recorded_move.py:85-98,141-146; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:2143-2172,2532,2809; v_numbers.json frame_without_head; v_daemon_loop.json finished_unplayed_moves_kept=6 |
| The REST route /api/move/play/recorded-move-dataset builds RecordedMoves synchronously on the daemon event loop: about 90-130 ms from the local cache on this PC, longer on the CM4, plus a network download if the dataset is not cached. This stalls WS command processing and all daemon play_move loops. PlayRecordedMoveCmd uses asyncio.to_thread instead. | reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:177-192; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:2714-2721; v_misc.json recordedmoves_emotions_load_ms_offline [89.0,128.0,117.3] |
| For strategy (2), the client knows what it sent, but not exactly what reached the motors. The daemon samples only the latest target at its control tick, with unknown phase, so intermediate targets are overwritten. It also composes tracking and speech offsets before IK, and may adjust body yaw through IK-safe. Commanded-vs-measured analysis should log the sent stream and, for validation, the daemon's target_head_joints. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:544-594,596-667; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:200-261; reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/state.py:114-129 |

수치 재계산: 38개 중 34개 일치. 불일치:
- cancel_move to client loop return 1.46 ms: A single sample. Re-measured 0.4-16.5 ms over 24 trials (median ~8 ms): uniform within one asyncio wakeup.
- final shortfall 20 deg 1 s goto: 0.072 / 0.0027 / 0.00015 / -0.004 deg: Single random draws. Six trials each gave linear 0.0001-0.253, ease 0.00004-0.011, minjerk 0-0.0012, cartoon -0.032 to -0.0001 deg. Report these as a range or bound.
- upload payload 3 s @100 Hz: 66,899 B JSON (6 chunks) / 12,676 chars gzip+b64 (2 chunks): Depends on content. A full-precision 6-DoF sample gave 117,234 B JSON (10 chunks of 12 KiB) and 57,364 chars gzip+b64 (5 chunks). Either way it is far below the limits.
- upload payload 10 s @100 Hz: 223,906 B JSON (19 chunks) / 45,888 chars gzip+b64: Depends on content. The sample gave 392,421 B (32 chunks) and 188,980 chars (16 chunks).

| C01 | ✅ 확인 | The Wireless robot has 9 Dynamixel servos per the official hardware datasheet: body 1x custom XC330-M288-PG (an XC330-M288-T with plastic gear), antennas 2x XL330-M077-T, Stewart platform 6x XL330-M288-T. The installed config and the motor-controller repo agree: IDs 10, 11-16 and 17-18, an XC330 base, and XL330s elsewhere. | https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/platforms/reachy_mini/hardware — 'Base: 1x Custom Dynamixel XC330-M288-PG (XC330-M288-T with plastic gear)'<br>https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/platforms/reachy_mini/hardware — 'Stewart Platform: 6x Dynamixel XL330-M288-T'<br>https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/platforms/reachy_mini/hardware — 'Antennas: 2x Dynamixel XL330-M077-T'<br>https://github.com/pollen-robotics/reachy-mini-motor-controller — 'the stewart platform (6x XL330), the base rotation (XC330) and the antennas (XL330)'<br>reachy_mini/assets/config/hardware_config.yaml:56-164 — body_rotation id 10, stewart_1..6 ids 11-16, right_antenna 17, left_antenna 18 | Old comments in reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:221,229,397,447 still say the body and antennas are 'feetech motors'. They are leftovers from earlier hardware and change no behaviour; ignore them when citing motor models. |
| C02 | 🟡 부분 수정 | Wireless compute and power: a Raspberry Pi CM4104016 (WiFi, 4 GB RAM, 16 GB flash) sits on a CM4 board fed 6.8-7.6 V by the power board, with a Dynamixel TTL connection and a LiFePO4 2000 mAh 6.4 V battery. The FAQ requires the 7V-5A adapter to power the motors. | https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/platforms/reachy_mini/hardware — 'Raspberry 4 Compute Module - CM4104016 (Wifi, 4GB RAM, 16GB flash)'<br>https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/platforms/reachy_mini/hardware — 'Power input voltage: 6.8 - 7.6V'<br>https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/platforms/reachy_mini/hardware — 'LiFePO4 Battery, 2000mAh, 6,4V, 12,8Wh'<br>https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/troubleshooting — 'Ensure the 7V-5A power supply is plugged in.'<br>https://github.com/pollen-robotics/reachy_mini — 'Runs onboard (RPi CM4), autonomous, includes IMU.' | Wireless: Raspberry Pi CM4104016 (WiFi, 4 GB RAM, 16 GB flash) on a CM4 controller board that gets 6.8-7.6 V from the power board and carries the Dynamixel TTL connection. The LiFePO4 2000 mAh 6.4 V battery sits on the power board. The FAQ's first-startup checklist asks for the 7V-5A supply because USB alone cannot power the motors. That note is generic and Lite-oriented: the Wireless also runs from its battery, so the adapter is not its only motor power source. |
| C03 | ✅ 확인 | Datasheet ratings (ROBOTIS). XL330-M288-T: 288.4:1, 4096 pulse/rev, no-load 103 rpm at 5.0 V (123 at 6.0 V), stall 0.52 N·m at 5.0 V (0.60 at 6.0 V). XL330-M077-T: 77.5:1, 383 rpm at 5 V (456 at 6 V), 0.215 N·m (0.228). XC330-M288-T (metal gear): 288.35:1, 81 rpm (97), 0.93 N·m (1.10). All are rated 3.7-6.0 V, recommended 5.0 V. | https://emanual.robotis.com/docs/en/dxl/x/xl330-m288/ — spec rows: 'Gear Ratio 288.4 : 1'; no-load 76/103/123 rev/min at 3.7/5.0/6.0 V<br>https://emanual.robotis.com/docs/en/dxl/x/xl330-m288/ — 'Input Voltage 3.7 ~ 6.0 [V] (Recommended : 5.0 [V])'<br>https://emanual.robotis.com/docs/en/dxl/x/xl330-m077/ — spec rows: 'Gear Ratio 77.5 : 1'; no-load 278/383/456 rev/min at 3.7/5.0/6.0 V<br>https://emanual.robotis.com/docs/en/dxl/x/xc330-m288/ — 'Full Metal Gear, 2 Bearing'; gear ratio 288.35 : 1; no-load 59/81/97 rev/min | The robot powers the motors above 7.0 V (see C04 and missed findings), outside the rated range. Treat the 6.0 V speeds as approximate reference points, not proven ceilings. |
| C04 | ✅ 확인 | The motors run above their rated voltage on purpose. The FAQ says the higher voltage is intentional. XL330 Max Voltage Limit defaults to 7.0 V. The 1.11.0 config sets shutdown_error=52, which clears the Input-Voltage shutdown bit (XL330 default is 53), and setup_motor.py says it removes that error. The actual motor-bus voltage is not documented. | https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/troubleshooting — 'We are using a higher voltage on Reachy Mini, it's on purpose'<br>https://emanual.robotis.com/docs/en/dxl/x/xl330-m288/ — Max Voltage Limit(32) initial value 70 (unit 0.1 V); Shutdown(63) initial 53<br>reachy_mini/assets/config/hardware_config.yaml:56-164 — shutdown_error: 52 on all 9 motors<br>reachy_mini/tools/setup_motor.py:3 — docstring: 'removing the input voltage error'<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pollen-robotics/reachy-mini-motor-controller/main/Changelog.md — v1.5.2 (paraphrase): stopped rebooting on input voltage errors | No number is published for the bus voltage, but the evidence narrows it. The error fires only above the 7.0 V limit. The SDK drops the error only while Present Input Voltage (address 144) is at most 7.8 V (robot/backend.py:701-725). The CM4 board that carries the Dynamixel link is fed 6.8-7.6 V. So about 7-7.6 V is likely. The XC330-M288 factory Shutdown is already 52 (e-manual), so the 52 setting only matters for the XL330s. |
| C05 | ✅ 확인 | Bus and protocol: 1,000,000 bps. Motor controller v1.5.6 writes Goal Position (address 116) with a Protocol-2 sync write and sync-reads Present Position (address 132) for all 9 IDs. It writes no Profile Velocity/Acceleration and no position limits. rustypot converts radians to ticks as 4096*(π+rad)/(2π), truncated to an integer, so 0 rad = 2048. | https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pollen-robotics/reachy-mini-motor-controller/v1.5.6/src/controller.rs — 'serialport::new(serialport, 1_000_000)'<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pollen-robotics/reachy-mini-motor-controller/v1.5.6/src/controller.rs — 'xl330::sync_write_goal_position('<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pollen-robotics/rustypot/v1.4.2/src/servo/dynamixel/xl330.rs — '(4096.0 * (PI + value) / (2.0 * PI)) as i32'<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pollen-robotics/reachy-mini-motor-controller/main/Cargo.toml — 'version = "1.5.6"' and 'rustypot = "1.4.2"'<br>reachy_mini/assets/config/hardware_config.yaml:3-4 — baudrate: 1000000<br>reachy_mini_motor_controller/reachy_mini_motor_controller.cp312-win_amd64.pyd — compiled; offline introspection lists only goal-position, torque, operating-mode, goal-current, PID and raw read/write methods | RobotBackend sends three sync-write packets per tick: Stewart (6 IDs), body (1) and antennas (2) (robot/backend.py:206-228). set_all_goal_positions, which writes all 9 at once, is never called. The Rust loop writes each command as it arrives (tokio select on rx.recv()) and sync-reads on its own 20 ms timer. The Python rustypot 1.13.1 in the venv is used only by tools/setup_motor.py. |
| C06 | 🟡 부분 수정 | The real-robot control loop runs at 50 Hz. Each tick writes Stewart, body and antenna goals (torque on, position mode), reads all positions, runs IK if the target changed, and publishes joint state. The FAQ says to expect about 50 Hz (period about 19.99 ms, read about 1.94 ms, write about 0.19 ms). | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:71-79 — control_loop_frequency = 50.0; read_position_loop_period = 1/50 s<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:200-261 — _update(): set_stewart_platform_position / set_body_rotation / set_antennas_positions, then read, then IK<br>https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/troubleshooting — 'You should see values around 50Hz (~20ms period)'<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pollen-robotics/reachy-mini-motor-controller/main/src/control_loop.rs — 'let mut interval = time::interval(read_position_loop_period);' | The Python control loop runs at a nominal 50 Hz. Each tick, with torque on and in position mode, it does the following. (1) It queues goal positions for the Stewart, body and antenna motors. (2) It fetches the Rust controller's cached last read; the bus sync-read runs on the Rust side's own 20 ms timer, not in step with the Python tick. (3) It runs FK. (4) It runs IK whenever ik_required is set. Because nothing clears that flag after a successful IK, IK in practice runs every tick once a pose or body-yaw target exists. (5) It publishes state. The write comes before IK in _update, so a new IK result reaches the motors on the next tick, at least 20 ms later. The FAQ reference statistics are quoted correctly. |
| C07 | ✅ 확인 | goto_target path in 1.11.0: the daemon builds a GotoMove that starts from the present (measured) head pose, body yaw and antennas. play_move evaluates it at 100 Hz (time_trajectory followed by linear_pose_interpolation with yaw_as_scalar=True) and calls set_target_head_pose. IK runs in the 50 Hz control loop. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:944-977 — GotoMove(start_head_pose=self.get_present_head_pose(), ...)<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:826-923 — play_move(play_frequency=100.0) loop sets targets<br>reachy_mini/motion/goto.py:51-74 — evaluate(): time_trajectory + linear_pose_interpolation(..., yaw_as_scalar=True)<br>https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/API/reachymini — 'Go to a target head pose and/or antennas position using task space interpolation' | The loop condition is t < duration, and nothing evaluates t = T afterwards (abstract.py:904-925; the code comment at 1233-1236 says so). The last target written is therefore one play tick short of the endpoint (see missed findings). In MuJoCo the 'present' pose is the simulator's site pose, not FK. |
| C08 | ✅ 확인 | There is no firmware motion profile. Neither SDK 1.11.0 nor motor controller v1.5.6 writes Profile Velocity or Acceleration, whose RAM default is 0, meaning no profile and infinite velocity. Each 50 Hz goal is therefore a step that the Dynamixel position PID tracks. | https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pollen-robotics/reachy-mini-motor-controller/v1.5.6/src/controller.rs — no profile_velocity/profile_acceleration writes (checked at tag)<br>https://emanual.robotis.com/docs/en/dxl/x/xl330-m288/ — Profile Velocity(112): ''0' represents an infinite velocity'<br>reachy_mini (grep, all *.py) — no 'profile_velocity'/'profile_acceleration' writes; only Placo velocity limits (reachy_mini/kinematics/placo_kinematics.py:219-228) | This is confirmed at source level only. A raw-packet passthrough exists (daemon/app/routers/move.py:271 to robot/backend.py:737-750), but nothing calls it by default. Velocity Limit(44) bounds Goal Velocity only (e-manual), so it does not cap speed in position mode. |
| C09 | ✅ 확인 | At startup RobotBackend writes PID gains from hardware_config.yaml, which is the default CLI path. Stewart motors get P=300, I=0, D=0; body and antennas get P=200, I=0, D=0, written at address 80 in D,I,P order. Factory defaults are higher: XL330-M288 P400/D0, XL330-M077 P400/D400, XC330-M288 P1100/D500. | reachy_mini/assets/config/hardware_config.yaml:56-164 — pid [200,0,0] body/antennas, [300,0,0] stewart_1..6<br>reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:82-91 — async_write_pid_gains(motor_id, p, i, d)<br>reachy_mini/daemon/app/main.py:682-695 — default --hardware-config-filepath = assets/config/hardware_config.yaml<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pollen-robotics/reachy-mini-motor-controller/main/src/control_loop.rs — 'const DIP_GAIN_ADDR: u8 = 80;'<br>https://emanual.robotis.com/docs/en/dxl/x/xc330-m288/ — Position P Gain initial 1,100; Position D Gain initial 500<br>https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/troubleshooting — 'reduce P to 180 on motors 10 (foot), 17 and 18 (antennas)' | The Args dataclass default is None (main.py:79). A daemon started programmatically without the yaml path therefore writes no gains. The writes are queued asynchronously, and the SDK never reads them back; motor-controller #47 reports that writes have no retry. The 1100/500 body figures belong to XC330-M288-T; the custom plastic-gear motor's defaults are not documented. |
| C10 | ✅ 확인 | Stewart motors have EEPROM position limits from hardware_config.yaml: s1 -48..+80°, s2 -80..+70°, s3 -48..+80°, s4 -80..+48°, s5 -70..+80°, s6 -80..+48°. Body and antennas are 0-4095, i.e. unlimited. By default the daemon checks these settings and re-flashes them at startup. | C11 | ✅ 확인 | The MuJoCo model uses the same Stewart ranges and yaw_body ±160°. Its position actuators inherit the joint ranges, so control inputs are clamped per joint; antennas are unlimited. The loop sleeps max(0, 0.002 minus step time) per physics step with no catch-up (mujoco/backend.py:227-288), while play_move uses the wall clock. Nobody has measured whether wall-clock control stays at 50 Hz on this Windows host. |
| C12 | ✅ 확인 | The MuJoCo actuators are idealized. chosen_actuator inherits 'perfect_actuator' (kp=10, kv=0.1, forcerange ±20 N·m), which is not an identified XL330 model. The simulation docs still call the physics realistic. | reachy_mini/descriptions/reachy_mini/mjcf/joints_properties.xml:3-28 — perfect_actuator kp=10 forcerange -20..20; xc330m288t 'probably wrong, would need to re-identify'<br>reachy_mini/descriptions/reachy_mini/mjcf/reachy_mini.xml:20-24 — <default class="chosen_actuator"/> nested in perfect_actuator<br>https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/platforms/simulation/get_started — 'provides a realistic physics environment to prototype, test, and debug' | The official sources also disagree on simulation fidelity: troubleshooting v1.11.0 calls MuJoCo support work in progress, while the simulation guide says the simulator behaves like a real Lite. |

The 1.11.0 code uses max_body_yaw=160° and max_relative_yaw=65° in the analytical IK, ±160° in the MJCF, and ±2.8 rad in Placo. | https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/SDK/core-concept — 'Body Yaw \| [-160°, +160°]'; 'Max 65° difference between Head and Body Yaw'<br>https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/troubleshooting — 'Body Yaw: [-180°, 180°].'<br>https://github.com/pollen-robotics/reachy_mini/blob/main/AGENTS.md — table row: Body yaw [-160, +160] degrees<br>reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:85-90 — max_relative_yaw=np.deg2rad(65), max_body_yaw=np.deg2rad(160)<br>reachy_mini/kinematics/placo_kinematics.py:227-228 — set_joint_limits('yaw_body', -2.8, 2.8) | AGENTS.md was read from main, which is newer than 1.11.0. | C16 | ✅ 확인 | Despite the docs, 1.11.0 does not clamp pitch or roll. The Rust inverse_kinematics_safe (v1.0.3) clamps only body yaw and relative yaw. Offline IK solved pitch 45°/50° and roll 45°/50° exactly (FK round-trip error ≤0.02°) and returned NaN from about +51° pitch and ±52° roll. This is open upstream issue #1449, reported on 1.11.0. | https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/SDK/core-concept — 'The SDK will automatically clamp values to the closest valid position.'<br>https://github.com/pollen-robotics/reachy_mini/issues/1449 — 'Documented ±40° head pitch/roll clamp is not implemented'<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pollen-robotics/reachy_mini_rust_kinematics/v1.0.3/src/lib.rs — 'max_relative_yaw: Option<f64>, max_body_yaw: Option<f64>) -> Vec<f64>' (no pitch/roll limit)<br>reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:80-95 — only yaw limits passed<br>scratch probe_fk_continuation_out.json — pitch 50° -> FK 50.01°; first IK NaN pitch+ 51.0°, roll ±52.0° | The IK solves 50° pitch and 50° roll, but both exceed the yaml motor limits: pitch-down is OK only up to 48.0° (s3 binds) and roll up to 47.7°. The derivation has no collision model and treats each axis alone. |
| C19 | ✅ 확인 | automatic_body_yaw defaults to True in ReachyMini, although the docstring says 'Default is False'. With it on, a body-yaw target is clamped to within ±65° of the head's target yaw and to ±160°: head 0° with body 90° gives body 65°, and head 90° alone gives body 25°. With it off, body yaw passes through unclamped and a large relative yaw gives NaN. | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:113 — automatic_body_yaw: bool = True<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:139 — docstring '...Default is False.'<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:161 and reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1159-1166 — flag pushed to daemon kinematics<br>scratch probe_body_yaw_out.txt — auto=True head_yaw=0 body_req=90 -> body_joint=65.00<br>https://github.com/pollen-robotics/reachy_mini/blob/main/AGENTS.md — paraphrase: body yaw change alone pivots the body under the head | The flag lives in the daemon's single kinematics object, and every ReachyMini() constructor sends it again. There are four methods: linear, minjerk, ease_in_out, cartoon. | https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/API/reachymini — 'duration: float = 0.5, method: InterpolationTechnique = <InterpolationTechnique.MIN_JERK...>'<br>https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/API/reachymini — 'Use None to keep the current yaw.'<br>https://huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/SDK/python-sdk — 'Interpolation methods: linear, minjerk (default), ease_in_out, cartoon.'<br>reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:672-722 — defaults, duration check, wait_for_task_completion(timeout=duration + 1.0)<br>reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:108-115 — InterpolationTechnique enum | The client's None-check also covers body_yaw, which defaults to 0.0 (reachy_mini.py:695-698). Yaw is interpolated as a signed scalar Euler angle, the pitch/roll residual by rotation-vector SLERP, and translation linearly. For methods with s in [0, 1] the yaw path stays between its endpoints and never takes the back route. Cartoon (s from -0.1 to 1.1) can extrapolate past ±180° when both endpoint yaws are near ±180°; for example, +170° to -170° overshoots to -204°. |

With x forward and z up, positive pitch means nose down. The daemon-side Backend.goto_sleep ends with torque off. The SDK's ReachyMini.goto_sleep() runs on the client (two gotos and a 2 s sleep) and leaves torque on. Neither wake_up enables torque. |
| C28 | 🟡 부분 수정 | The analytical FK is stateful. Each call does one pseudo-inverse update plus a line search, seeded by the previous solution. Far from the seed it can converge to another branch: offline, the joints for z=+10 mm returned FK z=-102 mm. Small continuation steps gave ≤0.03° error. | https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pollen-robotics/reachy_mini_rust_kinematics/v1.0.3/src/lib.rs — forward_kinematics: single pseudo_inverse(1e-6) step + backtracking, seeded by t_world_platform<br>reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:99-142 — fk(no_iterations=3) loops the Rust step; 'Head is not upright, recomputing FK'<br>scratch probe_limits_out.json vs probe_fk_continuation_out.json — jump-seeded FK wrong; continuation FK pitch 50.01° for 50° | The analytical FK is stateful: each call takes one Newton step with a line search, seeded by the last solution. A far seed can converge to another branch. From the default seed this was reproduced for yaw +60°, giving z ≈ -103 mm, and more iterations do not fix it. The analyst's z=+10 mm to -102 mm case depends on a previously moved seed. Small continuation steps give about 0° error. To reconstruct poses offline, process samples in order from a nearby seed or use the pose the daemon reports. Iterations alone are not enough. |

**검증자가 추가한 발견**

| 내용 | 근거 |
|---|---|
| A goto never commands its exact endpoint. play_move loops while t < T and never evaluates t = T, so the last target written is evaluate(t) with t in [T-10 ms, T), and it stays in place until the next command. Worst-case shortfall: linear 3.3%, 2.0% and 1.0% of amplitude at T = 0.3, 0.5 and 1.0 s; minjerk at most 0.035%; ease_in_out at most 0.22%. Cartoon ends 0.05-0.5% past the target. Steady-state error and RMSE should be measured against the last sample actually commanded. Alternatively, the executor can send one set_target to the exact endpoint after the goto returns. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:904-925 (loop with no evaluation after it); abstract.py:1233-1236 (code comment that the last written target falls one eased tick short); verify_official_docs_hw/v_interp_out.txt |
| In 1.11.0, set_target, set_head_joints, set_body_yaw, set_antennas and set_full_target are dropped while any goto or play_move runs. The daemon only logs a warning, and SDK WebSocket commands are fire-and-forget, so the client is never told. Two gotos are both accepted and interleave. The executor must serialize moves and must never stream set_target during a goto, for example after a client-side TimeoutError. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1661-1699 (_maybe_ignore); reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:166-172 (no response to commands) and 174-200 (one asyncio task per goto); abstract.py:283, 488-493 (re-entrant RLock); reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:216-225 (REST answers 'ignored') |
| Latency chain on the real robot. A pose sample goes through play_move at 100 Hz, then the latest sample is picked up by the 50 Hz Python tick. IK runs at the end of that tick, but the write happens at the start of the next one, so command-to-motor latency is at least 20 ms. Measured joints are the Rust thread's cached read, taken on its own 20 ms timer that is not synchronised with the Python loop. Once per second the loop also polls hardware-error registers with raw reads, which can cause periodic jitter; check max_control_loop_interval. Lag analysis must budget for 20-40 ms of pipeline delay before attributing anything to the motors. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:200-261 (write, then cached read, FK, IK), 325-335 and 685-735 (1 Hz error polling); abstract.py:544-594, 609, 626, 638 (IK flag never cleared); control_loop.rs v1.5.6 (tokio::select! on rx.recv() and interval.tick()); motor-controller .pyd docstring 'last successfully read' (verify_official_docs_hw/introspect_out.txt) |
| The SDK's state stream has no timestamps, and the client keeps only the last message, so a laptop-side log cannot separate network delay from motor lag. The daemon has a better telemetry source. GET /api/state/full and WS /api/state/ws/full?frequency=... return, on request, measured head joints, commanded joint targets after IK (target_head_joints), target body yaw, target antennas, the head pose and a daemon-side UTC timestamp. Use it to log command against measured on the robot's clock. Note that requesting target_head_pose before any target is set asserts. | reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:995-1007 (JointPositionsMsg and HeadPoseMsg have no time field); reachy_mini/io/ws_client.py:69-70, 186-190, 215-226; reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/state.py:88-142 and 145-196; reachy_mini/daemon/app/main.py:312-333 (/api prefix); troubleshooting v1.11.0 shows the /api/state/ws/full URL |
| The daemon offsets the commanded pose before IK in two cases: speech-wobbler offsets and daemon-side face tracking. Only the daemon's own scripted sleep and reset sequences switch these off. SDK gotos do not, so experiments must call disable_wobbling() and stop_head_tracking() first, or commanded and requested poses will differ. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:566-583 (tracking blend and speech offsets applied before IK), 721-758 (tracking sets the IK flag every tick), 1374-1384 (_quiesce_aim_sources, used only by goto_sleep and reset_to_sleep); the API v1.11.0 page lists disable_wobbling and stop_head_tracking |
| With torque off, the daemon accepts every motion command, reports success and keeps its 50 Hz loop running, so a disabled robot looks healthy. On the robot backend nothing is written to the motors when torque is off. Before each run, the executor and Safety Checker should read motor_control_mode from the daemon status. | troubleshooting v1.11.0 (entry on a robot that does not move and reports no error, with the get_status().backend_status.motor_control_mode check); reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:203 |
| MuJoCo specifics that affect HW4 telemetry. The loop is not locked to real time: it sleeps the leftover of each 2 ms step, with no catch-up, against wall-clock play_move timing. The 'present' head pose comes from the MuJoCo site (z minus 0.177 m), not from the analytical FK. Antenna signs are flipped internally. Motor mode always reads Enabled. MujocoBackendStatus has no control_loop_stats, so loop rate in sim must be measured from timestamps. None of this has been measured yet. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:227-288, 243, 294-308, 260, 335, 337-343; reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:77-81 |
| The motor bus most likely runs at about 7-7.6 V, above the 6.0 V datasheet rating. The Input-Voltage error bit fires only above the 7.0 V Max Voltage Limit. The SDK suppresses that error only up to a measured 7.8 V, reading Present Input Voltage at address 144. The CM4 board that carries the Dynamixel link is fed 6.8-7.6 V. Real no-load speed and torque may therefore exceed the 6.0 V figures. Logging the input voltage (address 144) would tie the motor-spec comparison to the measured operating point. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:701-725; e-manual xl330-m288 (Shutdown bit 0 definition, Max Voltage Limit 70); hardware v1.11.0 CM4 Controller Board section |
| Official sources disagree on collisions and simulation fidelity. The troubleshooting page says the head may touch the body during some official motions and calls MuJoCo support a work in progress. The simulation guide says the simulator behaves like a real Lite. AGENTS.md (main) says gentle body collisions are safe and that the SDK clamps automatically, which the code contradicts. There is no collision model on Windows (no Placo), so the HW4 Safety Checker must add margins and cannot rely on the SDK for clearance. | troubleshooting v1.11.0 (Hardware & Limits and Simulation entries); huggingface.co/docs/reachy_mini/v1.11.0/platforms/simulation/get_started (section 5); github.com/pollen-robotics/reachy_mini/blob/main/AGENTS.md (safety line) |
| Trajectory execution options in 1.11.0. Client-side play_move streams set_target_head_pose, set_target_body_yaw and set_target_antenna_joint_positions at 100 Hz from the laptop. Its timing is exposed to Wi-Fi jitter, it caps t at duration minus 10 ms, and it is not recorded. The Python SDK cannot upload a move for daemon-side playback. Chaining goto_target runs on the robot, but each goto restarts from the measured pose at zero velocity. That causes back-steps or tremor when segments are short, the issue #1130 pattern. | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:1115-1171; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:944-977; grep of reachy_mini.py finds no upload or play_uploaded client method; github.com/pollen-robotics/reachy_mini/issues/1130 |
| Open item: on the Wireless, the robot backend reads a BMI088 IMU every control tick and publishes it at 50 Hz (accelerometer, gyroscope, quaternion). If the IMU is in the head, its gyroscope would give an encoder-independent check of head motion for lag analysis. No checked source documents where the IMU is mounted, so this is unconfirmed. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:131-142, 280-285, 535-574; reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:1010-1013; python-sdk v1.11.0 IMU section (Wireless only) |

수치 재계산: 39개 중 38개 일치. 불일치:
- Sequence step 3: set_target_antenna_joint_positions does not set ik_required (abstract.py:657-667). set_target_body_yaw ignores unchanged values (620-624). The final t = T sample is never evaluated.

The (raw−2048)·360/4096 mapping is marked INFERRED, and the YAML comments (−48/+80°, +70/−80° ...) agree with it point by point. The ±1024 offsets are not hidden in the compiled code: they are YAML values that the readable SDK tool tools/setup_motor.py writes into the XL330 EEPROM as homing offsets, together with the raw position limits. Only the raw↔rad conversion inside the compiled controller is unreadable. The SDK MuJoCo model's Stewart joint ranges (−48/+80, −80/+70, −48/+80, −80/+48, −70/+80, −80/+48 deg) match the inferred mapping exactly, so confidence in the mapping can be raised. |

IK(SLEEP_HEAD_POSE) peaks at 47.93°. HW3's '69.1° < 72.3°' argument was therefore invalid, though not because of a different convention. Reproduced with body yaw 0: neutral ±35.90°, sleep−neutral change 83.83°, sleep margin 22.03°. — The SDK uses this constant only to initialise the MuJoCo and mockup simulators (mujoco/backend.py:188-193; mockup_sim/backend.py:55-63). |

| H8 | ✅ 확인 | MotionController is an 8-mode safety state machine (TORQUE_OFF, STARTING, HOLD, ACTIVE, STOPPED, SHUTTING_DOWN, FAULT, ESTOP). A single sender thread runs at rate_hz (5–100, default 50) on an absolute-clock schedule; it skips missed ticks and caps the limiter dt at 1.5 periods. tick() never raises; failures go to FAULT. A health thread checks a 1 s stall watchdog, reads the REST motor mode every 2 s and the daemon loop stats every 10 s. | hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/controller.py:1-32<br>hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/controller.py:73-98<br>hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/controller.py:392-453<br>hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/controller.py:455-501<br>hw3/docs/PHASE4_5_REPORT.md:60-99 | The stall watchdog only reports; it does not change the mode. emergency_torque_off sends from the caller's thread, outside the single sender thread. |

Ramp commands skip the box and use guard.check with no scaling before the limiter. In both chains a refusal sends nothing, and refusals lasting more than 0.5 s trigger Motion Stop. |
| H10 | 🟡 부분 수정 | Startup reads the REST motor mode, torso yaw and present pose, and refuses unless the pose is within 8°/8 mm of sleep or neutral (or force is set). It then calls enable_motors, confirms 'enabled' over REST after 0.3 s, and runs a PC-side minimum-jerk ramp of ≥3 s computed from the caps. Shutdown ramps to neutral (≥1 s), then to sleep (≥3 s), settles 0.5 s, checks the pose, calls disable_motors and confirms 'disabled' over REST. Any failure → FAULT with torque left ON. It waits for operator confirmation unless the pose is within 8°/8 mm of sleep or neutral (or force is set). It then calls enable_motors, confirms 'enabled' over REST after 0.3 s and runs a PC-side minimum-jerk ramp of ≥3 s sized from the caps. Shutdown runs neutral (≥1 s) → sleep (≥3 s) → 0.5 s settle → pose check → disable_motors → REST 'disabled' check. Any failure goes to FAULT, which never turns the torque off by itself: it stays ON after a shutdown pose or read failure, and its state is unknown after a failed disable. R1 passed: 6.56 s ramp, 1.0 s + 6.32 s shutdown, final TORQUE_OFF. |

The daemon ignores SetFullTargetCmd (what set_target sends) and the other target commands while a move runs. The daemon-side Backend.goto_sleep (REST /api/move/play/goto_sleep, WebSocket GotoSleepCmd) plays a sound and disables the motors at the end. The SDK client ReachyMini.goto_sleep is a different, client-side goto_target sequence: it plays go_sleep.wav through the client media manager (a no-op with no_media) and does not disable the motors. |

| The SDK MuJoCo model's Stewart joint ranges are exactly −48/+80, −80/+70, −48/+80, −80/+48, −70/+80, −80/+48 deg, and yaw_body is ±160°. The actuators inherit these ranges. This matches HW3's INFERRED raw→deg mapping within 0.04° (raw quantisation), giving readable SDK corroboration for the joint-limit analysis. The SDK's own SLEEP_HEAD_JOINT_POSITIONS violates 4 of these 6 ranges. | reachy_mini/descriptions/reachy_mini/mjcf/reachy_mini.xml:74, 253, 275, 297, 319, 341, 363 (ranges in rad), 625-631 (inheritrange="1"); hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/limits.py:26-34; verify_sdk_numbers.out.json |
| In MuJoCo the motor mode is always 'enabled' and torque on/off calls do nothing. HW3's start/stop sequence would therefore always FAULT at shutdown ('torque off not confirmed'). The HW4 executor needs backend capability flags or a sim-aware sequence before reusing the controller with MuJoCo. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:337-347; hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/controller.py:709-712, 764-768 |
| The MuJoCo model drives every joint with an idealised 'perfect_actuator' (kp 10, kv 0.1, ±20 N·m) instead of the servo-like xc330m288t class (kp 2.54, ±0.8, itself commented 'probably wrong'). MuJoCo tracking error will not reproduce the real robot's +4.1° gravity sag and ~9° lifting lag (HW3 R1). Real-dynamics priors exist only in the HW3 R1/R2 logs. | reachy_mini/descriptions/reachy_mini/mjcf/reachy_mini.xml:20-31, 253-363 (class chosen_actuator), 625-633; hw3/docs/PHASE4_5_REPORT.md:497-501 |
| Two timing biases affect lag analysis. First, a new set_target takes 1–2 daemon periods (20–40 ms) plus Wi-Fi time to reach the servos: each loop writes the previous iteration's IK targets first, then reads positions and runs IK. Second, the SDK state cache stores the last received message with no timestamp, and HW3's present.csv and PresentSampler stamp samples with the PC read time, so sample age is unknown. HW4 measured-vs-commanded lag estimates include both biases. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:203-209, 241-257; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:544-583, 596-609; reachy_mini/io/ws_client.py:183-190, 213-226; hw3/reachy_s2s/apps/motion_preview.py:156-168; hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/sinks.py:112-116 |
| The SDK's trace-based angle functions under-read small rotation errors on non-orthonormal matrices. HW3 found the daemon's head matrices and SLEEP_HEAD_POSE have determinant ≈1.0005. Tested offline with delta_angle_between_mat_rot: true 0.5–1.5° reads 0.000°, 2° reads 0.82°, 3° reads 2.38°. HW4 RMSE and peak-error code must orthonormalise first (HW3's SVD rotation_distance_deg) or work in Euler angles, and must not use distance_between_poses for tracking errors. | reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:156-205; hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/pose.py:111-124; hw3/docs/PHASE4_5_REPORT.md:502-507; probe_trace_angle.py |
| On the real robot the sleep↔neutral ramp starts from the measured rest pose (pitch 28.0°, z −45.6 mm, torso yaw 8.17°), not the SDK constant. Its minimum motor margin is 16.65° (Stewart 5) and its maximum joint change 89.21°. The analyst's 22.03°/83.83° holds only for the SDK constant at body yaw 0. I reproduced 16.653° (joint 5) and 89.206° with IK at body yaw 8.174°. | hw3/results/sessions/20261017_063145_motion_preview_live/preview.json (commands.ramps); hw3/docs/PHASE4_5_REPORT.md:384, 462, 490; verify_sdk_numbers.out.json (r1_rest_pose_margin_deg) |
| HW3's conversation joint guard (≤12° Stewart change from neutral, ≥10° margin, torso ≤3°, body yaw 0) allows these single-axis extents: roll ±16.6°, pitch +16.7/−18.9°, yaw ±32.2°, x +19.9/−16.8 mm, y ±16.7 mm, z +8.5/−9.3 mm. HW4 expressive amplitudes beyond these need a newly justified envelope and cannot reuse the 12° guard unchanged. | hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/limits.py:153, 194-210; hw3/config/default.toml:216-218; probe_envelope.py |
| Every ReachyMini() connection enables automatic body yaw, so the IK turns the torso whenever head yaw exceeds 65° relative to the body. Tested offline: head yaw 66° gives body yaw 1°, 70° gives 5°, 100° gives 35°. HW3's 3° torso check would therefore reject head yaw above ~68° even in ramp mode. Large HW4 'look-around' motions must budget for this (Rust solver, internals not readable). | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:113, 161, 1096-1103; reachy_mini/kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:80-90; hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/limits.py:151, 167, 202-209; probe_bodyyaw.py |
| HW3 never turns off daemon head tracking, and its Motor-ON disable_wobbling is a no-op unless HW3 itself enabled wobbling. The design promised to disable both. The daemon adds wobble offsets and the tracking aim to every target before IK. The HW4 executor should explicitly call disable_wobbling() and stop_head_tracking() (both exist in SDK 1.11.0) at startup. | hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/controller.py:151, 566, 692-694; a grep of hw3 for head_tracking finds nothing; hw3/docs/PHASE4_5_DESIGN.md:175; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:566-583, 1333, 1374-1380; reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:269-291 |
| Antenna 'perk' saturates at vertical. The SDK neutral antennas are (−0.1745, +0.1745) rad, commented as a 10° offset from vertical, and sleep folds to (−3.05, +3.05). Any perk s > 0.1745 rad carries each antenna past vertical to the opposite side; HW3's LISTENING perk 0.20 and PROCESSING ant_r +0.30 already do. HW4 antenna amplitudes should be defined relative to vertical, and this geometry should be checked in MuJoCo (inference, medium confidence). | reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:67-68; hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/pose.py:70-72, 132-133; hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/presets.py:59, 64 |
| The robot's clock is not NTP-synchronised (HW3 saw its logs dated 10-05). HW4 telemetry must align commands and measurements on PC clocks (perf_counter_ns) only, never on daemon-side timestamps. | hw3/docs/IMPLEMENTATION_NOTES.md:12-13 |
| Running the SDK MuJoCo daemon on this Windows PC: it paces 2 ms physics steps (50 Hz control by decimation) using time.time(), which resolves only to 15.625 ms on the HW4 Python 3.12. The real-time factor and control period of the sim should be measured before using it for timing or lag results. | reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:90-91, 228, 285-286; hw3/docs/IMPLEMENTATION_NOTES.md:45; HW4 venv time.get_clock_info (time and monotonic resolution 0.015625) |
| The HW3 report's header still says no motor command was ever sent to the robot (software-stage text), which contradicts its own R1–R3 sections. Citations of HW3 robot results should point to §13–15, not the header. | hw3/docs/PHASE4_5_REPORT.md:6 vs 484-567 |

수치 재계산: 35개 중 31개 일치. 불일치:
- Ramp tracking lag (R1): Doc-quoted. My zero-order-hold recompute gives 9.10° on the lifting ramp. On the lowering ramp the head did not lag: 4.1° sag at the start, −0.5…+3.4° during the motion, 1.7° at the end.
- Daemon control loop (R1): write_dt is ~0.04–0.41 ms (events.jsonl:17 = 0.04), not 0.07–0.41. Other values correct: 49.523–49.684 Hz, max 20.14–21.21 ms, read_dt 1.86–1.88, nb_error 0.
- SetFullTargetCmd size / build time / bandwidth at 50 Hz: Doc PC estimate. With SDK 1.11.0 pydantic JSON: 143 B for an identity head, 206 B for neutral in the torso-yawed world frame, ~300 B for a typical pose, i.e. ≈10–15 KiB/s at 50 Hz.
- table: reuse matrix — platform_timing 'robot-verified yes (all apps)': 14 apps enable it via prepare_runtime and timing_check toggles it itself; 7 offline/report tools do not.

## 비평 단계 (교차 차원)

**종합 평가**: Coverage of the Phase-1 SDK topics is strong and mostly source-grounded: the end-to-end goto_target path, the closed forms of the four interpolations, the IK/FK structure, the 50 Hz and 100 Hz command flow, and joint limits from yaml, URDF and MJCF. The main corrections the report must carry:  - **Commanded targets.** The FullState target fields are dropped. move_playback_apis C28 and the 'missed' items of move_playback_apis and official_docs_hw are wrong, which the critic confirmed offline. So no daemon API in 1.11.0 exposes commanded targets. - **Windows timing.** Windows numbers depend on the regime (idle loop vs cross-thread wakeups, variable time.time granularity) and are all emulated. - **Body-yaw clamp.** The automatic body-yaw clamp uses psi = atan2(-R01, R00), confirmed by a critic probe. - **Motor identity.** The per-joint motor models come from the official datasheet (official_docs_hw), not from the SDK comments.  Also use the verifier corrections rather than the analyst text, notably H6 (same joint convention), K17 (45/101 held ticks), CBT-14 (no fixed dead band) and C24 (Windows numbers apply only to Windows-hosted loops).  The highest-value open work: 1. Enforce localhost-only, sim-verified connections in code before anything connects. 2. Design and validate the client-side telemetry and IK reconstruction. --mockup-sim allows an exact check. 3. Compare the IK-derived required joint speeds with the XL330/XC330 specs at the real operating voltage. Short CARTOON moves reach 43-66% of the 5 V no-load speed. 4. Measure the timing of a live MuJoCo or mockup daemon, which the user permits.  Phase-0 validation criteria and a single risk/hardware-pending register still have to be written, and evidence files must be copied out of the scratchpad.  Critic artifacts (read-only probes; nothing outside the scratchpad was modified): - probes/completeness_critic/crit_probe.py and crit_probe.out.txt (FullState drop, psi rule, clock granularity) - probes/completeness_critic/crit_speed_probe.py and crit_speed_probe.out.json (required Stewart speeds for 4 methods x 3 durations x 4 moves)

| 모순 | 관련 차원 | 해결 방법 |
|---|---|---|
| Commanded targets via /api/state/full. goto_pipeline G25 (verifier: refuted) and control_backends_telemetry CBT-20 say FullState silently drops every target_* field. move_playback_apis C28 was nevertheless marked 'confirmed' with the opposite claim. Two 'missed' items build on it: move_playback_apis ('read target_head_joints via REST /api/state/full?with_target_head_joints=true') and official_docs_hw ('returns ... commanded joint targets after IK'). Critic check: FullState (reachy_mini/daemon/app/models.py:149-160) declares no target fields and no 'extra' setting (pydantic default ignore). state.py:114-129 adds the target_* keys and state.py:142 validates them away. An offline FullState.model_validate given all four target keys kept none (completeness_critic/crit_probe.out.txt). The only other move-state API, StateSnapshot.is_move_running (io/protocol.py:145, WebRTC only), carries no targets. | goto_pipeline, control_backends_telemetry, move_playback_apis, official_docs_hw | Treat the G25 refutation as authoritative: in 1.11.0 no daemon API returns commanded or post-IK targets. Strike C28 and both 'missed' recommendations. Telemetry must log commands client-side and reconstruct joint targets with local AnalyticalKinematics, validated in --mockup-sim (see gaps). Also note that with_target_head_pose=True raises AssertionError while no target exists (state.py:116). |
| Usefulness of daemon timestamps. control_backends_telemetry (summary, CBT-27) and official_docs_hw (missed) recommend /api/state/ws/full daemon UTC timestamps for separating Wi-Fi jitter. hw3_reuse (missed) says the robot clock is not NTP-synced and daemon timestamps must never be used. Source facts: the timestamp is datetime.now(timezone.utc) taken when the response is built (state.py:141). head_joints is read from the Rust cache at that same moment, while head_pose is the last tick's FK (CBT missed). The WS loop sleeps 1/frequency after each send (state.py:196). reachy_hw3 docs/IMPLEMENTATION_NOTES.md:12 records that the robot has no internet and its clock was wrong (logs dated 10-05). | control_backends_telemetry, official_docs_hw, hw3_reuse | Both are partly right. Use PC perf_counter_ns arrival stamps as the only absolute time base. Daemon timestamps can serve only as a relative clock, after fitting offset and drift per session; with no internet an NTP step is unlikely, but check monotonicity. Use them only to bound transport jitter, never as the sample time. Take measured data from the per-tick /ws/sdk stream. |
| Predicted play_move rate on Windows. interpolation_math (C24 and summary: 'both loops would run at roughly 62-67 Hz'), limits_safety (missed: about 62 Hz) and move_playback_apis (C3: 65.6 Hz) give the idle-event-loop rate. goto_pipeline (G26 correction: irregular ticks from under 1 ms to 24 ms, mean 11.8 ms) and control_backends_telemetry (CBT-17 correction: 99-145 Hz) show that cross-thread wakeups make the loop faster and irregular. In a live daemon, every control tick issues two loop.call_soon_threadsafe wakeups whenever at least one /ws/sdk client is connected (io/ws_server.py:59-63, 86-103). The goto caller is such a client, so the idle figure does not describe a live Windows MuJoCo daemon. official_docs_hw's 'worst-case' endpoint shortfall (linear 3.3% at D=0.3 s) also silently assumes P=10 ms. | interpolation_math, limits_safety, move_playback_apis, goto_pipeline, control_backends_telemetry, official_docs_hw | Quote both regimes only as offline stub results. Measure play_move ticks in a live sim daemon with an instrumented harness (gap 4) before stating any rate. Express every endpoint-shortfall bound as 1 - s(1 - P/D) with the measured P distribution, separately for the Windows sim and for the robot's Linux daemon (unmeasured). |
| time.time() granularity on this PC. goto_pipeline G30 (confirmed), hw3_reuse H20 (correction) and hw3_reuse (missed) say time.time ticks every 15.6 ms. control_backends_telemetry CBT-17 and move_playback_apis (missed) measured about 1 ms steps. Critic re-measurement now: clock_info nominal 0.015625 s; observed time.time steps median 5.09 ms (min 0.30, max 16.1); time.monotonic 15-16 ms; time.sleep(0.002) median 2.51 ms (crit_probe.out.txt). Granularity follows the system-wide timer interrupt rate, which other processes change. This matters because play_move computes t, and its loop test, from time.time() (daemon/backend/abstract.py:903-920), and the MuJoCo and mockup pacing also use it (mujoco/backend.py:285-286, mockup_sim/backend.py:136-137). | goto_pipeline, hw3_reuse, control_backends_telemetry, move_playback_apis | Describe time.time granularity as variable (about 1-16 ms, nominal 15.625 ms), and record time.get_clock_info plus a measured granularity in every session's metadata. Attribute the MuJoCo real-time shortfall mainly to time.sleep(0.002) taking about 2.5 ms, not to time.time resolution. HW4 logs use perf_counter_ns only. |
| Direction of sim-vs-real tracking bias. limits_safety (summary) says simulated tracking 'will look better than the real robot'. goto_pipeline (missed) calls the lower-bound assumption unverified. control_backends_telemetry (CBT-14 correction, missed) found the MuJoCo Stewart joints friction-dominated: t90 0.24-0.70 s, and 0.05 rad moves stall at 10-40% residual after 1 s. The cause is that the passive ball joints inherit damping 0.15 and frictionloss 0.1 (reachy_mini.xml:20-24 and 264-352, class chosen_actuator). goto_pipeline's '0.01 rad dead band' was measured on yaw_body, while CBT reports Stewart residuals creeping below 0.004 rad after 3 s. HW3 real data (hw3_reuse H18) show 4.1 deg pitch sag and about 9 deg lag on the rising ramp. | limits_safety, goto_pipeline, control_backends_telemetry, hw3_reuse | Make no claim about the direction of the bias. Report MuJoCo dynamics per joint group (yaw_body and antennas fast; Stewart slow and friction-limited), always with the settle time. Quantify sim vs real by replaying the HW3 R1 command stream in headless MuJoCo (gap 7). |
| Motor identity and spec provenance. limits_safety (L17 and an open question) says the exact motor per joint is never stated, and Python comments call the body and antenna motors Feetech (robot/backend.py:221, 229, 397, 447). L7 and L18 call the 0.52 N·m / 1.47 A pair unlabelled. official_docs_hw C01/C03 (confirmed) give the datasheet assignment: body custom XC330-M288-PG, antennas XL330-M077-T, Stewart XL330-M288-T. 0.52 N·m is the XL330-M288-T stall torque at 5.0 V, the same constant used at robot/backend.py:587 (from_Nm_to_mA = 1.47/0.52*1000). | limits_safety, official_docs_hw, kinematics_ik | Adopt the official hardware datasheet (cite URL and access date) as the per-joint motor assignment, and list the 'feetech' comments as legacy source inconsistencies. Verify on the same ROBOTIS e-manual page that 1.47 A is the XL330-M288-T stall current at 5 V. Treat the PG body motor as unspecified; if XC330-M288-T is used as a proxy, state it as an assumption. |
| Confidence in the compiled motor-controller behaviour. limits_safety (L1, L16 correction), kinematics_ik (K21) and hw3_reuse (H5) treat the raw-to-rad conversion and the controller's register writes as unreadable compiled code. official_docs_hw C05/C08 (confirmed) state them from upstream sources: rustypot converts rad to ticks as 4096*(pi+rad)/(2*pi), truncated, and motor-controller v1.5.6 writes no Profile Velocity or Acceleration. However, official_docs_hw itself notes that control_loop.rs was read on main, not the v1.5.6 tag. control_backends_telemetry CBT-07 found rustypot 1.4.2 embedded in the binary, while the separately installed Python rustypot is 1.13.1. | limits_safety, kinematics_ik, hw3_reuse, official_docs_hw, control_backends_telemetry | Re-read controller.rs, control_loop.rs and rustypot's dynamixel/xl330 conversion at the exact tags (reachy-mini-motor-controller v1.5.6, rustypot 1.4.2). Confirm the installed wheel versions with importlib.metadata. Then label these facts 'upstream source at matching tag, binary not decompiled'; otherwise keep 'inferred'. Live register values (108/112/44, PID at 80-84) still need a read-only hardware check after approval. |
| Wake-up at daemon start. limits_safety (missed, L28) and control_backends_telemetry (CBT-03) say starting the robot daemon runs wake_up by default (wake_up_on_start=True, daemon/app/main.py:93, 747-757). kinematics_ik (missed) says the shipped Wireless launcher disables it. Critic confirmed: daemon/app/services/wireless/launcher.sh:18 runs 'python -u -m reachy_mini.daemon.app.main --wireless-version --no-wake-up-on-start'. | limits_safety, control_backends_telemetry, kinematics_ik | Both are true, for different launch paths (CLI default vs the systemd launcher). Report that the robot's own daemon, if started by this 1.11.0 unit, does not wake up at boot. Any client-side enable_motors or torque-on is still a motor command that needs approval. Confirm the robot's actual launch flags once it is online. |
| Which yaw the automatic-body-yaw clamp uses. goto_pipeline (G21), limits_safety (L8), official_docs_hw (C19), move_playback_apis (C8) and hw3_reuse (missed) state the clamp in terms of the commanded head yaw: \|yaw - body\| <= 65 deg. kinematics_ik's verifier (K12/K13) found it uses psi = atan2(-R01, R00). The critic probe matches K12 exactly: roll 30 / yaw 100 gives body 36.508 deg (not 35), pitch 30 / yaw 100 gives 33.682, and roll 10 / yaw 80 gives 14.849 (crit_probe.out.txt). | kinematics_ik, goto_pipeline, limits_safety, official_docs_hw, move_playback_apis, hw3_reuse | State the rule with K12's psi and cite the probe evidence. The Safety Checker and the joint-target reconstruction must call the compiled AnalyticalKinematics.ik, as HW3 JointGuard does, never a re-implemented 65 deg rule. |
| Daemon-side upload path. official_docs_hw (missed) says 'the Python SDK cannot upload a move for daemon-side playback'. move_playback_apis C15-C18 show that the UploadMove*/PlayUploadedMoveCmd protocol models exist and are type-valid. There is no ReachyMini wrapper, and the Python WSClient silently drops their start/finish broadcasts. | official_docs_hw, move_playback_apis | Phrase it as: 'no ReachyMini wrapper; sendable as raw typed commands, but completion is unobservable from the Python client and untested against a live daemon'. Keep it off the HW4 critical path. |
| HW3 streaming rate. goto_pipeline G30 and move_playback_apis C30 describe HW3 as streaming set_target at 50 Hz. hw3_reuse (H12 correction, H24) shows that HW3 ticks at 50 Hz but sends only on change (deadband plus a 1 s keepalive): about 44 sends/s on the R1 ramp, about 1/s at rest, and 541 sends in 1715 ticks. | goto_pipeline, move_playback_apis, hw3_reuse | When reusing HW3's MotionController for HW4 experiments, remove the deadband and log every tick. When using HW3 R1 data as a commanded reference, rebuild it with a zero-order hold over the send times. |

The other shows Stewart joint velocities via AnalyticalKinematics.ik (reuse crit_speed_probe.py). Toolchain check by the critic: the HW4 .venv has matplotlib and PIL, but scipy and pandas are NOT installed. Installs are forbidden, so the analysis code must use numpy only. |
