## 해결 단계

| 항목 | 해결 | 결과 | 근거 | 남은 불확실성 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| resolve_0 — Commanded targets via /api/state/full: G25 (refutation) + CBT-20 ("FullState drops every target_* field") vs move_playback_apis C28 ("confirmed": read target_head_joints via REST) and the two dependent 'missed' items (move_playback_apis REST target read; official_docs_hw "returns ... commanded joint targets after IK") | 예 | G25 and CBT-20 are right. Strike C28 and both 'missed' recommendations.  In reachy_mini 1.11.0, neither GET /api/state/full nor WS /api/state/ws/full ever returns a target_* field. get_full_state reads all four backend target attributes (state.py:114-129). It then returns FullState.model_validate(result) (state.py:142). FullState (models.py:149-160) declares no target field and has no model_config, so pydantic's default extra='ignore' drops them silently. FastAPI's response_model=FullState would drop them a second time (fastapi/routing.py:322-345).  This was tested offline by calling the real route coroutines with a stub backend: - All four target attributes were read, and none survived the model dump, FastAPI serialize_response, or the WS frames. - Values of the wrong type under the target keys are discarded without any error. - The generated OpenAPI lists the four with_target_* query flags, but the FullState response schema has no target property. This contradiction in the published API contract explains the C28 and docs misreading.  Wording for the report: no daemon API exposes the daemon's own commanded targets, either the task-space pose or the post-IK joints. Only three things come close, and none is daemon telemetry: - (a) start/stop_recording sends back the client's own set_target() records unchanged. They use the client clock, are pre-IK, and cover set_target only (goto ticks are never recorded). They include commands the daemon ignored while a move was running, and they record body_yaw=None as 0.0. - (b) In --mockup-sim, the present head_joints equal the post-IK targets one or two 50 Hz ticks late. This makes it a usable validation oracle. - (c) The real-robot raw serial passthrough /api/move/ws/raw/write. It runs in a compiled extension, is unverified and unsafe, and is out of scope.  Separately, with_target_head_pose=True raises AssertionError at state.py:116 whenever backend.target_head_pose is None: - Over REST this probably becomes HTTP 500 (inferred, not run). - On the WS stream every frame is skipped, with a single warning whose message text is empty.  The suggested telemetry design holds, with conditions. Log commands on the client and rebuild the joints with a local AnalyticalKinematics(). Its output is bit-for-bit identical to the daemon's IK step only if all of these hold: - the engine is AnalyticalKinematics (default; visible at /api/kinematics/info); - automatic_body_yaw is never toggled (no API reports it); - face tracking is off and speech-wobble offsets are zero; - the last valid joints are held when IK fails.  For goto_target, the per-tick poses exist only inside the daemon: they start from the present pose and use daemon time. The client must re-evaluate GotoMove using an estimated start pose and t0, so client-streamed set_target is the only way to get exact command logs.  Side finding: REST POST /api/move/goto accepts an interpolation field but never uses it, so it always runs MIN_JERK. | FullState declares only control_mode, head_pose, head_joints, body_yaw, antennas_position, timestamp, passive_joints, doa and imu, with no model_config: reachy_mini/daemon/app/models.py:149-160. Probe B: model_config={} and fields_with_target=[] (pydantic 2.14.1, fastapi 0.143.2). Output in probes/resolve_0/resolve_probe.out.txt.<br>get_full_state exposes four with_target_* flags (reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/state.py:88-104). It writes target_head_pose, target_head_joints, target_body_yaw and target_antennas_position into the dict (state.py:114-129), then returns FullState.model_validate(result) (state.py:141-142).<br>Probe C ran the real get_full_state coroutine with all target flags on. The stub logged reads of all four target attributes, but the returned FullState had model_extra=None and no target_* key, so the drop happens at state.py:142.<br>Second filter: the route's response_model is FullState (return annotation at state.py:104; probe C prints route_response_model='FullState'). fastapi/routing.py:322-345 re-validates and serializes through that field. In probe C, even a raw dict containing the target keys serialized to the same 9 keys.<br>The drop is silent and happens before type checks. Probe D: FullState.model_validate with object(), str, list and dict values under the four target keys succeeded and kept none of them.<br>The OpenAPI contract contradicts itself. Probe E: GET /state/full lists with_target_head_pose, with_target_head_joints, with_target_body_yaw and with_target_antenna_positions as query params, but the 200 response ($ref FullState) has no target property.<br>head_joints in the response are present values, not targets: state.py:118-119 calls backend.get_present_head_joint_positions(). Probe C confirmed the returned head_joints equal the stub's present joints and differ from its IK target.<br>WS /api/state/ws/full calls the same get_full_state and sends model_dump_json() (state.py:145-185), so it drops targets the same way. Probe G (fake websocket object): 3 frames, none with a target key.<br>AssertionError: state.py:114-117 asserts target_head_pose is not None (line 116), and probe F reproduced AssertionError (empty message) there. target_head_pose starts as None (reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:222-224). It is set by set_target_head_pose (abstract.py:596-609), e.g. the wake_up gotos (abstract.py:1284-1320, skipped if already awake at the init pose, 1296-1299), or by the robot's enable_motors pin (daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:365-366).<br>REST error mapping is inferred, not run: main.py has only an asyncio loop exception handler (reachy_mini/daemon/app/main.py:574-588) and no FastAPI handler. An unhandled AssertionError should therefore reach Starlette's generic 500 response (starlette/middleware/errors.py:258-259).<br>On the WS stream the AssertionError is caught by the generic except with a de-duplicated warning (state.py:189-195). Probe G: 0 frames in 0.35 s at 20 Hz and a single 'Skipping full-state frame: ' warning with empty text, so the stream goes silent.<br>No other REST response carries motion targets. Probe E2 scanned OpenAPI for 81 operations across 14 routers: the only 2xx field with 'target' in its name is face_target in GET /daemon/status (face tracking). The only target fields are in the POST /move/set_target request body (FullBodyTarget, models.py:116-122). The update and wifi_config routers failed to import (missing semver and nmcli), but grep finds 'target' there only as Thread(target=...).<br>The kinematics router has no IK endpoint. /api/kinematics/info returns only the engine name and the collision flag; /urdf and /stl serve files (reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/kinematics.py:29-57).<br>Move router outputs contain no targets: /move/running returns UUIDs (move.py:131-134); /move/ws/updates sends move_started/completed/failed/cancelled plus uuid (move.py:80-101); POST /move/set_target returns status strings and is ignored while a move runs (move.py:216-233).<br>WebRTC/SDK messages carry present state only. StateSnapshot (reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:125-150; docstring at 126 says 'Present-state snapshot sent to WebRTC clients') is built from get_present_* getters (abstract.py:1577-1609). JointPositionsMsg and HeadPoseMsg (protocol.py:995-1007) are filled with present values in all three backends (mockup_sim/backend.py:116-131, mujoco/backend.py:262-277, robot/backend.py:241-273). Probe H: no 'target' field in any AnyServerMsg member except face_target.<br>StateSnapshot.is_move_running (protocol.py:145) reaches clients via WebRTC (GetStateCmd reply at abstract.py:1813-1814; PoseFrame at abstract.py:1619-1635 and 3019). On the LAN SDK WebSocket, command replies are discarded by a no-op send (reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:166-171).<br>The recording API (the strongest candidate counterexample) only sends back what the client recorded. Client set_target() builds the record with client time.time() and sends AppendRecordCmd only while recording (reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:650-670, 1045-1061). The daemon's only append_record caller is the AppendRecordCmd handler (abstract.py:2051-2053), and stop_recording publishes the buffer unchanged (abstract.py:1045-1077). Probe I: the published RecordedDataMsg.data equals the client-supplied records exactly.<br>The recording misses and misstates commands. goto_target() sends a GotoTaskRequest without recording anything (reachy_mini.py:705-722), and the daemon's per-tick play_move targets (abstract.py:904-925) are never appended. set_target() still records when the daemon ignores it during a running move (abstract.py:1661-1669, 1691-1699). It records body_yaw=0.0 when body_yaw is None (reachy_mini.py:662), while the daemon leaves body yaw unchanged (abstract.py:1695-1696).<br>Raw passthrough: /api/move/ws/raw/write forwards arbitrary bytes to the motor bus (move.py:257-274; robot/backend.py:737-749, which delegates to the compiled motor controller). Sim backends raise NotImplementedError (abstract.py:1441-1453). It is not a state API and is unsafe; do not use it without explicit approval.<br>Two in-repo uses confirm the pattern. The only other in-process reader of targets is internal antenna-touch logic (reachy_mini/daemon/app/startup_app.py:248-252), not exposed. The SDK's own rerun logger requests /api/state/full with every with_target_* flag set to 'false' and logs only present head_joints and antennas (reachy_mini/utils/rerun.py:186-200).<br>Rebuilding joints client-side is exact under conditions. Probe J called the unbound Backend.update_target_head_joints_from_ik (abstract.py:544-594) with tracking off and zero offsets, and compared it to a local AnalyticalKinematics().ik. Across 5 poses, including a yaw-80 deg clamp case (body yaw comes out at 15 deg), the results were identical bit for bit (max diff 0.0). IK also does not depend on call history (forward order, reverse order and a fresh instance give identical results).<br>Conditions for an exact rebuild: the daemon blends the face-tracking aim (abstract.py:566-569) and speech-wobble offsets (abstract.py:571-583) into the pose before IK. Probe J: a 5 deg yaw offset changed the joints by up to 0.087 rad, and automatic_body_yaw=False changed the yaw-80 case by 0.262 rad. The engine is selectable (abstract.py:200-217; default AnalyticalKinematics at main.py:87; CLI flag at main.py:798-802). automatic_body_yaw can be toggled (abstract.py:1159-1166; protocol.py:333-336; default True at kinematics/analytical_kinematics.py:33-39), but no GET reports its state.<br>When IK fails (ValueError at abstract.py:587-588), every backend catches it and keeps the previous joint targets (mockup_sim/backend.py:108-114, mujoco/backend.py:247-255, robot/backend.py:253-261). A client-side rebuild must hold the last valid solution.<br>goto_target poses exist only inside the daemon. Daemon goto_target starts from the present head pose, body yaw and antennas (abstract.py:966-977) and evaluates GotoMove (reachy_mini/motion/goto.py:51-74) on daemon time.time() inside play_move (abstract.py:904-925). A client-side log of a goto therefore holds only the request.<br>Mockup-sim can serve as a validation oracle. Each 50 Hz tick copies target_head_joint_positions into the present state before recomputing IK (reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mockup_sim/backend.py:71, 89-99, 107-114, 143-147). So in --mockup-sim, present head_joints equal the post-IK targets one or two ticks late. This was not run because starting the daemon is forbidden in this phase.<br>Side finding: REST POST /api/move/goto accepts 'interpolation' (move.py:41) but never passes it to backend.goto_target (move.py:137-149), so REST gotos always use MIN_JERK (abstract.py:950). The SDK WS path does forward method (reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:176-188).<br>HW3 impact: a grep over reachy_hw3 (excluding .venv) finds no reader of with_target_*, target_head_joints, state/full or ws/full. Its start/stop_recording methods are microphone recorders (reachy_s2s/robot/audio_manager.py:701-713).<br>Earlier probes agree, and C28 was never tested. Every earlier probe shows targets dropped: verify_goto_pipeline/v6_ik_mj_state.out.txt:49-53, control_backends_telemetry/fullstate_probe.out.txt, verify_control_backends_telemetry/v_fullstate.out.txt and completeness_critic/crit_probe.out.txt (all under scratchpad/wf1). verify_move_playback_apis contains no FullState or target probe (grep), so C28's 'confirmed' verdict was not backed by running anything. | - The robot's actual daemon version is unconfirmed (robot offline). If it is not 1.11.0, FullState may differ, so re-check the version once the robot is reachable. - The online official docs and newer SDK releases were not checked (offline-only rule). The installed 1.11.0 code is authoritative under the no-upgrade constraint. - HTTP 500 for REST with_target_head_pose=True is inferred from Starlette, not run, since no server may be started. - The mockup-sim oracle check (rebuilt joints vs. present head_joints with a 1-2 tick lag) was not run because starting the daemon is forbidden. - How accurately GotoMove can be rebuilt client-side is unmeasured: the start pose is sampled as 'present' at goto start, and t0 alignment depends on the client and daemon clocks. - Rust IK internals (reachy_mini_rust_kinematics, compiled) were checked only on 5 poses for exact match and history independence. - What raw /ws/raw/write does with the compiled motor controller (locking against the 50 Hz loop, register semantics) is unknown, and it was deliberately not exercised. - The update and wifi_config routers could not be imported (missing semver and nmcli), so they were checked by grep only. |
| time.time() granularity on this Windows PC. G30/H20 say it ticks every 15.6 ms, CBT-17 and move_playback measured about 1 ms, and the critic measured a 5.09 ms median. Also checked: the cause of the MuJoCo real-time shortfall, and whether HW4 should log with perf_counter_ns only. | 예 | Each claim was a correct snapshot of a value that keeps changing. The suggested check holds, with three corrections.  What time.time() is on this PC (CPython 3.12.14, Windows 11 26200): - It reads GetSystemTimeAsFileTime(). - get_clock_info reports 0.015625 s. That is the nominal tick (GetSystemTimeAdjustment returns 156250 x 100 ns), not how often the value actually changes. - The value changes at every system timer interrupt. Measured steps ran from about 0.30 ms to about 16.2 ms. - Window medians were 1.0 ms at 13:27-13:55 (five probes, and HW3 on 2026-10-16), 5.09 ms at 14:15 (critic), and 1.0-4.8 ms in my runs at 14:28-14:33. - The medians can shift within seconds. Other processes cause the shifts in two ways:   (a) Timer-resolution requests: whenever NtQueryTimerResolution read 1.0 ms, the median step was 1.0 ms.   (b) High-resolution timer expirations, even while the reported resolution stays at 15.625 ms. A separate process running time.sleep(0.002) in a loop capped steps at p95 2.59 ms and max 4.8 ms. A time.sleep(0.010) loop capped them at max 10.6 ms. A pure busy loop changed nothing.  Corrections to the suggested wording: 1. Say "about 0.3-16 ms", not "about 1-16 ms". 2. "Follows the timer interrupt rate" must include high-res timer expirations. NtQueryTimerResolution alone does not predict the step size. 3. time.monotonic() is GetTickCount64() with fixed 15/16 ms steps. perf_counter is QPC: 10 MHz (100 ns steps), counted from boot, and comparable across processes on this PC.  Effect on SDK loops: smaller than "15.6 ms ticks" suggests. The SDK reads time.time() right after waking from a timer, and that wake has just refreshed the system time. - Staleness after a time.sleep(0.002) wake: median 0.006 ms, p95 0.21 ms. - After an asyncio.sleep wake: median 0.14 ms, p95 0.71 ms. - At arbitrary instants: median 3.1 ms, max 16 ms. - So play_move's t (abstract.py:904-911) stays within about 1 ms of the true elapsed time. - On a daemon hosted on Windows, play_move runs at about 62 Hz instead of 100 Hz. The cause is asyncio's timer: loop.time() is time.monotonic() and the IOCP wait uses millisecond timeouts. time.time is not the cause.  MuJoCo real-time shortfall: it comes mainly from time.sleep(0.002) taking about 2.5 ms (median 2.511 ms, mean overshoot 0.50 ms). - In an emulation of mujoco/backend.py:227-288 with no work, the real-time factor is 0.793 whether the loop measures 'took' with time.time or perf_counter. - time.time adds a small extra loss: it reads the short work interval as 0 in 99.7% of steps, so the work time is never subtracted from the sleep. - With realistic work the factor is 0.7735 with time.time vs 0.7930 with perf_counter. That is about 2 of the ~22 percentage points lost. - Other agents' runs on the real model gave 0.788 and 0.799.  Recommendation: - HW4 timestamps use perf_counter_ns only. - Each session's metadata records:   - get_clock_info for time, monotonic and perf_counter;   - the measured time.time step median, p95 and max over at least 1 s, at session start and end;   - NtQueryTimerResolution's current value (a read-only query);   - whether HW4 itself called timeBeginPeriod (HW3's helper does). - Re-stamp SDK timestamps when they arrive: the client set_target record's 'time' is time.time(), and FullState.timestamp is datetime.now(). - On Wireless hardware the daemon runs on the robot's onboard CM4. This Windows behaviour applies only to client code on the PC and to a MuJoCo or mockup daemon running on the PC. | SDK play_move: t0=time.time() at reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:879; loop test at :904; t=time.time()-t0 at :911 after the cancel check; elapsed and asyncio.sleep(sleep_period-elapsed) or sleep(0.001) at :921-925. The joint-space goto loop at abstract.py:1021-1034 also uses time.time with asyncio.sleep(0.01).<br>SDK sim pacing: mujoco/backend.py:90-91 sets timestep 0.002 s and decimation 10 (50 Hz control). Loop start_t=time.time() at :228, took=time.time()-start_t at :285, time.sleep(max(0, timestep-took)) at :286. Mockup: mockup_sim/backend.py:71 (50 Hz), start_t at :87, elapsed and time.sleep at :136-137.<br>Client-side clock reads run on this PC for any backend: reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:660-661 (set_target record 'time': time.time()), :915-924 (_goto_joint_positions uses time.time and time.sleep(0.01)), :1149-1169 (client async_play_move loop on time.time). FullState timestamp is datetime.now(timezone.utc) at reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/state.py:141.<br>reachy_mini/daemon/daemon.py:213-219 (comment): the Wireless variant runs autonomously on its onboard CM4, reached over Wi-Fi/LAN; the Lite daemon runs on the user's desktop. So the Windows clock behaviour does not apply to the Wireless hardware daemon's play_move.<br>Clock identity, measured 14:28 (probes/resolve_3/clock_probe.out.json:6-40): time=GetSystemTimeAsFileTime() res 0.015625; monotonic=GetTickCount64() res 0.015625; perf_counter=QueryPerformanceCounter() res 1e-07; QPF 10 MHz. GetSystemTimeAdjustment increment is 156250 (15.625 ms), adjustment disabled. Every step is a multiple of 100 ns.<br>Earlier snapshots under scratchpad/wf1: control_backends_telemetry/timing_probe.out.json:25-26 (13:27, min step 0.9995 ms); goto_pipeline/timing_probe_stdlib.out.txt:5 (13:29, median 0.9999, max 1.74 ms); verify_move_playback_apis/clocks.json:23 (13:47, median 1.0002 ms); verify_control_backends_telemetry/v_timing.out.json:36 (13:49, median 1.0 ms); verify_goto_pipeline/v5_timing.out.txt:5 (13:55, median 1.000, min 0.314, max 14.958 ms); completeness_critic/crit_probe.out.txt:119-124 (14:15, median 5.089, min 0.303, max 16.126 ms).<br>HW3 measured the same on 2026-10-16: reachy_hw3 results/sessions/20261016_215156_timing_check/timing.json:17-21 (time_ns GetSystemTimeAsFileTime, declared 15.625, observed min 0.3723, median 1.0 ms) and 20261016_215434_timing_check/timing.json:17-21 (min 0.8878, median 1.0 ms). Yet reachy_hw3 docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:133-134 says time.time has 15.6 ms resolution 'measured', and docs/IMPLEMENTATION_NOTES.md:45 cites get_clock_info 15.625 ms. The docs mix up declared and observed resolution; the 15.6 ms figure is correct only for monotonic.<br>Cause test at 14:32-14:33 (probes/resolve_3/clock_probe2.out.json:3-199). A second process looping time.sleep(0.002): steps p95 2.59/2.58 ms, max 4.76/2.94 ms, 0% at or above 15 ms; in rep0 NtQueryTimerResolution read 15.625 ms at every 100 ms sample. A time.sleep(0.010) child: max 10.58/10.55 ms. A busy-loop child with no timers: median 3.40 ms, p95 15.67, max 16.09, 8.1% at or above 15 ms, the same as the 15.625 ms baselines (median 3.6-4.8, p95 15.2-15.7, max 16.1).<br>Timer-resolution requests from other processes also change it. In probe-2 rep1 the sampled NtQueryTimerResolution switched between 1.0 and 15.625 ms (this probe never requested a change) and the baseline median step fell to 1.0009 ms (clock_probe2.out.json, rep1_baseline_b at :133). In probe 1 the 'before' segment read 1.0 ms with median step 1.0006 ms, and 15.625 ms afterwards (probes/resolve_3/clock_probe.out.json:624-681).<br>Staleness of time.time when read (probes/resolve_3/clock_probe2.out.json:200-227). Arbitrary instants: median 3.11, p95 12.73, max 15.98 ms. Right after a time.sleep(0.002) wake: median 0.006, p95 0.21, max 1.90 ms. Right after an asyncio.sleep(0.01) wake: median 0.14, p95 0.71, max 1.68 ms. The wake interrupt itself refreshes the system time.<br>play_move-shaped asyncio loop (clock_probe.out.json:576-623): t minus true elapsed time (centred) p05 -0.55/-0.49 ms, p95 +0.08/+0.11 ms, worst -4.8 ms. Loop period median 16.2/16.1 ms, about 62 Hz instead of 100 Hz.<br>asyncio timer source in the stdlib (cpython-3.12/Lib/asyncio): base_events.py:738-745 loop.time() returns time.monotonic(); :421 _clock_resolution = get_clock_info('monotonic').resolution; :1967 end_time = self.time() + self._clock_resolution; windows_events.py:761-774 calls GetQueuedCompletionStatus with a millisecond timeout. These, not time.time, set play_move's ~16 ms period on a daemon hosted on Windows.<br>time.monotonic over 1 s steps 15/16 ms, plus one 31 ms gap where the process missed a tick (clock_probe.out.json:395-409), matching the critic's 15-16 ms. GetTickCount64 has a fixed cadence that other processes do not change.<br>perf_counter steps are 100 ns. A child process's perf_counter_ns() fell between the parent's before and after samples, and the parent value 6501.29 s matched GetTickCount64 uptime of 6501.47 s. So it is QPC counted from boot and comparable across processes on this PC (clock_probe.out.json:418-425).<br>time.sleep(0.002) actual duration, n=500 at 14:29 (clock_probe.out.json:426-435): min 2.009, p05 2.069, median 2.511, mean 2.500, p95 2.636, max 4.353 ms; mean overshoot 0.50 ms. This matches earlier medians of 2.508 (13:27), 2.498 (13:49) and 2.513 ms (14:15), even though time.time granularity changed in between.<br>MuJoCo pacing emulation, 2500 steps per variant (clock_probe.out.json:442-573). No work: real-time factor 0.7933 with time.time 'took' vs 0.7927 with perf_counter. 0.06 ms per step plus 0.25 ms every 10th step: 0.7735 with time.time (took read as 0 in 99.72% of steps) vs 0.7930 with perf_counter. 3 ms every 10th step: 0.7565 vs 0.7770. Control rate 37.8-39.7 Hz instead of 50 Hz.<br>Other agents' runs on the real model agree: control_backends_telemetry/mujoco_probe.out.json:256-266 (factor 0.788, decimated loop 39.2 Hz) and verify_control_backends_telemetry/v_mujoco.out.json:543-548 (factor 0.799, 39.96 Hz). Both used time.time pacing.<br>HW3 reuse caveat: reachy_hw3 reachy_s2s/platform_timing.py:19-31 calls winmm.timeBeginPeriod(1) and releases it at exit, and docs/PHASE2_REPORT.md:36-37 says all HW3 apps turn it on. Reusing it in HW4 would change time.time granularity for every process while HW4 runs, so session metadata must log it.<br>Probe scripts (read-only: no timeBeginPeriod, no daemon, no network): probes/resolve_3/clock_probe.py and probes/resolve_3/clock_probe2.py. Both ran on the HW4 venv python with -B and wrote empty stderr files. | 1. The CPython C source is not installed here, so two points are inferred rather than read in source. get_clock_info('time').resolution coming from GetSystemTimeAdjustment rests on the two values being equal (156250 x 100 ns). time.sleep using a high-resolution waitable timer rests on behaviour: 2.0-2.6 ms sleeps, versus Event.wait(1 ms) taking a median of 15.9 ms in HW3's timing.json. 2. I could not tell which processes caused the 0.3-16 ms baseline pattern or the 1.0 ms periods (other workflow agents, browser/Electron, audio). Finding out needs admin tools such as powercfg /energy, which I did not use. 3. That a running MuJoCo daemon caps time.time steps at about 2.5 ms is an inference from a child process running the same time.sleep(0.002) loop; the real daemon was not started. Staleness of t inside a busy daemon event loop (WebSocket/Zenoh callbacks running before play_move resumes) was not measured. 4. The clock behaviour of the Wireless robot's CM4 was not measured, because the robot is offline. 5. A Windows 11 rule may ignore timer-resolution requests from minimized or hidden windowed processes, which would make results depend on window state. Not tested. 6. Python 3.13+ is said to move time.time to GetSystemTimePreciseAsFileTime. This comes from memory of the changelog and cannot be checked here. It does not matter while the venv stays pinned to 3.12.14. 7. An inference to verify in Phase 3: with a real-time factor of about 0.79, MuJoCo physics time constants and lag would look about 1.25x longer in wall-clock time, so sim-based lag analysis needs to account for this. |

The custom XC330-M288-PG body motor has no published torque, speed, current or efficiency figures. The effect of its plastic gears is unknown, so XC330-M288-T values are only a proxy. 2. The model numbers (X-series address 0) of IDs 10, 17 and 18 on the user's unit were never read. Reading them needs the robot (read-only register access) and explicit approval, and no SDK client API for raw register reads was verified. 3. The actual motor supply voltage is unmeasured. The 5 V assumption rests on strings in the compiled controller. backend.py:701-713 accepts input-voltage errors as harmless up to 7.8 V, above the XL330's 6.0 V rating and its 7.0 V max-limit register, so the Python code does not pin the voltage. 4. Compiled-controller behaviour is inferred only from strings and class attributes. Three points are unconfirmed:    - what get_motor_name_id() actually returns;    - what set_body_rotation_operating_mode(0) does on X-series firmware;    - that no Feetech code exists (a missing source-path string is strong evidence, not proof). 5. Where the 'feetech' comments came from (for example a prototype) is unverified. No reachy_mini commit message mentions feetech, and the installed wheel carries no history. 6. Datasheet numbers are nominal stall and no-load points at 5.0 V, not continuous or thermal ratings. The usable continuous torque of each joint is unknown. 7. The HF datasheet page has no version marker. It was pinned through the copy at the v1.11.0 tag, and later hardware revisions could differ. |
| Are daemon timestamps useful? CBT-27 and official_docs_hw recommend /api/state/ws/full UTC stamps for separating Wi-Fi jitter; hw3_reuse says the robot clock is not NTP-synced, so daemon timestamps must never be used. | 예 | Both sides are partly right, and neither "recommend" nor "never" holds as stated. Corrected rule for HW4:  R1 (absolute time): the PC's perf_counter_ns is the only session reference clock. Never compare daemon stamps with PC wall time, or across sessions or boots. HW3 recorded the robot clock as unsynced and about 11 days off.  R2 (relative time): the robot clock can still be used. - /ws/full stamps are datetime.now(timezone.utc), i.e. the robot's time.time() wall clock, with microsecond precision on the wire. - Intervals between stamps need no fitting. - One-way delay variation needs a per-session offset and rate fit against perf_counter_ns arrival times. Use a lower-hull (LP) fit, not HW3-style OLS: on synthetic data, OLS slope error reached about 177 ppm (p95) when Wi-Fi stall bursts were present. - Gate every trial: stamps strictly increasing, intervals at least 1/f and bounded, no step in the fit residuals, and the coarse offset (daemon UTC minus PC UTC) stable to within drift x T. A robot clock step also corrupts the daemon's own goto timing (play_move uses time.time()), so this gate decides whether the trial itself is valid.  R3 (scope limits both CBT and official_docs_hw missed): - The stamps only bound downlink jitter on the /ws/full connection itself. - A stamp is the time the response was built, not the motor sample time. head_joints, body_yaw and antennas are separate reads of the Rust cache and are 0 to about 20 ms older than the stamp. head_pose is the previous tick's FK. The real motor sample timestamp (FullBodyPosition.timestamp) exists but the daemon throws it away. - The stamps cannot expose uplink or command jitter, because commands are fire-and-forget with no acknowledgement. - They cannot pair commanded with measured values: FullState silently drops every target_* field. - They do not see the /ws/sdk per-client queue (up to 100 messages, oldest dropped first, about 0.67-1.0 s of backlog that the client cannot see).  R4 (where measured data comes from): take the dense per-tick series from /ws/sdk. - JointPositionsMsg and HeadPoseMsg carry no time field, and the SDK client keeps only the latest message. - HW4 therefore needs its own listener that stamps perf_counter_ns on receipt. - Log the roughly 1 Hz DaemonStatus backend_status.last_alive (robot time.time(); real robot backend only), which arrives on the same queue, as the in-band robot-clock anchor for detecting backlog. - Optionally run a parallel /ws/full at about 50 Hz with with_head_joints=true (the default is 10 Hz and no head_joints) to probe delay and offset.  R5 (exception that strengthens the CBT side): goto_target and play_move build and time the commanded trajectory on the daemon with the robot's time.time(). t0 is never reported; TaskProgress gives only a completion stamp in naive robot local time. Placing daemon-generated commands on the PC timeline without uplink+downlink bias therefore requires a fitted robot-to-PC clock map.  R6 (simulation on this Windows PC with Python 3.12): MuJoCo or mock daemon stamps come from GetSystemTimeAsFileTime, whose observed granularity varies from 0.3 to 16 ms depending on system state. MuJoCo sim time is not exported. Use client perf_counter_ns only.  All lag results must carry the 0 to about 20 ms sensor-sampling-age uncertainty that no timestamp can remove.  Why it matters: a 10 ms timing error looks like 0.6-3.4 degrees of tracking error for a 30-degree, 0.5 s move. The range depends on interpolation; peak normalized velocity is 1.0 for linear, 1.875 for min-jerk, 2.0 for ease-in-out and 5.59 for cartoon. A 100 ms Wi-Fi stall looks like 6-34 degrees. | Brief citation correction: the clock note is at reachy_hw3 docs/IMPLEMENTATION_NOTES.md:13, not :12. It says the robot had no internet and its clock was wrong (NTP not synced, journal dated 10-05). The notes are dated 2026-10-16 (:3). This rules out absolute daemon time only, not intervals.<br>HW3 never wrote 'never use daemon timestamps'. Its actual policy is 'one clock: perf_counter_ns' (reachy_hw3 docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:133-134) plus 'the robot's clock may be wrong without internet (NTP)' (reachy_hw3 reachy_s2s/apps/daemon_logs.py:10). HW3 itself used robot-clock interval statistics: control_loop_stats from /api/daemon/status (reachy_hw3 reachy_s2s/robot/connection.py:80-84), which are computed from robot time.time() differences (reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:185-186,305-319).<br>Measured HW3 data: 72 control_loop_stats records from 10 real-robot sessions on 2026-10-17 show mean loop frequency 49.23-50.0 Hz and max interval 20.1-30.1 ms, with no step-like values. This is sparse, weak evidence that robot-clock intervals were sane (reachy_hw3 results/sessions/*/events.jsonl; probes/resolve_1/hw3_loopstats.out.txt).<br>HW3 already maps a remote clock onto perf_counter_ns: fit_sample_clock uses an OLS slope (accepted if within 0.5%) plus a lower-envelope offset (reachy_hw3 reachy_s2s/evaluation/loopback.py:23-56; reachy_hw3 reachy_s2s/robot/audio_manager.py:658-672; reachy_hw3 docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:318-320). The method is reusable, but the slope should come from a lower-hull fit.<br>The /ws/full stamp is set at reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/state.py:141, after all getters (:108-139) and before validation, serialization and send (:142,185). datetime.now(tz) is built from time.time() (stdlib cpython-3.12/Lib/_pydatetime.py:1818-1821), so it is the robot wall clock. On the wire it is ISO-8601 'Z' with microseconds (probes/resolve_1/serialize_probe.out.txt).<br>On the robot, head_joints, body_yaw and antennas_position are three separate c.get_last_position() cache reads made at request time (state.py:119,123,127 -> reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:480-517; reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:1088-1091). The compiled docstring reads 'Get the last successfully read motor positions.' The Rust read loop is configured at 20 ms (robot/backend.py:71-79), so values are 0 to about 20 ms older than the stamp. The loop internals are compiled and cannot be checked.<br>The motor sample time exists but is discarded. The compiled FullBodyPosition has a timestamp attribute, but get_all_joint_positions keeps only body_yaw, antennas and stewart (robot/backend.py:489-495). Built offline, FullBodyPosition.timestamp is UNIX-epoch wall time, +0.02 to +8 ms ahead of Python 3.12's coarse time.time(). The binary contains SystemTimeError and GetSystemTimePreciseAsFileTime. Confidence that the read loop stamps the same way is medium (probes/resolve_1/introspect_mc.out.txt, probes/resolve_1/fbp_clock.out.txt, probes/resolve_1/scan_pyd.out.txt).<br>head_pose returns current_head_pose (abstract.py:1093-1098), which is updated once per tick from that tick's FK (robot/backend.py:241-247; abstract.py:1146). Within one /ws/full frame, head_pose can be about one tick older than head_joints and come from a different motor sample. /ws/sdk is internally consistent: joints and FK pose come from the same read (robot/backend.py:241-274).<br>ws_full sleeps for one period after each send (state.py:165,185-196), so frames are 1/f plus build, send and scheduling time apart, unsynchronized with the 50 Hz tick. Inference: the age of the sampled values relative to the stamp drifts as a slow beat (sawtooth), not as random noise. Defaults are frequency=10 and with_head_joints=False (state.py:148,151).<br>/ws/full cannot deliver commanded targets. The with_target_* values are put into the result dict (state.py:114-129), but FullState has no target_* fields and uses the default config (reachy_mini/daemon/app/models.py:149-160), so pydantic drops them. The offline wire JSON contains no target keys (probes/resolve_1/fullstate_targets_probe.out.txt). No other daemon/app route exposes daemon targets (grep).<br>Command delivery cannot be observed: SDK commands are fire-and-forget with no ack and no receipt time (reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:166-172). Daemon stamps therefore characterize only the downlink; uplink jitter of PC-streamed set_target stays unmeasured.<br>/ws/sdk state messages have no time field: JointPositionsMsg and HeadPoseMsg carry only type and values (reachy_mini/io/protocol.py:995-1007; field list in serialize_probe.out.txt). For measured /ws/sdk data, the PC arrival time is the only time available.<br>Each /ws/sdk client gets asyncio.Queue(maxsize=100) and the oldest message is dropped on overflow (io/ws_server.py:92-103,126). Each tick publishes joint_positions and head_pose, plus imu_data when the BMI088 is present (robot/backend.py:263-285). At 100-150 messages/s that allows about 0.67-1.0 s of hidden backlog, and with no seq numbers it cannot be seen in the messages.<br>The SDK client overwrites a single _last_joint_positions/_last_head_pose and records no arrival time (reachy_mini/io/ws_client.py:165-207). HW3's PresentSampler polls that cache at 25 Hz and stamps the poll time (reachy_hw3 reachy_s2s/motion/sinks.py:91-118). HW4 needs its own /ws/sdk listener that stamps perf_counter_ns on receipt.<br>The daemon broadcasts DaemonStatus about once per second into the same per-client queue (reachy_mini/daemon/daemon.py:829-839; io/ws_server.py:105-110). On the robot it contains backend_status.last_alive, the robot time.time() at the end of the last good tick (io/protocol.py:67-74,164-179; robot/backend.py:287,344-350). MuJoCo and mockup statuses have no last_alive (protocol.py:77-88).<br>goto_target timing lives on the robot clock. ReachyMini.goto_target sends a GotoTaskRequest and waits for TaskProgress (reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:705-722). The daemon runs play_move and times t0 and every evaluation with time.time() (io/ws_server.py:174-200; daemon/backend/abstract.py:879-925,944-977). That is the same clock as the /ws/full stamps, and t0 is never reported.<br>TaskProgress.timestamp is a naive datetime.now() taken on the robot when the goto task finishes (io/ws_server.py:192-197) and is serialized without a zone (serialize_probe.out.txt), so its timezone is unknown. TaskRequest.timestamp is naive PC time (io/ws_client.py:273) and the daemon handler does not use it.<br>A robot clock step would corrupt the motion itself, not just the logs. play_move evaluates t = time.time() - t0 (abstract.py:904-925), and time_trajectory raises ValueError for s outside [0, 1] (reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:117-122). A backward step rewinds the trajectory, or aborts it if t < 0. A forward step ends it early. Stamp monotonicity is therefore a check on whether the trial is valid.<br>Size of the effect: from the SDK's time_trajectory, peak normalized velocity is 1.0 (linear), 1.875 (min-jerk), 2.0 (ease-in-out) and 5.59 (cartoon). For an example 30-degree, 0.5 s move, each 10 ms of timing error appears as 0.6/1.1/1.2/3.4 degrees of error (probes/resolve_1/timing_sensitivity_result.json).<br>SYNTHETIC test (assumed drift 40 ppm over 60 s; 3 ms + exp(4 ms) delay; 1% per-message chance of a 50-300 ms stall; 20 seeds), not robot data. Offset-only fitting leaves about 2.3 ms error. OLS slope error is -50 ppm p50 and 177 ppm p95, giving 5-13 ms errors (0.1-0.4 ms without stalls). The lower-hull fit stays under 0.3 ppm and about 0.01 ms. With stamps quantized to 15.625 ms, the error floor is about 15.5 ms (probes/resolve_1/synth_clock_fit_result.json).<br>In simulation, stamps come from the PC wall clock. On this PC with Python 3.12, time.time() uses GetSystemTimeAsFileTime (nominal 15.625 ms). Over 5 s, datetime.now(timezone.utc) changed in 2075 steps: 902 of 1.0 ms and 49 of 15-16.5 ms, ranging 0.3-16 ms and varying with system state (probes/resolve_1/clock_hist_result.json; reachy_hw3 docs/IMPLEMENTATION_NOTES.md:45-46).<br>MuJoCo backend: 2 ms physics step with decimation 10 gives 50 Hz publishing, paced by sleeps measured with time.time() (reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:90-93,227-288). /ws/full head_joints are read live from qpos (:319-335), while head_pose is set once per decimated tick (:243). data.time is exposed by no route (grep), so the physics sample time is not observable.<br>PC arrival stamps are not clean either. HW3 measured thread wake-up under CPU-bound Python contention at p50 5.5 ms and max 17.2 ms, even with timeBeginPeriod(1) (reachy_hw3 docs/PHASE2_REPORT.md:33,36-38). Arrival stamps can only be late, which favours lower-envelope estimators.<br>Robot time-sync state cannot be seen through the SDK. The logs route streams only 'journalctl -u reachy-mini-daemon -b' (reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/logs.py:25-37), and the SDK has no NTP or timesync code (grep). The only check available is the coarse offset (daemon UTC minus PC UTC) and how stable it stays during a session.<br>Routes: /api/state/ws/full (daemon/app/main.py:312,322; state.py:22,145) and /ws/sdk (main.py:334; daemon/app/routers/sdk_ws.py:8-14). The SDK uses /ws/sdk (io/ws_client.py:85; reachy_mini.py:582-586). WebRTC PoseFrame has a seq number but no time field (io/protocol.py:125-161; abstract.py:1577-1635). | 1. Robot OS time sync is unverified (timesyncd, chrony, fake-hwclock, or a LAN NTP server via DHCP), and the robot is offline. Whether its clock can step or slew mid-session (for example if the Café network has internet) is unknown. Each session should log the coarse offset at start and end and check stamp monotonicity. 2. The robot clock's rate error against PC perf_counter is unmeasured; the 40 ppm in the synthetic test is an assumption. HW3's -0.16% audio-rate result describes the audio stream (possibly packet loss), not the system clock, and should not be reused as clock drift. 3. Several Rust internals are compiled and unverifiable:    - whether the read loop stamps FullBodyPosition with SystemTime the same way the Python constructor does;    - the actual read-loop period and its phase against the Python 50 Hz tick;    - the bus read duration;    - whether the cache can update between the three getter reads within one /ws/full frame.    The 0 to about 20 ms sample age is therefore a configured bound, not a measured one. 4. Real downlink delay distributions are unmeasured: /ws/sdk backlog during Wi-Fi stalls, and uvicorn/TCP buffering and backpressure thresholds. In particular, whether a long stall shows up as a gap in the daemon stamps (send_text awaits before the sleep) or as arrival delay. 5. Observer effect is unmeasured: /ws/full at 50 Hz shares the CM4 asyncio event loop with play_move (100 Hz) and might perturb goto timing. 6. The robot's timezone, needed to read the naive TaskProgress stamps, is unknown. It can be found by comparing them with concurrent /ws/full UTC stamps. 7. MuJoCo real-time factor on this PC was not measured, because starting the daemon was forbidden. A sibling probe (probes/control_backends_telemetry/timing_probe.out.json) found time.sleep(2 ms) averaging about 2.44 ms, which would make the sim run slower than wall time. This is not verified in the actual daemon. 8. The official online documentation was not consulted (no network allowed), so the official_docs_hw wording itself was not checked. 9. Untested idea: /ws/full head_joints and /ws/sdk joint messages are float-identical copies when they come from the same Rust sample. Exact matching between them could give /ws/sdk messages a robot-clock bound. Its match rate on real data is unknown. |
| Predicted play_move rate on Windows (idle-loop 60-67 Hz from C24/limits_safety/C3 vs wakeup-driven 99-145 Hz from G26/CBT-17), and the P=10 ms assumption behind official_docs_hw's 'worst-case' endpoint shortfall (linear 3.3% at D=0.3 s) | 예 | Both rate figures are valid only as OFFLINE STUB results. They describe two regimes of one mechanism, and neither is a live-daemon measurement.  Mechanism: on this interpreter (CPython 3.12.14, Windows), the asyncio loop clock is GetTickCount64 and loop._clock_resolution is 15.625 ms. After every select() returns, for any reason, _run_once marks as due every timer whose deadline is earlier than now + 15.625 ms (stdlib base_events.py:1967-1974). play_move sleeps at most 10 ms (abstract.py:866, 921-925). So each sleep ends at the first wakeup of any kind on the event loop. If nothing wakes the loop, the sleep ends when the IOCP wait times out, which takes about 14-17 ms on this PC. That gives about 60 Hz idle and about 100-145 Hz with irregular ticks when other threads wake the loop. A direct poke test confirms it: asyncio.sleep(0.010), poked from another thread at about 2 ms, returned after a median of 2.6 ms. A 0.016 s sleep poked the same way was not shortened (median 32 ms).  Which regime applies depends on the entry point: - **Daemon side, SDK goto_target:** Backend.play_move runs on the uvicorn asyncio.run loop. _broadcast wakes that loop at least twice per MuJoCo control tick whenever any /ws/sdk client is connected, and the goto caller is always one. So the idle figure does NOT describe daemon-side play_move during an SDK goto. - **Daemon side, no /ws/sdk client:** the idle figure does apply, for example to a REST goto. - **Client side, ReachyMini.play_move:** this loop runs in a fresh asgiref asyncio.run loop that receives no cross-thread wakeups, so the idle figure applies.  C24's 'both loops ~62-67 Hz' is therefore right only for the client loop. G26/CBT-17 are the better stub model for SDK gotos, but they are still not live rates.  The play_move tick rate is also not the rate at which commands reach the actuators. MuJoCo writes ctrl only at control ticks: every 10 steps of 2 ms, which ran at 38.5-39 Hz in the stub and is nominally 50 Hz. The robot applies targets at a nominal 50 Hz. Extra ticks caused by wakeups mostly re-read the same time.time() value.  Endpoint: the shortfall is exactly 1 - s(1 - g/D), where g = D - t_last, not P. In the stub, g for the backend loop reached 24.4 ms, which is a linear shortfall of 7.7-8.1% at D = 0.3 s. The client loop clamps t to at most D - 10 ms, so 3.3% at D = 0.3 s is its minimum, not its maximum. The '3.3% worst case' is wrong for both loops on Windows.  On the robot's Linux daemon, ticks of at least 10 ms are expected (epoll rounds timeouts up to whole ms, and the clock has ns resolution), but this is unmeasured. | Path keys: unprefixed paths are relative to site-packages. STD = ~/AppData/Roaming/uv/python/cpython-3.12-windows-x86_64-none/Lib. WF1 = ~/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/861127bbcc84232c/a0000d09-6d77-4b5c-91fa-08cd85a297a3/scratchpad/wf1. RS2 = WF1/resolve_2. All numeric results below come from OFFLINE STUBS on this PC (no daemon, no network, no robot).<br>Interpreter facts (RS2/probe_tick_regimes.out.txt, env block): time.time() is GetSystemTimeAsFileTime and time.monotonic() is GetTickCount64, both with nominal resolution 0.015625 s. The default loop is ProactorEventLoop with _clock_resolution = 0.015625 (STD/asyncio/base_events.py:421, 738-745). Measured monotonic steps were 15-16 ms. Measured time.time() steps had a median of 1.0 ms and a max of 13.7-16.1 ms, which depends on the global Windows timer state.<br>asyncio.sleep(d) sets a timer at loop.time() + d (STD/asyncio/tasks.py:653-665; base_events.py:765, 782). _run_once waits on select(timeout) until the earliest timer (base_events.py:1953-1961). Once select returns, for any reason, it moves every timer with when < time() + _clock_resolution to the ready queue (base_events.py:1967-1974).<br>play_move sleeps sleep_period - elapsed, which is at most 10 ms (play_frequency=100: reachy_mini/daemon/backend/abstract.py:829, 866, 921-925). Because 10 ms < 15.625 ms, the timer is due at the first loop wakeup after it is set. With no wakeup, the sleep ends only when GetQueuedCompletionStatus(ceil(ms)) times out (STD/asyncio/windows_events.py:443-445, 761-774).<br>Mechanism test (RS2/probe_tick_regimes.out.txt, POKE DEMO): asyncio.sleep(0.010), poked once by call_soon_threadsafe at about 2 ms, returned after a median of 2.57 ms (15/15 runs before 5 ms); unpoked, the median was 14.07 ms. sleep(0.015) poked returned at a median of 2.9 ms. sleep(0.016) poked returned at a median of 32.4 ms (only 2/15 early). This matches the 15.625 ms threshold.<br>Daemon loop identity: the daemon awaits server.serve() inside its own asyncio.run(run_server()) (reachy_mini/daemon/app/main.py:570-640). uvicorn's loop factory is used only by Server.run() (uvicorn/server.py:85-88), so the server runs on the default Windows Proactor loop. /ws/sdk is handled on that loop (reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/sdk_ws.py:11-14).<br>ReachyMini.goto_target sends a GotoTaskRequest over the client's /ws/sdk WebSocket and waits for it to finish (reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:705-722; reachy_mini/io/ws_client.py:85-96, 268-280). The server starts backend.goto_target, which calls play_move, as asyncio.create_task on the same loop that handle_client stored as self._loop (reachy_mini/io/ws_server.py:116-131, 174-200; abstract.py:944-977).<br>WSServer.start wires a single Publisher(self._broadcast) into all four backend publishers (ws_server.py:59-71). Each _broadcast makes exactly one loop.call_soon_threadsafe(_put) call unless _loop is None or no client queue exists (ws_server.py:86-103). call_soon_threadsafe writes a byte into the proactor self-pipe (STD/asyncio/base_events.py:842-850; proactor_events.py:804, 820-831).<br>Wakeup sources: - MuJoCo: 2 messages per control tick (reachy_mini/daemon/backend/mujoco/backend.py:262-277). - Robot: 2 messages per tick plus an IMU message when the IMU is present (reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:263-285). - Daemon status: 1 broadcast per second (reachy_mini/daemon/daemon.py:829-839; ws_server.py:105-110). Each _put also makes a _send_loop task step ready (ws_server.py:140-144).<br>Consequence: the idle-loop figures (C24 62-67 Hz, limits_safety about 62 Hz, C3 65.6 Hz) do not describe daemon-side play_move while an SDK client issues goto_target. They describe the daemon loop only when no /ws/sdk client is connected, for example a REST /api/move/goto (reachy_mini/daemon/app/routers/move.py:76-108, 137-149) combined with the early return at ws_server.py:88-90.<br>Client loop: ReachyMini.play_move = async_to_sync(async_play_move) (reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:1115-1171). For a plain synchronous caller, asgiref runs it with asyncio.run in a fresh single-worker thread (asgiref/sync.py:217-243, 302-323). WSClient receives messages on a plain thread and never touches an asyncio loop (ws_client.py:94-96, 165-207). So the idle regime is the right model for the client loop, and C24's 'both loops' is correct only for that loop.<br>Stub, idle regime (RS2/probe_tick_regimes.out.txt, R0): a faithful replica of the backend loop ran at 59.9-60.2 Hz. Tick dt median was 16.5-16.6 ms, p95 about 19.6-19.8 ms, max 26.0-30.8 ms, with no ticks under 1 ms. The client-loop replica ran at 60.5 Hz. Earlier stubs agree: about 59-67 Hz (WF1/verify_goto_pipeline/v5_timing.out.txt; WF1/interpolation_math/timing_output.txt).<br>Control case (R1): the MuJoCo-cadence stub thread ran (2 ms paced steps, 2 model_dump_json calls every 10th step) but with no WS client, so _broadcast returned early. The loop still ran at 60.6 Hz (dt median 16.1 ms). Thread CPU/GIL activity alone did not change the rate; the call_soon_threadsafe wakeups did.<br>Stub with one WS client (R2: real _broadcast/_put/Queue/_send_loop logic, with only the socket send removed): 118.2 Hz. dt mean 8.46 ms, median 7.5 ms, p5 0.024 ms, max 26.1 ms; 37% of ticks were under 1 ms and 24% over 15 ms. This falls inside the earlier 99-145 Hz range from lambda-poke stubs (WF1/verify_control_backends_telemetry/v_asyncio_wakeups.out.json; WF1/goto_pipeline/harness_play_move.py:109-121).<br>Faster ticks are mostly redundant: in R2 there were 78.5 distinct t values per second against 118.2 ticks per second, because sub-ms ticks re-read the same time.time() value. t is derived from time.time() (abstract.py:879, 904, 911). Its effective step here was about 1 ms (median) only because the global timer resolution was raised; the nominal step is 15.625 ms.<br>Command rate is not the tick rate: MuJoCo writes data.ctrl only at control ticks, every 10 steps of 2 ms each paced by time.sleep(max(0, 0.002 - took)) (mujoco/backend.py:90-91, 227-260, 283-286). The stub cadence, without mj_step/IK/viewer cost, reached 38.5-39.0 Hz (median 25.0 ms), consistent with v5's 40 Hz. The robot applies targets at a nominal 50 Hz (robot/backend.py:71, 163-198, 203-228).<br>Applied-sample age at stub control ticks (R1/R2 control_tick_stub): the applied target's t lagged real elapsed time by a median of about 7.5 ms. p95 was 15.6 ms without a WS client and 23.9 ms with one. Wakeup-driven ticks land just after the control tick has already copied the target, because publishing follows the ctrl write (mujoco/backend.py:257-277).<br>Endpoint, exact: the backend loop exits when time.time() - t0 >= D without ever evaluating t = D (abstract.py:904-925). GotoMove.evaluate uses s = time_trajectory(t/D) for head pose, antennas and body yaw (reachy_mini/motion/goto.py:51-74). So the final commanded fraction is exactly s(1 - g/D) with g = D - t_last, and g <= (last tick interval) + (one time.time() step).<br>Stub g, backend loop, 80 runs: 0.095-24.35 ms (idle max 17.7 ms; cadence max 24.35 ms; with WS client max 24.2 ms). That is a linear shortfall of up to 7.7% at D=0.3 s and 4.9% at D=0.5 s, against 3.33% and 2.0% if g were 10 ms (RS2/probe_tick_regimes.out.txt per_D). Stub ticks reached 31 ms, so g of about 31 ms or more cannot be excluded.<br>Client loop floor: the client clamps t = min(time.time()-t0, D-1e-2) (reachy_mini/reachy_mini.py:1155), so g is always at least 10 ms and 3.33% at D=0.3 s is its MINIMUM linear shortfall. In the idle stub, g reached 16.2 ms at D=0.3 (5.4%) and 22.0 ms at D=0.5 (4.4%). official_docs_hw's '3.3% worst case' is therefore not a worst case for either loop.<br>Shortfall as a function of g, from the SDK curves (reachy_mini/utils/interpolation.py:117-153), with e = g/D and c2 = 2.5949: - linear: e - minjerk: 10e^3 - 15e^4 + 6e^5 - ease_in_out: 2e^2 - cartoon: -2*c2*e^2 + 4*(c2+1)*e^3 (negative, i.e. a residual overshoot) At D=0.3 s and g=24.4 ms: linear 8.13%, minjerk 0.47%, ease 1.32%, cartoon -2.66% (RS2/shortfall_table.out.txt).<br>Linux robot (source-based expectation, unmeasured): the default Unix loop is a selector loop on EpollSelector, which rounds timeouts up to whole ms (STD/asyncio/unix_events.py:1444; asyncio/selector_events.py:63; selectors.py:442-459, 617). _clock_resolution comes from the monotonic clock resolution (base_events.py:421), expected to be about 1 ns on Linux but not verified. If so, wakeups cannot end the 10 ms sleep early, ticks are at least about 10 ms, and g is at most about one tick.<br>Latent risk, inferred from source: the backend loop reads time.time() twice, at abstract.py:904 and 911. If the clock passes D between those two reads, t/D > 1 and time_trajectory raises ValueError (utils/interpolation.py:121-122). Offline, GotoMove.evaluate(D + 1 us) did raise (RS2/shortfall_table.out.txt). The WS task handler would return this as a goto error (ws_server.py:179-198). The client loop is protected by its clamp. How often this happens was not measured. | No live measurement was possible because this subtask's rules forbid starting the daemon. So no play_move rate or g distribution can be stated for a live Windows MuJoCo daemon. The stub leaves out effects that push in opposite directions: - More wakeups: IOCP completions from real websocket.send_text, other connected clients (dashboard, /api/move/ws/updates) and HTTP polling. - Slower control ticks, hence fewer broadcast wakeups per second: mj_step, IK/FK, viewer.sync, the 25 Hz offscreen render thread and GStreamer CPU/GIL load.  Also unknown: - The daemon process's own timer resolution. If any loaded library calls timeBeginPeriod, the idle wait would drop from about 15.6 ms toward 1-2 ms. - The time.time() step at run time. It was 1 ms median here only because some other process raised the global timer resolution; the nominal step is 15.625 ms. This step sets how t, t0 and g are quantized. - The live control-tick rate. The 38.5-39 Hz stub figure excludes physics and IK cost. - The 170-183 ms maximum control-tick gaps in the stub. These were not analyzed; they are likely an artifact of the probe's own busy-wait clock sampling.  The robot's Linux daemon is entirely unmeasured: Python version, whether uvloop is present, the real _clock_resolution, CPU load and the IMU message rate. How often the ValueError race at abstract.py:904/911 occurs is unmeasured.  Next step (gap 4): the main agent, with approval, runs the sim daemon headless in-process from a launcher, with no SDK file edits. It wraps GotoMove.evaluate / Move.evaluate to log (perf_counter, t), logs MuJoCo control ticks, and reports per goto: tick dt distribution, distinct-t rate, g = D - t_last, and 1 - s(1 - g/D) for each method. Do this separately for SDK goto_target (daemon loop with the WS client), client-side play_move, and later the robot. |
| Direction of sim-vs-real tracking bias. The four claims in conflict: limits_safety says 'sim looks better than real', goto_pipeline calls the lower-bound assumption unverified and reports a 0.01 rad yaw dead band, CBT-14 says the Stewart joints are friction-dominated and stall, and hw3_reuse H18 reports 4.1 deg sag and about 9 deg lag. | 예 | The bias has no single direction. Its sign depends on the pose, the metric and the speed, so the report should not say 'sim looks better than real'. It should give scoped numbers.  1. Only real trajectory available (HW3 R1, replayed in headless MuJoCo). This is one slow trajectory. In this replay the shipped MuJoCo model tracked much better than the real robot away from the sleep pose:    - Neutral hold: static pitch error +0.008 deg in sim vs +4.13 deg real; z error -0.075 mm in sim vs -3.24 mm real.    - Rise, contact-free part: pitch RMSE 1.71 deg (sim) vs 6.95 deg (real); time lag 0.12-0.13 s (sim) vs 0.73-0.78 s (real).    - Fall, contact-free part: pitch RMSE 1.25 deg vs 2.17 deg; lag 0.19-0.21 s vs 0.45-0.78 s.    - Near the sleep pose the sim was worse: pitch error +4.24 deg vs +1.68 deg real. The cause is a sim-only artifact: the shipped MJCF head collider (xl_330) touches the body collider (body_down_3dprint) there. With contacts disabled the sim error drops to 0.135 deg.  2. MuJoCo dynamics per joint group (sim time; settle = time to stay within 5% of the step):    - yaw_body is fast only for large steps. For steps of 0.05 rad or more, t90 is 0.05-0.11 s and settle is 0.12-0.38 s. For steps of 0.002-0.01 rad it creeps slowly (t90 0.92 s, settle 1.2 s) but still converges to a residual of 1e-5 rad or less by 3 s. MuJoCo's friction is soft here, so there is no dead band.    - Antennas are fast: t90 0.05-0.16 s, settle 0.07-0.23 s.    - Stewart platform is slow. At head level, t90 is 0.48-1.26 s and settle is 0.78-1.64 s, and smaller moves are slower. The cause is that the 7 passive ball joints use the 'chosen_actuator' class, so they get damping 0.15 and frictionloss 0.1. Zeroing both brings head t90 down to 0.06-0.22 s. Friction is the larger share and damping is not negligible.    - The long-run Stewart joint residual (0.002-0.004 rad per joint) comes from gravity against kp = 10, not from friction.  3. Corrections to the earlier claims:    - CBT-14: the joint t90 range reaches 1.33 s outward and 2.06 s returning. 'Stall' should be 'creep'.    - goto_pipeline: the 0.01 rad yaw dead band is not reproduced.    - hw3_reuse H18: 'about 9 deg lag' is really a 9.10 deg peak pitch error that includes about 4 deg of static sag. The actual time lag is about 0.75 s.  4. A sim daemon on this PC runs at a real-time factor of 0.77. Wall-clock sim lags are therefore about 1.3x the sim-time lags.  The sim is a valid lower bound on R1 only away from the sleep pose. Nothing is known for fast expressive motions. | All 9 actuated joints and all 7 passive ball joints (passive_1..7) use class 'chosen_actuator'. It is an empty child of 'perfect_actuator': joint damping 0.15, frictionloss 0.1, armature 0.001; position kp 10, kv 0.1, forcerange +/-20. Sources: reachy_mini/descriptions/reachy_mini/mjcf/reachy_mini.xml:20-25, 74, 253, 264, 275, 286, 297, 308, 319, 330, 341, 352, 363, 374, 381, 498, 512, 624-634 (same defaults in joints_properties.xml:3-8). The compiled model confirms 21 passive ball DOFs, all with damping 0.15 and frictionloss 0.1 (scratchpad/wf1/resolve_4/probe_dynamics.out.json 'compiled').<br>The motor-identified class xc330m288t (kp 2.54, forcerange +/-0.8, damping 0.095, frictionloss 0.058) exists but is unused. Its own comment reads 'probably wrong, would need to re-identify' (reachy_mini.xml:26-31; joints_properties.xml:9-14). The shipped sim therefore uses an idealized actuator, not an identified motor.<br>The real backend runs a 50 Hz loop that writes goal positions to the motors' own position controllers (reachy_mini/daemon/backend/robot/backend.py:71-79, 203-209). Default PIDs are (300,0,0) for stewart_1..6 and (200,0,0) for body_rotation and both antennas, in operating_mode 3 (reachy_mini/assets/config/hardware_config.yaml:56-164). They are written at startup (robot/backend.py:82-91), with the default path set in daemon/app/main.py:682-694. MuJoCo models none of this P-only motor loop.<br>MuJoCo backend details: 2 ms physics step with decimation 10 (mujoco/backend.py:90-91). Each tick sets ctrl[:7] = IK joints and ctrl[-2:] = -antennas (257-260). The published pose is the 'head' site minus 0.177 m in z (243, 294-308); the real backend publishes FK of the measured joints instead (abstract.py:1146). Torque enable/disable is a no-op (mujoco/backend.py:337-347), so R1's torque-off phases cannot be simulated.<br>The friction constraints are soft. Compiled dof_solref is (0.02, 1) and dof_solimp is (0.9, 0.95, 0.001, 0.5, 2) on every actuated and passive DOF (resolve_4/probe_r1_refine.out.json friction_constraint_params). Measured result: yaw_body steps of 0.002, 0.005 and 0.01 rad all give t50 0.284 s, t90 0.922 s, settle 1.196 s, and a residual of 1e-5 rad or less at 3 s. This is linear creep with no 0.01 rad dead band (probe_dynamics.out.json steps_baseline).<br>yaw_body for larger steps (sim): 0.02 rad gives t90 0.664 s / settle 0.94 s; 0.05 rad gives 0.108 / 0.38 s; 0.2 rad gives 0.052 / 0.154 s; 0.5 rad gives 0.050 / 0.116 s, with peak speed 11.6 rad/s (probe_dynamics.out.json).<br>right_antenna steps (sim): 0.05, 0.2 and 1.0 rad give t90 0.156, 0.064 and 0.054 s, and settle 0.232, 0.110 and 0.068 s. The residual stays at 0.0004 rad or less (probe_dynamics.out.json).<br>Stewart steps from neutral, head level via FK (sim). z +5 mm: t90 0.48 s, settle 0.78 s. Head yaw 10 deg: 0.68 / 0.96 s. Pitch 15 deg: 0.72 / 1.08 s. Roll 5 deg: 1.08 / 1.46 s. Pitch 5 deg: 1.14 / 1.52 s. Pitch 2 deg: 1.26 / 1.64 s (resolve_4/head_level_settle.out.json).<br>Stewart joint level for a 15 deg pitch step: t90 is 0.238-1.328 s outward and 0.318-2.062 s returning. The two low-motion joints (stewart_2 and stewart_5, 0.053 rad each) still have 11% and 21% of their move left at 1 s outward, and 22% and 31% returning, but they keep converging. CBT's 0.24-0.70 s range leaves these two joints out (probe_dynamics.out.json).<br>Ablation on the 15 deg pitch step (joint t90). Zeroing passive-ball damping and frictionloss: 0.054-0.072 s (head t90 0.06 s). Zeroing passive frictionloss only: 0.104-0.336 s. Zeroing passive damping only: 0.068-0.904 s. Gravity off: 0.286-1.666 s, essentially unchanged. Conclusion: the passive ball-joint dissipation causes the slowness, friction contributes more than damping (probe_dynamics.out.json steps_ablation_pitch15).<br>The long-run Stewart residual (0.002-0.004 rad, sign alternating by joint) is gravity divided by kp = 10. It is unchanged with friction removed (stewart_5 at 5 s: 0.00409 vs 0.00413 rad) and drops to 4e-5 rad or less with gravity off. At head level this is 0.008-0.015 deg pitch and -0.075 mm z at neutral, and +0.23 deg at 15 deg pitch (probe_dynamics.out.json; head_level_settle.out.json).<br>The shipped sim has head-body contact near the sleep and rest poses. The colliders are body_down_3dprint (reachy_mini.xml:85-91) and the head xl_330 (reachy_mini.xml:418); collisions are re-enabled after init (mujoco/backend.py:201-207). At the SLEEP init pose there are 5 contacts with 0.9-2.7 mm penetration and 22-66 N normal force. At R1's rest pose the pitch error is +2.74 deg with contacts and +0.34 deg without; at R1's final sleep command it is +4.52 deg vs +0.27 deg (resolve_4/diag_sleep_contacts.out.json).<br>Replay method: the 541 commands HW3 actually sent in R1 (motion.csv; sends = 541 at reachy_hw3/results/sessions/20261017_063145_motion_preview_live/events.jsonl:19; deadband skip logic in reachy_hw3/reachy_s2s/motion/controller.py:818-840 and 913-927). Each was converted to a world pose as Rz(8.174 deg) times the torso-frame pose and sent with body_yaw 8.174 deg (controller.py:27-31; sinks.py:46-48; events.jsonl:5). Commands were applied zero-order-hold at 50 Hz ticks in a sim loop that copies the backend, with SDK AnalyticalKinematics for IK and FK (abstract.py:210-213, 586, 1146). Results were compared at the present.csv timestamps (25 Hz client cache; reachy_hw3/reachy_s2s/apps/motion_preview.py:140-168). Script: resolve_4/probe_r1_refine.py.<br>Hold window, 20-28 s. Real (recomputed from present.csv, n = 197): pitch +4.134 deg, z -3.239 mm, x -6.811 mm, roll -0.841 deg, ant_r -0.0375 rad. This matches reachy_hw3/docs/PHASE4_5_REPORT.md:497-498. Shipped sim: pitch +0.008 deg, z -0.075 mm, x -0.013 mm, roll +0.017 deg, antennas about 1e-4 rad (probe_r1_refine.out.json).<br>DERIVED, not directly measured: the SDK IK of the measured mean hold pose implies real joint errors of +0.143 rad on stewart_2, -0.137 rad on stewart_5, and 0.017-0.039 rad on the other Stewart joints. The FK round trip agrees within 0.02 deg and 0.03 mm. The sim's errors at the same hold are 0.002-0.0025 rad (probe_r1_refine.out.json derived_real_hold_joint_error; probe_dynamics.out.json hold_joint_err_rad_mean).<br>Rise, contact-free part (commanded pitch below 18 deg, t 14.68-18.43 s, n = 94). Real pitch error: mean +6.67 deg, RMSE 6.95 deg, peak +9.10 deg at 15.44 s, time lag 0.73 s (offset-invariant fit) or 0.78 s (velocity cross-correlation, r = 0.85). Sim: mean +1.68 deg, RMSE 1.71 deg, peak 2.03 deg, lag 0.13 s or 0.12 s. For z: real RMSE 4.25 mm with lag 0.17 or 0.26 s; sim RMSE 1.52 mm with lag 0.10 or 0.02 s (probe_r1_refine.out.json).<br>Fall, contact-free part (commanded pitch below 18 deg, t 29.50-33.47 s, n = 99). Real pitch error: mean +1.49 deg, RMSE 2.17 deg, lag 0.45 s or 0.78 s depending on method. Sim: mean -1.10 deg, RMSE 1.25 deg, lag 0.21 s or 0.19 s. z lag is 0.30 or 0.22 s real vs 0.13 or 0.07 s sim (probe_r1_refine.out.json).<br>End of the fall, at the sleep pose (t 35.81-36.34 s). Real pitch error +1.68 deg. Shipped sim +4.24 deg, with roll -1.59 deg and x +1.33 mm. Sim with contacts disabled: +0.135 deg. Sim contacts were active over t 11.57-14.43 s and 33.67-36.39 s, roughly where commanded pitch is 19 deg or more and z is -32 to -35 mm or lower. In this region the sim is worse than real (probe_r1_refine.out.json; trace_v2.py output).<br>The '9 deg lag' wording is wrong. The 9.10 deg figure (my zero-order-hold recompute; the HW3 report says 8.9 deg at PHASE4_5_REPORT.md:499) is the peak pitch error on the rise, and it includes about 4 deg of static sag. The measured time lag is about 0.73-0.78 s. The 3.97 deg peak on the fall, at 29.54 s, is just the static sag at the start of the ramp; the HW3 report gives 'within 3.3 deg'.<br>R1 exercised only slow motion. Stewart ramps peaked at 7.99 deg/s pitch and 13.05 mm/s z, and the antennas at 0.85 rad/s (preview.json:69-76; PHASE4_5_REPORT.md:490). Body yaw was never commanded, so there is no real data at all on yaw_body dynamics or on fast expressive motions.<br>Real-time factor of the sim on this PC with the backend's own pacing (mujoco/backend.py:283-286): 0.767 and 0.774 in two runs, i.e. control ticks at 38.3-38.7 Hz wall-clock (probe_dynamics.out.json pacing_this_pc). Replaying with commands streamed in wall-clock time at RTF 0.8 raised the R1 rise pitch RMSE from 2.53 to 2.63 deg and the fall pitch lag from 0.36 to 0.38 s.<br>The two sim pose estimators (FK of joints vs the head site) differ by 0.04 deg pitch or less and about 0.02 mm z in the hold (-0.075 vs -0.098 mm). The choice of estimator is not a material source of bias (probe_dynamics.out.json r1_replay). | 1. The robot is offline. There is no real data for fast or expressive motions, step inputs, or yaw_body. Every sim-vs-real number comes from one slow trajectory, recorded in one session on one robot (HW3 R1, 2026-10-17). 2. The real lag includes Wi-Fi transport, the daemon tick and the client's 40 ms polling, none of which can be quantified from the logs. They likely add tens of milliseconds, small next to the ~0.6 s gap. 3. The real pitch lag on the fall depends on the method used (0.45 s vs 0.78 s). The pose-dependent sag contaminates both estimates. 4. The real joint errors are derived through SDK IK, assuming the robot was on the same IK branch. The joints themselves were never logged. 5. The cause of the real sag is unknown: P-only Dynamixel control vs friction or backlash. It is also unverified that the robot's daemon actually used the default hardware_config.yaml PIDs. 6. MJCF masses and inertias (head subtree 0.280 kg) have not been checked against the real robot. 7. It is unknown whether the real head touches the body near the sleep pose. With torque off it rests at about 28 deg. 8. The real-time factor of 0.77 applies to this PC only. The claim that a sim daemon stretches wall-clock lags assumes commands or gotos are timed by the wall clock (goto timing belongs to goto_pipeline). 9. The exact motor models live in compiled extensions and were not checked here.  Scripts, outputs and traces are in probes/resolve_4/: probe_dynamics.py, probe_r1_refine.py, diag_sleep_contacts.py and head_level_settle.py, each with a matching .out.json, plus the traces r1_replay_trace.csv and r1_replay_trace_v2.csv. |
